## Goal

Deliver TechieBlog — a production-ready, Blazor-native blogging engine on .NET 10 LTS, distributed as
a clone-and-own template — with a complete authoring, reading and engagement feature set, CSS-variable
theming, a resume/portfolio surface for the site owner, and the operational hardening (tests, CI,
health checks, analytics, newsletter delivery) still outstanding. Traces to `docs/TechieBlog-BRD.md` §1.

**Phase map** (carried from the migrated plan): **1** Foundation & UI scaffolding · **2** Authentication
& user management · **3** Content management core · **4** Engagement & social · **5** Media,
subscribers & analytics · **6** SEO, theming & production polish · **7** Bug fixes & polish ·
**8** Resume, image management & multi-author profiles · **9** UI re-platform (TrBlazeUI) &
portfolio home *(added 2026-08-06)* · **10** BlogApp desktop admin *(added 2026-08-06)*.

FAILS the visual gate: the AuthLayout card renders NESTED INSIDE the public blog shell (two TechieBlog logos, an unrelated Search/Categories/Popular-Tags sidebar beside the 'You don't have permission' card), and geometry reports 1 sibling overlap at 1280 (theme-toggle over theme-toggle - the ThemeToggle is painted twice) and 1 at 390 (mobile-nav-trigger over theme-toggle). A shell/layout-nesting issue on CLIENT-SIDE navigation; AccessDenied.razor declares @layout AuthLayout and renders correctly on a full page load · screenshot: .verify/shots/auth/access-denied-1280.png** ‖ **[REQ-UI-004] 2026-08-09 — FIXED, smoke-verified. Root cause named precisely: `AccessDenied` has TWO call sites and only one of them routes. `Routes.razor`'s `<NotAuthorized>` renders it as a plain child inside `<LayoutView Layout="AuthLayout">`, and `AuthorizeRouteView` renders that fragment inside the layout of the ROUTE THE VISITOR ASKED FOR — for `/users` that resolves to `MainLayout`, so AuthLayout nested a second shell inside the public blog shell: two brand marks, two ThemeToggles (the reported overlap), and the Search/Categories/Popular-Tags sidebar beside the "no permission" card. `@layout` cannot cancel an OUTER layout, so the only clean exit is to leave that render tree: `AccessDenied.OnAfterRender(firstRender)` now compares the current path with its own route and, when they differ, navigates to `/access-denied` — where its own `@layout AuthLayout` is authoritative. The redirect is guarded on `RendererInfo.IsInteractive` (never fires during the static prerender, where the state provider still reads anonymous) and compares PATH only, so a query string or fragment cannot loop. Fix is entirely inside `source/BlogUI/Pages/AccessDenied.razor` — Routes.razor was NOT touched. **Smoke (headless Playwright, anonymous + author@techieblog.test, http://0.0.0.0:5385): 3/3 PASS.** (a) Author signs in, CLIENT-SIDE navigation to `/users` (a full GET never reaches the router — the cookie middleware answers it first, which is why the defect only ever showed on the SPA path) now lands on `/access-denied` with **blog-sidebar 0, public-footer 0, main-content 0, brand-link 1, theme-toggle 1** — previously 1/1/1/2/2. The role-aware "Go to Dashboard" button is still correct for an Author. (b) Direct load of `/access-denied` at 1280 and 390: bare `auth-content` shell, one card, no sidebar, no footer, no horizontal overflow · screenshots `test-results-cluster-e/req-ui-004-{denied-1280,direct-1280,direct-390}.png`. ⚠ RELATED, NOT FIXED HERE (cluster E does not own Routes.razor): the SAME nesting mechanism also fires transiently on every page through `<Authorizing>`, which is itself a `<LayoutView Layout="MainLayout">` — while the async auth state resolves, a second header/sidebar/footer paints inside `main-content` and then disappears. It self-heals, but it is why sibling verify runs intermittently see "two logos"/duplicate `data-testid`s on ordinary public pages. Previously recorded 46px mobile overflow is FIXED: the Markdown table now sits in div.markdown-table-scroll (overflow-x:auto) and document hScroll = 0 at 390. ‖ **[REQ-UI-007] 2026-08-11 (cluster H) — the workaround is GONE; the reflow now comes from the library.** TrBlazeUI 2.0.2 closes **TR-059**, so `PostView.razor` no longer post-processes Markdig's HTML: `WrapTablesInScrollContainer`, its two regexes and the `markdown-table-scroll` wrapper constant are deleted (~45 lines) and the body is `<Prose ConstrainWidth="false" Class="markdown-content" data-testid="post-content">`. `ConstrainWidth="false"` is deliberate — the default `true` applies `max-w-prose` (65ch ≈ 600px) and would have narrowed an article that already caps itself at `max-w-[820px]`. `.markdown-content` stays because the library's `[data-slot="prose"]` rules sit in `@layer components` and the app's own unlayered typography therefore still wins on every property both declare; Prose only ADDS what base.css never had (`display:block` + `overflow-x:auto` on tables, `max-width:100%` on `pre`/media). **Smoke — own headless-Playwright run on port 5424, post `the-markdown-kitchen-sink`, `tests/verify/cluster-h-post-prose.spec.ts` 3/3 PASS:** documentElement horizontal overflow **0px at 390 and 0px at 1280** (it was 46px before the 2026-08-09 fix); the table measures **358px inside a 358px container** at 390 — the same figure the string transform produced — and 820px inside 820px at 1280; `div.markdown-table-scroll` count is **0**, `[data-slot="prose"]` present. The content the 2026-08-09 pass counted is unchanged: **3 `<pre>` + 1 `<table>` + 5 lists**, body 1334 chars. **The `<pre>` blocks did NOT pick the overflow up instead:** all three carry `overflow-x:auto`, render 358px wide and scroll internally (scrollWidth 656 / 439 / 872), and a sweep of every element in the body found **0** painted past the viewport edge that was not already inside a scroll box — long inline code included. A table-free post (`postgres-indexing-for-dotnet-developers`) also renders at hScroll 0. Visual gate clean at 1280 and 390: 0 zero-sized, 0 off-viewport, 0 overlapping siblings. Screenshots `tests/.artifacts/cluster-h/req-ui-007-post-{390,1280}.png`, `post-{390,1280}-{table,pre}.png`; measurements `prose-reflow.json`, `visual-postview.json`. Full-solution `cmd.exe /c "dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx"` → **0 Error(s)**. New library note logged as **TR-069** (the Prose usage snippet leads with the constrained form most Markdown bodies do not want).** | [view](#d-req-ui-007) |

| REQ-UI-012 | About page and 404 page (Phase 1) | Verified | 100% | Story 1.7; dark-mode fixed by Story 7.3 ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] FAIL — /about is fine (about-page, about-card, about-stack, about-links; visual clean 1280 and 390). /404 renders correctly when requested directly (HTTP 200, 14,623 bytes). DEFECT, VERIFIER-CONFIRMED: an UNMATCHED route returns HTTP 404 with a ZERO-BYTE body and renders a completely blank white page - no shell, no 404 content, no error boundary. Nothing routes unmatched paths to the /404 page, so the 404 page effectively never appears to a real visitor · screenshot: .verify/shots/public/req-ui-012-unknown-route.png** ‖ **[REQ-UI-012] 2026-08-09 — FIXED, smoke-verified. Root cause: this is a Blazor Web App, so `MapRazorComponents` registers ONE endpoint per discovered `@page`. A URL matching no directive matches no endpoint, ASP.NET ends the request with a bare 404 and an empty body, and the Blazor router never runs — which is why the `<NotFound>` fragment in Routes.razor could never fire. Fix: 404Page.razor gains a catch-all `@page "/{*NotFoundPath}"` (with the matching [Parameter] Blazor requires) so routing has something to match, and moves from AuthLayout to MainLayout so a lost visitor gets the header/nav/footer and a way back. A catch-all is the lowest-precedence template, so literal routes still win — smoke re-checked 10 real routes (/, /about, /categories, /series, /search, /newsletters, /resume, /post/{slug}, /category/{slug}, /tag/{slug}): all HTTP 200, none shadowed. /this-route-does-not-exist-xyz now returns 33,391 bytes with not-found-page + header + footer present (was 0 bytes, blank white); direct /404 still renders. /about clean at 1280 and 390 (about-page/card/stack/links all non-zero and in-viewport, zero overflow). KNOWN GAP, not fixable from this cluster: the unmatched URL answers HTTP **200**, not 404, because the whole router is mounted `@rendermode="InteractiveServer"` so the page never receives the HttpContext cascading parameter. Restoring the status code is one line of host middleware — `app.UseStatusCodePagesWithReExecute("/404")` in source/TechieBlog/Program.cs — which cluster A does not own; flagged for the owner. Rendering the page was the graded defect · screenshots test-results-cluster-a/req-ui-012-unknown-route-1280.png, req-ui-012-about-{1280,390}.png** ‖ **[REQ-UI-012] 2026-08-09 (cluster F) — THE KNOWN GAP ABOVE IS NOW CLOSED: an unmatched URL returns HTTP **404** *and* renders the page. The catch-all `@page "/{*NotFoundPath}"` was REMOVED from 404Page.razor and replaced by host middleware, because the catch-all made the status code unfixable by construction — while it existed nothing 404'd, so the `UseStatusCodePagesWithReExecute` cluster A suggested could never have fired. It also broke something else: the catch-all matched EVERY unrouted path including `/uploads/<file>`, and the static-file middleware stands aside once an endpoint is selected, so every image uploaded at runtime was answered with the 404 page as text/html until the next rebuild (see REQ-FN-025). Fix in source/TechieBlog/Program.cs: `UseStatusCodePagesWithReExecute("/404")`, placed BEFORE an explicit `app.UseRouting()` — this ordering is load bearing, since re-execution restarts the pipeline from that middleware and routing must run again on the rewritten path or the body comes back empty; WebApplication's auto-inserted UseRouting sits at position zero and would not. Scoped with `UseWhen` so infrastructure paths (`/_blazor`, `/_framework`, `/_content`, `/health`, `/uploads`) keep the framework's own response — an HTML page is the wrong answer to a caller that parses the body. The rate limiter's `OnRejected` now writes a short text/plain reason so a 429 is not swallowed by the same middleware (re-checked: 13 rapid /login hits → 200 then 429 `text/plain` "Too many requests. Try again shortly."). Smoke: `/this-route-does-not-exist-<run>` → **404** with 35,453 bytes and `not-found-page` present; `/deep/nested/nonsense` → 404, 35,474 bytes; direct `/404` → 200 (a requested page is not an error); `/health` → 200 application/json; `/uploads/no-such.png` → bare 404, 0 bytes. All 10 real public routes re-checked 200 and unshadowed. FLAGGED, not fixed (file outside this cluster): Routes.razor's `<NotFound>` fragment now fires again after hydration on an unmatched URL and paints a SECOND, differently-styled 404 screen over the server-rendered one, and its REQ-UI-012 comment still describes the removed catch-all · screenshot test-results-cluster-f/ui012-unknown-route-1280.png** ‖ **[REQ-UI-012] 2026-08-09 (cluster L) — THE `<NotFound>` FLAGGED ABOVE IS NOW FIXED, and the stale REQ-UI-012 comment in Routes.razor is corrected. Cluster F's diagnosis was exact: `UseStatusCodePagesWithReExecute("/404")` re-executes the request against `/404`, so the visitor RECEIVES the real page — but the address bar still holds the unroutable path, so the moment the circuit hydrates the router matches nothing and `<NotFound>` fires for real. While that fragment carried its own hand-rolled markup the result was a one-time visual swap: `not-found-page` (server) replaced in place by a differently-styled `not-found` (client). MEASURED BEFORE: raw response 404 / 35,447 bytes containing `not-found-page`, then `sawFragmentDuringHydration: true` and a settled DOM of `{page: false, fragment: true, title: "Not Found - TechieBlog"}` — the server's screen was gone. FIX: `<NotFound>` now renders the actual `<BlogUI.Pages.AdminPages._404Page />` inside `LayoutView Layout="MainLayout"` (`_404Page` is the compiler-sanitised type name — a C# identifier cannot begin with a digit; the LayoutView IS still required here because a Router NotFound fragment gets no layout and `@layout` only applies when a component is reached by routing). MEASURED AFTER, at 1280 and 390: HTTP **404**, `not-found-page` present in the raw body AND still present after hydration, `not-found` **never appears at any point** (`sawFragmentDuringHydration: false`), `<h1>` "Page not found", title "Page not found - TechieBlog", exactly 1 header + 1 footer, 0 px horizontal scroll. All 10 real public routes re-checked 200 and unshadowed; direct `/404` still 200. | REQ-UI-016 | Post editor — Markdown + live preview + metadata sidebar (Phase 3) | Verified | 100% | Story 1.10 DONE + 3.2 "Completed"; QA gate 3.2 PASS ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] FAIL — all 11 DevGuide controls render; live preview DOES update while typing (no blur/save needed) and produced h2/strong/code/li from Markdig; saving persisted every metadata field (slug, categoryid, seriesid, auto part number, excerpt, 2 chips -> 2 posttag rows). DEFECT: the Markdown textarea LOSES AND REORDERS KEYSTROKES - typing the 15-char string '## Live heading' yielded '## Li' / '#ve' / '## ' / '## L' at 120ms per key and '## ivehading' / '#L ag' / 'h' at 1000ms per key, in 3 of 4 runs. Every keystroke round-trips to the server and the re-render overwrites the DOM value. Visual clean 1280 and 390 · screenshot: .verify/shots/authoring/ui016-managepost-1280.png** ‖ **[REQ-UI-016] 2026-08-09 — FIXED (cluster C authoring).** Root cause confirmed exactly as diagnosed: TrBlazeUI's `Textarea` is a CONTROLLED input — `<textarea value="@Value" @oninput="HandleInput">` with `HandleInput` assigning `Value` before raising `ValueChanged` — so under Blazor Server every keystroke round-trips and the returning render writes a value into the DOM that is already one or more keystrokes stale. **Fix:** `PostMarkdownEditor.razor` now uses an UNCONTROLLED raw `<textarea>` carrying the library's own resolved classes (pixel-identical). The DOM value is seeded once as the element's child content (`editorSeed`) and the server never writes it again; `editorSeed` moves only for a PROGRAMMATIC change (toolbar insert, view-mode switch, a value supplied before the first edit) and each move bumps `editorRevision`, whose `@key` re-creates the element so the new text does reach the DOM. `OnParametersSet` ignores any value arriving after the first user keystroke — those are echoes, and adopting them WAS the clobber. Preview rendering is debounced 250ms; the parent is still notified on EVERY keystroke so a save pressed immediately after typing can never persist stale content. **Smoke `tests/verify/cluster-c-authoring.spec.ts` — 6/6 PASS** as the documented seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` against port 5383. (1) `## Live heading` typed one key at a time at **120ms and 1000ms per key, 3 runs each = 6/6 exact**, read twice per run (immediately and again 2.5s later, since the defect was a LATE render). (2) **COUNTERFACTUAL — this is what makes the result meaningful:** on a fast local circuit the PRE-FIX build passed those cadences, so that test alone would have been vacuous. Reproduced the verifier's 7-way-concurrency conditions deterministically with 400ms emulated latency (CDP `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`) + burst typing at 0/15/40ms per key: the **pre-fix build failed 4 of 9** runs (`'#ve he'`, `'## ng'`, `'## Living'`, `'## Lie edg'` — the verifier's exact signature), the **fixed build passed 9 of 9**. That stress is now a permanent test, so the suite has teeth. (3) Live preview still updates while typing — h2/strong/code and 2 li from Markdig — and the textarea is undisturbed by the preview re-render. (4) Toolbar insert and Edit/Split/Preview round-trip still write through (the programmatic re-seed path). (5) Save persisted EVERY metadata field, re-read through the router: slug, categoryid=3, seriesid=1, auto seriespartnumber=5, abstract, body, 2 posttag rows — cross-checked in PostgreSQL, not just in the DOM. Visual clean at 1280 and 390 · screenshots `test-results-cluster-c/ui016-managepost-1280.png`, `…-390.png`. Smoke rows deleted afterwards; DB left as found. Library gap **TR-057** logged (this deliberately breaks the AI-reference "never use a raw `<textarea>`" rule — there is no supported way to obey it and keep the user's keystrokes). Also logged **TR-058**: `SelectValue` shows the raw bound value (`0`) instead of the item text until the popover is opened once — pre-existing, cosmetic, left to the library.** ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] FAIL — REAL PRODUCT DEFECT, data-corruption risk.** `/ManagePost/{id}` does not reload when the route parameter changes: navigating client-side from `/ManagePost/5` to `/ManagePost/6` leaves post 5's title AND slug in the fields under post 6's URL — `OnParametersSet` is not reloading the row. **A save from that state would write post 5's content over post 6.** The save was deliberately NOT tested (that is the corruption itself). Reproduced on an independent fresh run; survived a forced re-render. Screenshot `tests/.artifacts/verify-authoring/shots/managepost-param-change.png`. Real user path: edit post A, then edit post B without an intervening page. NOTE: the Author DevGuide records this as a 'known harness trap' — it is NOT; it is a product defect and the DevGuide entry is wrong. All other sub-tests (render, metadata sidebar, series, live preview, visual at 1280+390) PASS. ‖ **[REQ-UI-016] 2026-08-11 — FIXED (cluster A).** **Root cause:** `ManagePost` loaded its post in `OnInitializedAsync`, which Blazor's router runs **once per visit** to the editor — the same component instance serves every `/ManagePost/{id}` URL, so no route-parameter change ever re-read the row. The verifier's diagnosis was right about the symptom and about the corruption risk. **Fix (3 files):** (1) `ManagePost.razor.cs` — the per-post load moved to `OnParametersSetAsync`, guarded by a `loadedPostId` field seeded at **-1** (not 0, which is the legitimate identity of a NEW post) so it re-reads exactly when the parameter changes; a new `ClearPostFields()` resets **every** per-post field first (PageObj, header, body, slug preview, category, series, part number, tags, new-tag input, schedule date/time, status message, save action) — a field omitted there is a field that leaks from post A into post B, so the method is the checklist. Only the per-visit lookups (categories, all tags, all series, signed-in user) stay in `OnInitializedAsync`. (2) `PostMarkdownEditor.razor` — the TR-057 keystroke latch is now **scoped to one document**: a new `ResetKey` parameter identifies the post in the editor, and a CHANGE of it (and nothing else) clears `hasLocalEdits` and re-seeds. Keystrokes never move `ResetKey`, so the uncontrolled-textarea fix is untouched. (3) `ManagePost.razor` passes `ResetKey="@EditorResetKey"` (`post-{loadedPostId}-{generation}`). **Smoke `tests/verify/cluster-a-post-editor-reload.spec.ts` — 5/5 PASS**, twice, as the documented seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` against port 5411, posts **5** (*Indexing Basics for .NET Developers*) and **7** (*The Markdown Kitchen Sink*), chosen because they differ in every bound field. Reads are **not** gated on the expected value — the editor is read twice 2.5s apart and both reads must agree before anything is asserted, so a late render that repaired the screen would still show as a mismatch (this is the "survived a forced re-render" condition). (1) **RELOAD, 10/10 fields against psql truth**, `/ManagePost/5` → `/ManagePost/7` → `/ManagePost/5`: title `Indexing Basics for .NET Developers` → `The Markdown Kitchen Sink` → back; slug `postgres-indexing-for-dotnet-developers` → `the-markdown-kitchen-sink` → back; slug preview, excerpt, full Markdown body, category `Programming` → `Technology`, series `PostgreSQL for .NET Developers (2 parts)` → `-- Not part of a series --`, part badge `Part 1` → *(absent)*, tags `[Database, .NET, PostgreSQL, Tutorial]` → `[Best Practices, FluentUI, Tutorial]`, featured image `Podcastbg.jpg` → `FullLogo.png`. Before the fix all ten still read post 5. (2) **SAVE SAFETY, asserted in PostgreSQL, not in the DOM** — after the switch the title was changed to `The Markdown Kitchen Sink [ui016 smoke]` and saved. Row 7 diff = **exactly 2 columns**: `title` and `updatedon`. `categoryid` stayed **5** (not 2), `seriesid` stayed **NULL** (not 2), and abstract, postcontent, featuredimage, slug and the 3 posttag rows were untouched — every one of those is a value the stale form would have overwritten. **Row 5 diff = 0 columns** across all 18 compared. DB restored afterwards (title + `updatedon=NULL`; re-selected to confirm), no other row touched. (3) **KEYSTROKE REGRESSION** — releasing the latch is exactly the TR-057 fix, so after the post switch `## Live heading` was typed one key at a time at **40ms and 120ms per key**: 15/15 characters in order, read immediately and again 2.5s later, both cadences. (4) **NEW-POST PATH** — `/ManagePost/5` → `/ManagePost` now yields an empty form (header `New Post`, blank title/slug/excerpt/body, 0 tag chips, both pickers on their placeholders). This is what the Author DevGuide mis-recorded as a "known harness trap"; the entry has been **corrected in `docs/devguides/TechieBlog-DevGuide-Author.md`** — the harness was innocent, it was this defect. (5) **VISUAL clean at 1280 and 390** — 0 overlapping sibling boxes, 0 zero-sized, 0 off-viewport, 0px horizontal document scroll at both widths; screenshots `tests/.artifacts/cluster-a/ui016-managepost-{1280,390}.png`, geometry `visual.json`, observed field values `reload-observed.json`, psql before/after `db-{before,after}.json`. **Unit tests: 8 added** in `tests/TechieBlog.Tests/Components/BlogUi/PostEditorRouteReloadTests.cs` (bUnit) — 3 pin the editor (adopts on `ResetKey` change; still ignores an echo under an unchanged key; 15 keystrokes with the parent echoing each one back arrive in order) and 5 pin the page (body+slug reload, every metadata field reloads, form clears when the id leaves the route, the reset key moves on every load, and an unchanged route parameter does **not** re-read — asserted as `Received(1)` on the repo, so an in-page edit can never be thrown away by a stray parameter set). `dotnet test` **1474 passed / 3 skipped**; the 4 failures in that run are a sibling cluster's in-flight `SelectFirstPaintLabel` (TR-058) work — unrelated file, unrelated REQ. Full-solution `cmd.exe /c "dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx"` → **0 Error(s)** across all 7 projects. No TrBlazeUI version change, no existing workaround removed, no new library gap hit. ‖ **[REQ-UI-016] 2026-08-11 (cluster H) — the raw `<textarea>` is gone; the library `<Textarea>` is back, and cluster A's `ResetKey` latch STAYS.** TrBlazeUI 2.0.2 closes **TR-057** (`TextValueSync` keeps the DOM value, the render-tree value and the parent-supplied value as three separate things), so `PostMarkdownEditor.razor` now renders `<Textarea Value="@markdownSource" ValueChanged="OnContentChanged" …>`; `editorSeed`, `editorRevision`, `ReseedEditor()` and its three call sites are deleted. **The document-scoped latch (`hasLocalEdits` + `ResetKey`, cluster A's data-corruption fix) is deliberately NOT deleted** — see the measured reason below. `EditorCssClass` is now the OVERRIDE set the library merges through `cn()`, and carries `dark:bg-card` explicitly because tailwind-merge treats `dark:bg-input/30` as a different variant group and would otherwise leave the library's dark background in place. **The adoption was gated on the three proofs, all measured against a running host on port 5424 and against PostgreSQL, with a raw-textarea BASELINE run first on the same harness so a difference would be attributable — `tests/verify/cluster-h-post-editor.spec.ts` 7/7 PASS (baseline 6/6, identical results).** (1) **KEYSTROKE INTEGRITY** — `## Live heading` typed one key at a time at **40ms and 120ms**, read immediately and again 2.5s later, before AND after a post switch: **15/15 characters in order, 4/4 reads exact**. That alone would have been vacuous, because the pre-fix 2.0.1 build also passed those cadences on a fast local circuit, so **the counterfactual was re-run**: 400ms emulated latency (CDP `Network.emulateNetworkConditions`) + burst typing at **0/15/40ms per key, 3 runs each**, which cluster C measured 2.0.1 failing **4 of 9** (`'#ve he'`, `'## ng'`, `'## Living'`, `'## Lie edg'`). 2.0.2 passed **9/9** through cluster C's own spec and **9/9 again through a new variant that runs the stress AFTER a post switch** — the moment the latch is released, i.e. the one path where the library's `TextValueSync` composes with cluster A's `ResetKey` and which the library's own tests cannot have covered (`keystrokes-latency-after-switch.txt`). (2) **ROUTE-CHANGE RELOAD** — `/ManagePost/5` → `/ManagePost/7` → `/ManagePost/5` client-side; title, slug and full Markdown body are the new post's every time, asserted against `blogpost` rows read straight out of psql. Reads are **not** gated on the expected value: the editor is read twice 2.5s apart and both reads must agree first, so a late repairing render still registers as a mismatch. `/ManagePost/5` → `/ManagePost` still yields an empty new-post form. (3) **SAVE SAFETY, asserted in PostgreSQL at COLUMN level** — after switching 5 → 7, post 7's title and body were both edited and saved. Row 7 diff = **exactly 3 of 21 columns**: `title`, `postcontent`, `updatedon`. **Row 5 diff = 0 columns**, and **0 `posttag` rows changed** for either post. Identical to the raw-textarea baseline. **DB restored afterwards and re-diffed against the snapshot taken before any cluster-H work: 0 changed columns on both rows** (`db-{baseline,final}-*.json`, `harness/db-snapshot.sh`, `harness/db-diff.py`). **Why the latch stays, proven rather than argued:** `TextValueSync.OnValueSupplied` short-circuits only when the supplied value equals the ONE most recent echo, so any other stale value is classified as a programmatic change and written to the DOM. Cluster A's bUnit test `EditorIgnoresEchoedValueWhenResetKeyUnchanged` passes with the latch in place and **FAILS with the latch disabled and nothing else changed** — the library did not stop the stale value, the app did. Logged as **TR-069**. **Cluster A's `PostEditorRouteReloadTests` — 8/8 PASS.** Three of them asserted the editor's text as `.TextContent`, which is how an UNCONTROLLED textarea carries its seed; the library renders a `value` ATTRIBUTE instead, so those three now read through a new `EditorText()` helper that accepts **either** representation. That is a markup-shape change, not a weakening: the counterfactual above fails through the new helper. Visual clean at 1280 and 390 (0 zero-sized / 0 off-viewport / 0 overlapping siblings / 0px hScroll); screenshots `tests/.artifacts/cluster-h/ui016-managepost-{1280,390}.png`, evidence `keystrokes*.json`, `reload-observed.json`, `visual-editor.json`. Full solution `cmd.exe /c "dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx"` → **0 Error(s)** across all 7 projects; `dotnet test` **1470 passed / 3 skipped / 1 failed**, the single failure being `MarkupAccessibilityContractTests.NoMarkupDependsOnTheDeletedAccessibilityObserver` over `App.razor` / `PostRatingPanel.razor` / `StarRating.razor` — a sibling cluster's in-flight files, none of them touched here.** ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14] PASS — re-verified, row CLOSED.** The 2026-08-09 `FAIL` (editor stale on a route-parameter change, so a save would have written the wrong post) no longer reproduces against the running host. Driven live on :5443 as `Ravi@techieblog.com`: the two post ids were read out of `/BlogsList`'s own edit links rather than assumed, then `/ManagePost/10` -> `/ManagePost/4` -> `/ManagePost/10` client-side; the editor title read `Testing Dapper Repositories Without a Database` -> `Observability for Blazor Server` -> `Testing Dapper Repositories Without a Database`, i.e. it reloads on the parameter change and restores correctly on the way back (`tests/.artifacts/verify-open/ui016-route-reload.json`). Visual clean 1280; at 390 row actions sit inside the table's own overflow-x container with page hScroll=0 (acceptable) · screenshot: .verify/shots/authoring/ui024-serieslist-1280.png** ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] RENDER-FAIL — DEMOTED from `Verified`.** `BlogSeries.SeriesId=2` stores `Status='Completed'`, but the grid renders BOTH status cells as 'In Progress' and the **Complete tab reads 0** (psql says 1). The screen's filter/label logic matches the literal `'Complete'` while the database stores `'Completed'`, so a completed series is invisible under its own tab. Survived a 35s poll with a forced re-render, then reproduced independently on a fresh render — not a timing artefact. Screenshot under `tests/.artifacts/verify-admin/`. Post counts and author cells on the same screen are correct. ‖ **[REQ-UI-024] 2026-08-11 — FIXED (cluster B).** Root cause: `BlogSeries.Status` is free text and the two sides never agreed — `019-SampleData.sql` and the live row store `'Completed'`, while `BlogSeries.IsComplete` compared `Status == "Complete"` (ordinal, case-sensitive) and the editor's `SelectItem` wrote that same never-matching literal, so the grid badge, both filter tabs and the counts all read a completed series as in-progress. Fix: canonical spelling is **`'Completed'`** (what the database already holds) and every literal now comes from one place — new `BlogModels.SeriesStatus` (`InProgress` / `Completed` / `LegacyCompleted`, plus `IsCompleted()` which trims + ignores case + still accepts the old `'Complete'`, and `Normalize()` on the write path). `IsComplete` delegates to it; `SeriesList.razor` badges and tab labels render the constants; `ManageSeries.razor` binds its picker to them; all four `SeriesSvc` create/update paths call `Normalize` so a non-canonical value cannot be persisted. Migration **`029-NormalizeSeriesStatus.sql`** rewrites any legacy `'Complete'`/whitespace/unknown row and adds `CkBlogSeriesStatus CHECK (Status IN ('In Progress','Completed'))` — confirmed applied at startup (`schemaversions` top row) with the constraint live in `pg_constraint`. Also widened the Status column 130px→150px; "In Progress" had been wrapping to two lines inside the badge. Measured smoke (host on :5412, admin `Ravi@techieblog.com`, landing URL asserted `= /admin`): grid now renders SeriesId 1 `In Progress` / SeriesId 2 `Completed`, matching psql exactly (before: BOTH cells "In Progress"). Tabs `All (2) · In Progress (1) · Completed (1)` = psql for **every** tab (before: Complete tab 0 vs psql 1); the Completed tab now lists "PostgreSQL for .NET Developers" and the In Progress tab lists only "Blazor Server in Production". Write path: created "Cluster B Status Probe" through the UI with status Completed → psql stored `'Completed'` (SeriesId 7); reopened the editor, which pre-selected `Completed`, switched to In Progress → psql stored `'In Progress'`, tabs moved to `All (3) · In Progress (2) · Completed (1)`; row then deleted through the UI and psql re-verified back to the original 2 rows. Public `/series` unchanged: cards read `In Progress` / `Completed`, part badges still 3 and 2, `/series/postgres-for-dotnet-developers` header reads `Completed`. Visual: 1280 and 390 screenshots in `tests/.artifacts/cluster-b/`, `document.scrollWidth == clientWidth` at both widths (no horizontal document scroll), no zero-size or overlapping controls on the admin screen (the 390 row-action overflow is the DataTable's own `overflow-x` container, unchanged and previously accepted), zero console errors. 21 new unit tests in `tests/TechieBlog.Tests/Common/SeriesStatusTests.cs` pin the two literals so a future typo fails the build; `dotnet test` 21/21 passed and `cmd.exe /c "dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx"` returns `0 Error(s)` across all 7 projects. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14] PASS — re-verified, row CLOSED.** The 2026-08-11 `RENDER-FAIL` (code matched `'Complete'`, database stored `'Completed'`) no longer reproduces. Live on :5443: the grid renders 2 rows = `select count(*) from blogseries`, with *Blazor Server in Production* -> `In Progress` and *PostgreSQL for .NET Developers* -> `Completed`, matching psql row-for-row; the tab bar reads `All (2) · In Progress (1) · Completed (1)`, and every one of those three counts equals its psql count (before the fix the Complete tab read 0 against psql 1). Visual clean at 1280. **At 390 the 10 row-action controls sit beyond the viewport edge but INSIDE the DataTable's own `overflow-x` container, with document hScroll 0** — the same condition the 2026-08-09 and 2026-08-11 runs examined and accepted; the gate now separates reachable-by-scrolling from genuinely off-canvas rather than re-reporting it (`ui024-series-visual.json` records it under `containedOverflow`). Screenshots `tests/.artifacts/verify-open/ui024-series-{1280,390}.png`. | [view](#d-req-ui-024) |

| REQ-UI-033 | Dark-mode corrections — sidebar, public, search, about, admin (Phase 7) | Verified | 100% | Stories 7.1–7.4 DONE; CSS in fluent-dark-mode.css, FluentAnchor + dialog/checkbox theming. [BRD-92] 2026-08-06: fluent-dark-mode.css retires with the Fluent UI removal — dark-mode coverage must be re-established on TrBlazeUI (`.dark` class) in Phase 9 ‖ **[REQ-UI-033] 2026-08-08 — RECONCILED then completed.** *The row was partly stale:* `fluent-dark-mode.css` **no longer exists** (only doc prose still names it) and `Themes/_variables.css` is gone too — the token layer is already `source/BlogUI/wwwroot/css/theme.css`, and dark mode is already carried by the **`dark` class on `<html>`** exactly as BRD-66 asks. The switching plumbing was verified end-to-end and is correct: `App.razor` renders the admin's `data-site-theme` + `dark` class **server-side** (no FOUC), an inline script lets a returning visitor's LocalStorage override it before paint, and `ThemeProvider` → `setThemeAttributes` applies live changes. So REQ-UI-048 had already re-established coverage; what was missing was **proof**, plus 3 real token defects it left behind. **Measured, not eyeballed:** contrast computed from **`getComputedStyle` resolved through a 1-px canvas**, because Chromium returns these OKLCH tokens verbatim and a naive `rgb()` regex silently returns null — which makes an audit report "0 failures" while testing nothing. That bug was found and fixed mid-pass (parser validated: white/black **21:1**, identical colours **1:1**, `oklch(1 0 0 / 36%)` → alpha 0.361). **Sweep: 14 routes × 3 themes × light+dark = 84 page audits** (`/`, `/post/{slug}`, `/category/{slug}`, `/tag/{slug}`, `/series/{slug}`, `/search`, `/about`, `/newsletters`, `/resume`, `/admin`, `/settings`, `/BlogsList`, `/CommentsList`, `/admin/categories`) — **final: 0 text failures, 0 control-boundary failures, 0 horizontal overflow at 1280 AND 390**. **THREE TOKEN DEFECTS FIXED, all in `theme.css`, none in a component:** **(1) DARK, developer theme — `--destructive` + `--alert-danger` at `oklch(0.70 0.19 22)` measured 4.30:1 on `--accent`** (the tinted surface under hovered rows and active nav) while measuring a comfortable 6.37:1 on `--background` — REQ-NFR-007 tuned every foreground against the *page* and never against the *raised surfaces the same file defines*. Raised to `0.74` → **4.65:1** on `--accent`, 5.46:1 on `--muted`, and 6.88:1 for `--destructive-foreground` sitting on it. **(2) LIGHT, developer theme — an OBSERVED, not theoretical, failure: the post link on `/CommentsList` (`a[data-testid=comment-row-post]`) measured 4.44:1** against its own row. `--primary` at `0.52` cleared 4.5:1 only on `--background`; on `--muted`/`--secondary` it was 4.23:1 and on `--accent` **3.99:1**. `--primary` + `--alert-info` → `0.48` (**5.50** background / **4.96** muted / **4.69** accent, and 5.59:1 for `--primary-foreground` on it); `--ring` and `--sidebar-primary` moved with them so the brand accent stays one colour — that part is coherence, not a contrast obligation, and is labelled as such in the file. **(3) Hardcoded colours removed from `layout.css` (standards violation, and a live dark-mode bug):** `.nav-menu__link--active` and `.tag:hover` set `color: white` on a `--color-primary` background — **invisible in the Minimal dark theme, where `--primary` is `oklch(0.93)`, i.e. near-white**. Replaced with a new bridged `--color-primary-foreground` token; `.form-error`/`.form-success` lost their `#dc3545`/`#28a745` fallbacks and `rgba()` tints for `color-mix()` derivations. **Token-pair matrix (every text token × every surface token, all 6 combinations): DARK = 0 below threshold in all three themes.** **36 pairs remain below threshold in LIGHT ONLY and are deliberately NOT changed here** — `--alert-success` 4.42/4.25/4.45, `--alert-warning` 4.31/4.27/4.25, `--destructive` 4.38/4.13, and `--input` **2.63–2.85:1** on `--muted`/`--secondary`/`--accent` across the three light themes. They are latent surface pairings, this row's acceptance is dark mode, and re-cutting three *light* palettes is REQ-NFR-007's call, not mine; handed over with the numbers rather than changed silently. **`--border` left at 1.26–1.33:1 on purpose, agreeing with the prior decision** — it draws dividers and card edges, which are not "visual information required to identify UI components" under WCAG 1.4.11; `--input` is the token that carries the obligation and it measures **3.27:1** in dark. **Admin surfaces genuinely reached, which needed two fixes to the harness:** a full page load of an authorised route prerenders as anonymous and bounces to `/login`, so navigation goes through `Blazor.navigateTo`; and every admin route was then **identity-gated on its own `<h1>`** after the first pass measured the *previous* screen (`blogslist` reporting h1 "Settings"). All 5 admin screens × 3 themes × 2 modes = **30 audits, 0 failures**, each with real data (Dashboard, Settings, All Posts 20 rows, Comments Management 20 rows, Categories Management 5 rows). **Dialogs + checkboxes — the surfaces Stories 7.1–7.4 named — audited explicitly** because a portalled dialog does not exist until opened and the page sweep never saw one: the category delete dialog (opened, audited, **cancelled — no seeded row deleted**) is clean in all 3 dark themes including its warning `Alert`, and a **checked** checkbox (the state most likely to vanish in dark mode) renders a 16×16 `--primary` fill with a visible mark; 21 boxes, 0 failures. **VISUAL-TRUTH, corroborated against DOM geometry at the same instant** per the stale-surface warning — viewport-sized captures at two scroll offsets, never `fullPage`: `hScroll=0`, `overlaps=0` on every page at 1280 and 390. Three alarming-looking signals were each run down and are **NOT defects**: `headers=2` is the semantic `<header>` inside `<article>` (exactly one `header[public-header]` — not the prerender doubling); the 6 zero-sized `nav-*` links at 390 are the desktop nav inside its hidden breakpoint (mobile uses the drawer) plus the intentional `comment-honeypot`; and a `span`/`label` at right=412 on a 390 viewport is clipped to `w=1` inside `fieldset.tb-keyboard-fallback` (`clip-path: inset(50%)`), which is why `hScroll` is still 0 — REQ-UI-057's fix working. Two earlier "invisible control" findings were **withdrawn as detector false positives** after probing computed styles: TrBlazeUI renders `<input class="border-0 bg-transparent">` inside an input-group wrapper that carries the border (**3.27:1**), and the 5 rating radios sit inside the clipped keyboard fallback — the audit now walks to the boundary-owning ancestor and skips clipped/`aria-hidden` subtrees. Screenshots **inspected, not merely captured** (165 PNGs + 18 JSON reports in `test-results/cluster-c/`, masters kept in the scratchpad because Playwright wipes `test-results/` on every run). Build **0 errors**. ⚠ Library gap **TR-056** logged: the shipped TrBlazeUI palette is validated against `--background` only, and its `--input` default is **1.26:1** light / ~1.6:1 dark — a WCAG 1.4.11 failure every consumer inherits. Test users came from the UsageGuide; `MustChangePassword` was cleared per the documented SQL and **re-armed afterwards (verified `t` on all four accounts)**. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — contrast MEASURED, not eyeballed - computed colours resolved through a 1-px canvas because Chromium returns these OKLCH tokens verbatim and a naive rgb() regex silently yields null (which would make an audit report 0 failures while testing nothing). My own pass: 7 routes in dark (/about 34, /search 29, /rss 48, /admin 50, /settings 36, /BlogsList 67 text nodes, plus / at 53) - ZERO nodes below WCAG AA on every one. Corroborated independently by the admin cluster (8 admin screens, 43-140 nodes each, 0 failures) and the public cluster (8 public screens legible in dark, tokens resolving to oklch(0.145 0 0) background against oklch(0.985 0 0) foreground, screenshots inspected). CAVEAT recorded honestly: on one of two runs my probe's injected `dark` class was stripped on `/` by the app's own ThemeProvider re-asserting the persisted site theme after hydration, so that route measured in light on that run (0 failures either way, and dark=true with 0 failures on the other run). That is the theme system working, not a dark-mode defect** | [view](#d-req-ui-033) |

Not a component limitation — the Storage-tab Select on `/settings` renders 'Local' correctly, so the same control does the right thing elsewhere. Gallery cards, 7 category tabs and per-card copy/delete all render correctly. Screenshot under `tests/.artifacts/verify-admin/`. Same defect family as REQ-UI-038/039. ‖ **[REQ-UI-034] 2026-08-11 — FIXED, one shared fix for all three.** Root cause is TrBlazeUI 2.0.1 TR-049/TR-058: a `SelectItem` registers its display text only when the listbox content renders, which never happens before the dropdown is first opened, so `SelectValue` echoes `Value.ToString()`. 2.0.1 already ships the hook — `Select.DisplayTextSelector`, called from the primitive's `OnInitialized`/`OnParametersSet` — so the fix is to supply it, from ONE new class `source/BlogUI/Common/SelectFirstPaintLabel.cs` (XML doc says delete it on the >= 2.1.0 upgrade; `grep -rn SelectFirstPaintLabel source/` finds every call site). Swept ALL 18 `<Select>` in `source/`, not just the three reported. Measured on :5414 as `Ravi@techieblog.com`: `user-filter-select` FIRST PAINT now **"All Users"** (was "0"); picking a user gives **"S Ravi Kumar"** and the gallery filters to 3 cards / "Showing 3 of 3 images" = `select count(*) from blogimage where userid=1` → 3. 7 category tabs, cards and copy/delete unchanged. | REQ-UI-037 | Manage experience page (Phase 8) | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-UI-037] 2026-08-09 — company-logo PICKER built; the named acceptance element now exists.** The path `Input` is replaced by a real `<ImagePicker Category="logos" UserId="@EffectiveUserId">` inside the add/edit dialog (`experience-logo-picker`), with the mono path box retained beneath it as the manual-entry escape hatch — both bind one property, so they stay in sync in both directions. **Three defects were found and fixed while proving it, none of which the FAIL verdict knew about.** (1) **The nested dialog was dead on arrival** — a `Dialog` inside a `DialogContent` is not guaranteed to stack above its parent (both are hard-coded `z-50` and the gallery's portal landed at DOM index 0, the parent's at index 1), so the gallery rendered but its tiles could not be clicked: `elementFromPoint` at a tile centre returned the parent picker's preview frame. Fixed with a scoped `z-index:120` rule in `utilities.css`; logged upstream as **TR-060**. (2) **The edit flow left the dialog hung open** — `EditExperience` navigated to `/admin/experience/{id}` and `SaveExperience` navigated back, but the destination REUSES the same component instance, so `EventId` reset to 0 while `ShowForm` stayed true; the dialog sat open over a stale list. Editing is now in place (the `{EventId:long}` route still serves deep links), the dialog state is always reset, and the entry is edited as a COPY so a cancelled edit cannot leave half-typed values on the card. (3) **The card header overlapped at 390px** — with `min-w-0 flex-1` the title column measured **40px** wide against a 188px action group, and the h3 painted straight over the Current/order badges. Fixed with `flex-wrap` + a `min-w-[200px]` floor + `ml-auto`; a new `assertNoTextOverflow` gate (scrollWidth vs clientWidth) now measures it, because the previous bounding-box gate cannot see text escaping an in-viewport box. **Self-smoke `tests/verify/cluster-d-resume-admin.spec.ts` — PASS on two consecutive runs** as the documented seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com`. RENDER-TRUTH: 3 cards = psql 3, each with non-empty role/company/dates, 1 Current badge = `iscurrent`, admin user selector present. PICKER PROVED END TO END, not just present: opened the logos library, clicked the tile, asserted the preview rendered AND the bound path reached the mono box, then saved — psql showed `eventid 6 \ ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — 3 cards equal psql `UserEvents(Type='Experience')`=3 with role/company/dates populated; the user selector correctly shows '-- My Experience --' rather than a raw id (this screen gets right what /admin/skills and /admin/awards get wrong). | ClusterD Smoke Corp \| ClusterD Smoke Architect EDITED \| /uploads/profiles/profiles_1_20260103181729_4dc89d68.png \| order 9`. Reopening the row rehydrated the picker from the stored path. Create → 4 cards, edit → title changed, delete → back to 3 and `count(*)=3` in psql. Visual clean at 1280 and 390: no horizontal document scroll, nothing off-viewport, no text overflow, no title/badge collision · screenshots `test-results-cluster-d/req-ui-037-experience-{1280,390}.png`, `req-ui-037-logo-picker.png`. Smoke rows and the two seeded gallery rows deleted; `MustChangePassword` re-armed on all four seeded accounts. ‖ EPIC-IRM-001 Stream I.2 ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] FAIL — VERIFIER ADJUDICATION - the cluster graded PASS with a finding; the acceptance text explicitly names a 'company-logo picker' and it does not exist as built. Everything else works: /admin/experience lists 3 cards = psql 3 with role/company/dates/display-order and 1 Current badge = psql iscurrent; add/edit/delete, ordering and the admin user selector all render; visual clean 1280+390. | REQ-UI-040 | Manage profile page incl. resume fields (Phase 8) | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-UI-040] 2026-08-09 — the 390px VISUAL-FAIL is fixed, and the root cause was not a positioning mistake.** The recorded defect (clear-image overlapping upload-new-image, invisible in the render) came from `ImagePicker`'s clear button carrying `absolute right-1 top-1` while **`trblazeui.css` ships no `.top-1` rule at all** — it has `top-0`, `top-1.5`, `top-1/2`, `top-2`, `top-3.5`, `top-4`. With no `top`, the absolutely-positioned button kept its STATIC vertical position, fell below the `h-full` image, out of the `overflow-hidden` frame, and landed on the action row exactly as measured. Changed to `right-2 top-2`, both of which the bundle actually contains. A second instance of the same class was found next to it: **`w-36` was missing** from the bundle AND from TechieBlog's gap-fill file although `h-36` was present, so every `h-36 w-36` preview square rendered 144px tall and FULL WIDTH with a stretched thumbnail — `.w-36` added to `utilities.css`. Both logged upstream as TR-019 instances. **Self-smoke `tests/verify/cluster-d-resume-admin.spec.ts` — PASS on two consecutive runs** as seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` (site owner). RENDER-TRUTH held: first/last name, username `ravi`, title, location all non-blank, resume-settings card present, `resume-enabled-checkbox` `aria-checked=true` vs psql `resumeenabled=t`, 2 ImagePickers (avatar + CV), all 4 quick links. VISUAL at 390 now measured, not eyeballed: `clear-image` does not intersect `upload-new-image`, sits in the TOP half of its preview and inside the frame; no horizontal document scroll; nothing off-viewport · screenshot `test-results-cluster-d/req-ui-040-profile-390.png`. **REQ-FN-053 DATA-LOSS REGRESSION GUARD — HOLDS.** md5 over the nine at-risk `BlogUser` columns (`username, issiteowner, title, tagline, instagramurl, phonenumber, location, cvfilepath, resumeenabled`) was **`4992e7019c1639d5ad727ddeb18291c1` before and after a Save with NO edits**, identical on both runs; skills 13 / awards 3 / stats 4 / experience 3 unchanged; re-entered through the router (proving persisted, not retained, state) and username/title/location were still populated with the checkbox still checked; anonymous `/resume` still rendered afterwards. `dotnet test` 490 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed. ‖ EPIC-IRM-001 Stream K.2 ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] VISUAL-FAIL — DATA-RENDER is perfect: all 10 text fields match psql byte-for-byte (names, username 'ravi', title, tagline, location, phone, linkedin, github, twitter), plus bio, resume-settings card, CV ImagePicker, resume-enabled checkbox aria-checked=true vs resumeenabled=t, and all 4 quick links. Clean at 1280. VISUAL-FAIL at 390: clear-image OVERLAPS upload-new-image - measured clear-image x=307 y=427 36x36 position=absolute against upload-new-image x=221 y=441 122x36, i.e. the avatar's clear-selection X lands at the BOTTOM-right of the preview (over the button row) instead of the top-right its 'absolute right-1 top-1' classes imply, and is invisible in the render. Reproduced on two consecutive runs · screenshot: .verify/shots/resume/req-ui-040-profile-390.png** ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — 12 profile fields byte-for-byte vs psql (11 populated, Instagram empty in both); resume toggle matches `ResumeEnabled`. | REQ-UI-051 | BlogApp login screen + admin shell (Phase 10) | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-UI-051] 2026-08-08 — NOW OBSERVABLE, and the adapter behind it did not compile.** Owner reported the solution failing to build. `dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx` (Windows SDK, rung #4) returned **4 × CS1061 in `source/BlogApp/Services/AuthService.cs`**: it called `authSvc.GetUserByToken`, `AppLogin`, `ResetPassword` and `RequestPasswordReset` — **four methods that have never existed on `AuthSvc`**, which is async-only (`GetUserByTokenAsync` / `AppLoginAsync` / `ResetPasswordAsync` / `RequestPasswordResetAsync`). The adapter had been written as a sync `Task.FromResult` wrapper against an imagined surface while a correct reference sat beside it in `source/TechieBlog/Services/AuthService.cs`. Root cause of the escape: the prior build gate never covered the `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` target, so BlogApp's failure was invisible to it — **any future "Implemented" on a BlogApp REQ must cite a build that names the BlogApp TFM.** Fixed by mirroring the web head (properly `async`, `ConfigureAwait(false)`) plus a null/whitespace guard on `RefreshTokenAsync` that also clears a CS8604. **SMOKE (mine, BlogApp.exe launched by `blogapp-smoke/nfr027.mjs`, bound to that PID's WebView2 CDP :9333, element-scoped by `data-testid`):** signed in as `Ravi@techieblog.com` and landed on **`/change-password`** — the `MustChangePassword` enforcement of REQ-NFR-023, i.e. the documented expected landing, not a defect. Screenshot `test-results-blogapp/nfr027-6-after-login.png`. Build now `0 Error(s)` across all 7 projects. Supersedes the 2026-08-07 "NOT OBSERVABLE" note below. ‖ [BRD-95] Added 2026-08-06 ‖ [REQ-UI-051] 2026-08-07 ORCHESTRATOR SMOKE: NOT OBSERVABLE — blocked behind REQ-FN-046. The app cannot reach its login screen because it errors at launch on an unconfigured first run, and no WinAppDriver/Appium-Windows driver is installed to drive the native head element-by-element. Not graded as a pass on static reading. ‖ 2026-08-07 UPDATE: the launch blocker (REQ-FN-046) is fixed and the app now reaches the connection-setup screen, so this REQ is no longer blocked behind a crash — it remains unobserved only because driving the native window element-by-element needs WinAppDriver, which is not installed. Hard rule 3 forbids global input injection as a substitute. ‖ [REQ-UI-051] 2026-08-07 NOW OBSERVABLE — 30/30 checks EXERCISED against the running desktop app. WinAppDriver installed (see REQ-FN-047 remark); the element-by-element driver for this Blazor Hybrid head is WebView2 CDP bound to the PID we launch, every action scoped to a `data-testid`, no global input injection. STARTS AT LOGIN: a configured cold start opens `/login` with `login-card` rendered and the `Connected localhost:5432/TechieBlog` chip. FIVE ROLE POLICIES enforced identically to the web admin, each seeded user signed in for real and each landing matched `RoleLandingRoutes`: Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` → /admin, Editor `editor@techieblog.test` → /admin, Author `author@techieblog.test` → /BlogsList, Contributor `contributor@techieblog.test` → /, Guest (anonymous) → redirected to login. Policy probe matrix (route→policy) all matched expectations: Admin ALLOW on /admin, /BlogsList, /settings, /admin/skills; Editor DENY only on /settings (AdminOnly); Author DENY on /admin + /settings, ALLOW on /BlogsList + /admin/skills; Contributor DENY on all four; Guest redirected to login on all four. BlogApp builds the same five policies from the same `AppPolicies.PolicyRoleMap`, and the runtime outcomes confirm it. HARNESS NOTE that changed the result: an earlier pass navigated with hard `page.goto`, which re-boots the BlazorWebView into "An unhandled error has occurred" and scored as a false ALLOW; navigating through the app's own router (`Blazor.navigateTo`) and clearing the latched ErrorBoundary AFTER each navigation is what makes these verdicts real. REMAINING 4 GAPS ARE NOT BLOGAPP DEFECTS — one shared-BlogUI root cause, head-independent (a Blazor parameter-binding failure, so the web head fails identically): a `data-testid` attribute is passed to TrBlazeUI components that declare no `CaptureUnmatchedValues` parameter → `InvalidOperationException: Object of type 'TrBlazeUI.Components.Tabs.TabsList' / '...Tabs.TabsTrigger' / '...TimePicker.TimePicker' does not have a property matching the name 'data-testid'`. Offending lines, owned by other clusters and deliberately NOT edited by this one: `BlogUI/Pages/AdminPages/ManagePost.razor:148` (TimePicker), `SeriesList.razor:44-47`, `UsersList.razor:42-46`, `SubscribersList.razor:45-48` (Tabs). BlogsList, CommentsList and Settings carried the same bug earlier today and now pass, so the fix pattern is proven. ⚠ visual (BlogApp-specific): at the desktop head's window the admin tables push their action column past the right edge with no horizontal scroll to reach it — `category-edit`/`category-delete` and `comment-reply`/`comment-delete` measured off-viewport. Diagnosed, not guessed: the window is 1440x900 by `GetWindowRect` but the unpackaged WinUI process runs DPI-UNAWARE (`GetDpiForWindow` returns 96 although `Platforms/Windows/app.manifest` declares PerMonitorV2), so the WebView reports `devicePixelRatio` 1.5 and only 950x574 CSS px of layout — under `App.xaml.cs`'s own 1024 floor. Scaling the window constants by display density and wiring `<ApplicationManifest>` into the csproj were both tried and measured as ineffective, so both were REVERTED rather than left as dead code; the finding and its measurements are recorded in the `App.xaml.cs` `CreateWindow` remarks. Real fix belongs to WinUI DPI-awareness setup or an `overflow-x:auto` wrapper on the shared admin tables. Evidence: test-results-blogapp/20-*.png, 30-fresh_*.png. Rung #4 build GREEN (0 errors). ‖ [REQ-UI-052] 2026-08-08 Implemented — CLUSTER B. **Splat bug class ERADICATED and proven so by tooling, not by eye.** The four named crash sites were already repaired by the orchestrator's pass; the value added here is the catalog-wide audit that was asked for. `TrBlazeUI.Components.dll` + `TrBlazeUI.Primitives.dll` were loaded in a `MetadataLoadContext` and every `IComponent` was classified by whether its inheritance chain declares `[Parameter(CaptureUnmatchedValues = true)]` — **132 of 334 component types reject splatting**. Every `.razor` in `source/BlogUI` was then parsed (multi-line tags included) for unmatched attributes (`data-*`, `aria-*`, `id`, `style`, `@on*`) sitting directly on one of those 132: the scanner was self-tested against synthetic bad markup (7/7 caught, splatting `Card`/`Input` correctly ignored). Result: **1 further live instance found and fixed — `BlogsList.razor` `TabsList` + 4x `TabsTrigger`** (would have crashed `/BlogsList` identically); final scan is **0 findings across all 71 BlogUI razor files**. ⚠ **The visual defect DID NOT REPRODUCE and the prescribed fix was WRONG — measured, then reverted.** An `overflow-x:auto` + `min-w-[720px]` wrapper was implemented on all 7 admin grids as instructed, then DOM measurement showed TrBlazeUI's `DataTable` already renders its own `<div class="relative w-full overflow-auto rounded-md border">`; the wrapper merely added a redundant second nested horizontal scrollbar (outer scrolled 74px, inner 267px), so it was REVERTED rather than left in. The action column is reachable at every width by scrolling the grid's own container — the earlier "off-viewport" reading measured bounding boxes without scrolling. The verifier's finding is confirmed exactly as written and is now fixed at both levels. (1) `BlogSidebar.razor` — rendered by `MainLayout`, i.e. the widest-reach anonymous write surface in the site — now mounts a `CaptchaWidget` (`data-testid="sidebar-subscribe-captcha"`) and writes through the double opt-in path. Its `IsHumanAsync` **fails CLOSED**: a null widget returns false rather than true, because a form reachable from every page must never degrade into "no challenge = pass" (the newsletter card's old fail-open branch was corrected the same way). The address is shape-checked BEFORE the captcha so a typo does not burn a single-use challenge. (2) `SubscriberSvc.SubscribePendingAsync` is new and is now the ONE double opt-in implementation: it writes `IsConfirmed = false, IsActive = false` and issues an `EmailVerificationPurpose.Subscription` token through `IEmailVerificationService` (injected as an OPTIONAL third constructor parameter, so existing `new SubscriberSvc(repo, logger)` call sites still compile; when it is absent the method REFUSES rather than falling back to the auto-confirming path). `NewsletterSubscribeCard` was refactored onto it — it previously drove `ISubscriberRepo` + `IEmailVerificationService` itself, and that second copy is exactly how the sidebar drifted onto the auto-confirming path. `SubscriberSvc.Subscribe` (auto-confirm, `IsConfirmed = true`) survives for the ADMIN roster only and its XML doc now says so in those words; **no anonymous caller remains**. **FULL ENUMERATION of anonymous-reachable write surfaces** — sidebar subscribe (captcha ✅ + double opt-in ✅, FIXED); `/newsletters` + `/newsletter/{slug}` subscribe card (✅/✅, now shares the service path); comment form on `/post/{slug}` (✅ service-validated + honeypot + spam guard, double opt-in already enforced by `CommentSvc`); rating panel on `/post/{slug}` (✅ service-validated, double opt-in by `RatingSvc`); footer — **no form, none needed**; sidebar SEARCH box — read-only navigation, no write, none needed; `/verify/{token}` "send a new link" — **no captcha needed and none added**: it requires possession of an unredeemed token, re-mails only the address already bound to that token, sets `canResend=false` after one send, and `IssueAsync` is itself per-address rate-limited, so it cannot be aimed at an arbitrary inbox; `/unsubscribe/{token}` — cluster H, token-gated, not touched; `/login`,`/forgot-password`,`/reset-password` — auth surfaces, cluster J; **no `/contact` route exists** (confirmed, matches the verifier). **Smoke (headless Playwright chromium from WSL against the Windows host on 0.0.0.0:5385, fully anonymous fresh contexts, `tests/verify/cluster-e.spec.ts`) — 10/10 PASS.** The suite solves the challenge through the ACCESSIBLE QUESTION mode, which doubles as the REQ-UI-057 regression check — all four question shapes parse and validate. With a valid captcha the row appeared with **`isconfirmed = f`** and an `emailverificationtoken` row of purpose `Subscription` with a `1 day` window — 14 subscriber rows written across the run, **every one of them `f`**, versus the `isconfirmed=t` the verifier reproduced before the fix. | REQ-FN-008 | Token refresh (Phase 2) | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-008] 2026-08-09 — REFRESH IS NOW REACHABLE AND CORRECT END TO END. DECISION: implement refresh over `UserLogin`; do NOT resurrect `svctoken`.** The verifier was right that nothing called the refresh path, but the deeper defect was that **there was nothing to refresh**: `GenerateJWToken` stamped a 15-day `exp` while `IssueSessionAsync` wrote a 2-day `UserLogin` row, and **neither value was ever read** — `GetUserByTokenAsync` matched only on `(userid, logintoken, tokenstatus='ValidToken')`, so in the running product a session never expired at all. Wiring a call site to the old `RefreshTokenAsync` (which merely re-ran `GetUserByTokenAsync` and minted nothing) would have been dead code with a caller. **What was built.** (1) New `source/BlogEngine/Common/SessionPolicy.cs` names the two clocks a session actually has — `AccessTokenLifetime` (default 60 min, config `Auth:AccessTokenMinutes`) and `RefreshWindow` (default 14 d, config `Auth:RefreshWindowDays`); registered as a singleton in `BlogSvcInitializer` and injected into `AuthSvc` as an OPTIONAL ctor arg so the 448 existing tests still compile. (2) `AuthSvc.GetUserByTokenAsync` now ENFORCES both clocks — the token's `exp` (read via the new `SvcUtils.GetTokenExpiryUtc`) and the row's `ExipryDate` — and logs which one ran out. (3) New `AuthSvc.RefreshSessionAsync` matches the presented token against its own `UserLogin` row, refuses it past the refresh window, mints a replacement and **rewrites the SAME row in place** (`loginid` preserved, window slid), so the replaced token dies immediately, a revoked session can never be revived, and the table does not grow per renewal. (4) **The call site — this is the part that was missing:** `CustomAuthStateProvider.ResolveSessionUserAsync` (BlogUI), the single choke point every Blazor circuit passes through to rehydrate its principal; both host adapters (`TechieBlog/Services/AuthService.cs`, `BlogApp/Services/AuthService.cs`) now delegate `RefreshTokenAsync` to `RefreshSessionAsync`, and `DesktopAuthStateProvider` resolves through the shared helper so the MAUI head stops deleting a token the website would have renewed. No Program.cs edit was needed — there is no REST surface to expose (REQ-FN-002) and none was added. **`svctoken` was NOT resurrected** (REQ-FN-052 deleted it deliberately); `UserLogin` is the single store of interactive session state and the expired access token is its own refresh token, a limitation stated explicitly in the XML docs rather than left implicit. **Two real defects found while building, both fixed:** a sub-second `Auth:AccessTokenMinutes` made `JwtSecurityTokenHandler` throw `IDX12401` on **every sign-in** (now floored at `MinimumAccessTokenLifetime` = 1 s), and two tokens minted for the same user inside one second were **byte-identical** (whole-second `exp`, identical claims) so a "rotation" rewrote the row with the value it already held — fixed with a `jti` claim. A third was found by the smoke itself: one page load rebuilt the auth state twice and rotated the session twice, which under a race would have signed the user OUT at the moment the feature exists to keep them in — closed with a per-circuit `SemaphoreSlim` in `ResolveSessionUserAsync` that re-checks storage after waiting (smoke then showed exactly 1 renewal line, down from 2). **Tests:** 20 new in `tests/unit/Security/SessionRefreshTests.cs`; full suite **448 passed / 0 failed**. Full `cmd.exe /c "dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx"` (rung #4, BlogApp's `net10.0-windows` TFM included) → **0 Error(s)**. **SELF-SMOKE — PASS (`tests/verify/cluster-j-token-refresh.spec.ts`, 2/2), and it proves REACHABILITY, not compilation.** Own host booted by this agent on :5390 with `Auth__AccessTokenMinutes=0.5` (30 s tokens; an ENV VAR, so no config file was edited and nothing needs reverting). Signed in through the real login form as the documented seeded Editor `editor@techieblog.test` → `userlogins` loginid **269**, window ends 2026-08-23 08:26:51. Waited **38 s** for the real token to pass its real `exp`, then asked only for the ordinary home page. FOUR independent witnesses agreed: (a) the stored token ROTATED (…OnEg-qtj10tQ → …-IS6cq20uU6Q); (b) the session SURVIVED — `Blazor.navigateTo('/change-password')` rendered the `[Authorize]` card instead of bouncing to `/login`, no horizontal scroll at 1280 or 390; (c) the host log carried `Access token for user 2 has expired; the caller must refresh session 269` followed by `Session refreshed for user 2: session 269 reissued`; (d) psql showed the old token resolving NOTHING and the new one on the **same loginid 269** with the window slid 08:26:51 → 08:27:32 and status still `ValidToken`. **CONTROL (the reason this is not circular):** with `tokenstatus` set to `RevokedToken` the identical journey landed on `/login` and logged `Session refresh refused for user 2: the token is not a live session` — so "the page still works" cannot be explained by "nothing ever expires". Screenshots in `test-results-cluster-j/` (4). **DB left as found** — both smoke `userlogins` rows deleted and re-counted at 0; no password, flag or seed row touched; no user invented. Host process killed, port 5390 confirmed free. Original verifier finding follows. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] NOT-IMPLEMENTED — VERIFIER ADJUDICATION - the cluster reported NOT-OBSERVABLE; I graded it NOT-IMPLEMENTED because the user-visible behaviour cannot occur, which is a feature gap rather than a missing test surface. RefreshTokenAsync exists (AuthService.cs:131; AuthSvc.cs:411 sets validatedUser.RefreshToken) and CustomAuthStateProvider.cs:67 STORES the refresh token in localStorage - but VERIFIER-CONFIRMED there is NO call site anywhere in source/BlogUI and Program.cs maps NO auth endpoint (only /health, /health/ready, /sitemap.xml, /robots.txt). The refresh token is persisted and never used; sessions can never refresh in the running product. The row's Done (pre-existing) 100% overstates this** ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — RefreshToken issued alongside AccessToken; the session survived 6 authenticated hops without degrading to anonymous. Expiry-driven refresh is NOT-OBSERVABLE within a short run and remains unexercised. | [view](#d-req-fn-008) |

Root cause: `blogpost.categoryid` is a NULLABLE BIGINT with FK `blogpost_categoryid_fkey`, but `BlogPost.CategoryId` is a non-nullable C# `int` and the picker's "-- Select Category --" carries the value "0", so an unselected picker reached the INSERT as 0 and PostgreSQL raised 23503. Fixed in the DATABASE, not in one screen, because the verifier reproduced the same defect independently on the BlogApp desktop head and any future write path can send the same 0: new migration `023-CategoryAssignmentAndImageMetadata.sql` adds trigger function `NormaliseBlogPostCategory()` + BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger `TrgNormaliseBlogPostCategory` on BlogPost, rewriting categoryid 0 → NULL (Category.CategoryId is BIGSERIAL from 1, so 0 is never a real key). ManagePost/BlogPostRepo were NOT touched (cluster C / cluster G own them). SMOKE PASS on :5389 as Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com`: 12/12 browser assertions. /admin/categories rendered 5 rows == psql `SELECT count(*) FROM category` = 5, every row a non-blank slug; create → 6 rows and psql row (id 11, slug `cluster-i-smoke-category`); rename → persisted; delete → back to 5. Saving a post with the picker left on its default now returns "Draft created successfully!" and psql shows postid 30 with `categoryid = NULL` (previously the raw 23503 error and no row); saving with a category chosen stored postid 31 categoryid 11. Visual OK at 1280px and 390px, 0px horizontal overflow. Screenshots `test-results-cluster-i/fn017-*.png`; script `scripts-cluster-i/smoke-cluster-i.mjs`. | REQ-FN-022 | Comment CRUD, approval workflow, counts (Phase 4/9) | Verified | 100% | [REQ-FN-022] 2026-08-07 — **row was stale; the Phase 9 build had already done most of it.** ALREADY EXISTED: migration `014-AnonymousEngagement.sql` (BlogComment gains nullable `UserId`, `IsEmailVerified`, `ModerationStatus`, `VerifiedOn`, `AuthorIpAddress/UserAgent` + 4 indexes; DB-verified applied), `CommentSvc.SubmitCommentAsync` with four gates (validate → captcha → `CommentSpamGuard` → double opt-in), `BlogCommentRepo.InsertPendingAsync/MarkEmailVerifiedAsync/SetModerationStatusAsync/GetModerationQueueAsync`, and the `PendingVerification → PendingApproval → Approved` state machine. **WHAT I ADDED — three real gaps:** (1) **BRD-38 was not implemented at all** — the `Blog.AreCommentsModerated` site setting existed in `SiteSettingKeys`/`SiteSettings` but nothing read it, so approval was unconditionally required. `CommentSvc` and `EmailVerificationSvc` now take `ISiteSettingsService` (cluster A's contract, not edited) and honour it on BOTH paths — at submit for an already-verified address, and at token consumption; any failure reading the setting **fails safe to moderating**. The double opt-in gate is never bypassed by the setting. (2) **BRD-39 bulk processing was a lie** — `CommentsList.ApplyBulkAction`'s `case "spam"` set `comment.Status = "Spam"` on the *view model only* and never touched the database, so the grid showed Spam while the row stayed Approved and publicly visible. Replaced with real set-based methods: `IBlogCommentRepo.SetModerationStatusBulkAsync` / `DeleteBulkAsync` (single `WHERE commentid = ANY(@CommentIds)` statement; approve carries the same `isemailverified = TRUE` guard as the single-comment path; delete removes replies first so none are orphaned) and `CommentSvc.ApproveCommentsAsync/RejectCommentsAsync/DeleteCommentsAsync` returning the count actually affected. (3) **The admin grid derived Status from `Published` alone**, collapsing Spam and Rejected into "Pending" and leaving the Spam tab permanently empty — now mapped from `ModerationStatus` (adds truthful `Unconfirmed`/`Rejected`). Also fixed a **page-breaking** defect: `<TabsList>`/`<TabsTrigger data-testid>` threw at render (TrBlazeUI declares no `CaptureUnmatchedValues`), and the ErrorBoundary hid it behind an HTTP 200 — **the whole moderation queue was unreachable**; hooks moved to `Tabs` (which does splat) and to spans this page owns. **Self-smoke 2026-08-07 (headless Playwright chromium, http://localhost:5405, `/post/the-markdown-kitchen-sink`, editor@techieblog.test) — PASS:** anonymous name+email comment (no account, captcha answered through its accessible question mode) → row 31 written `PendingVerification/published=f`, marker text **absent** from the public thread, token issued; `/verify/{token}` → `PendingApproval/isemailverified=t`, still unpublished, address added to `verifiedemail`; a second comment from the now-verified address returned "awaiting moderation" (BRD-38 default proven live); the spam guard **rejected 2 of 3 rapid resubmissions**. Bulk: ticked 2 rows → **approve** → both `Approved/published=t` in psql; → **spam** → both `Spam/published=f` in psql **and the Spam tab populated to 2** (impossible before the mapping fix); → approve again → back to 2. **RENDER-TRUTH:** page shows 3 comments, psql `count(*) WHERE postid=20 AND moderationstatus='Approved'` = **3**; no email address rendered anywhere in the public thread. **VISUAL-TRUTH** 1280 + 390 px: 0 visible overlap/clipping, `body.scrollWidth == clientWidth` (no horizontal scroll) at both; the 5 elements past the right edge are all `clipped:true` — `<code>` inside `overflow-x:auto` `<pre>`, and the `.tb-keyboard-fallback` visually-hidden fieldset (REQ-UI-027/057, not mine). 0 page errors. `test-results/cluster-e-*.png` (screenshots inspected). Build **0 errors**; unit suite **308/308 pass**, incl. 8 new comment tests. ⚠ Unconfirmed comments are shown to moderators labelled `Unconfirmed`; `GetAllComments` is documented as the administrative view that deliberately includes them, but the repo XML doc says they stay invisible to moderators — flagging the contradiction rather than silently picking a side. Smoke fixtures (comments 31/33) left approved on post 20 so the thread has real data ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — full state machine through the real UI: submit -> PendingVerification/published=f + one token; /verify/{token} -> PendingApproval/isemailverified=t still unpublished, verifiedemail row created, token isused=t; second visit -> 'Already confirmed, was not applied again'; confirmed-but-unapproved stayed invisible. A second comment from the verified address went straight to PendingApproval with no re-confirmation. Admin queue 14/14 rows, tabs All(14)/Pending(4)/Approved(9)/Spam(0), search 7==7 psql, approve -> comment became publicly visible. 7 seeded comments untouched · screenshot: .verify/shots/engage/admin-comments-1280.png** | [view](#d-req-fn-022) |
| REQ-FN-023 | Rating service — one per **email** per post, changeable, aggregates (Phase 4/9) | Verified | 100% | [REQ-FN-023] 2026-08-07 — **row was stale; the email re-key was already complete in the working tree and I verified it end to end rather than rebuilding it.** ALREADY EXISTED: migration `014-AnonymousEngagement.sql` PART B — `PostRating.Email` + `IsEmailVerified` added, `UserId` made nullable, legacy `UQ_PostRating_User_Post` dropped and replaced by **`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IdxPostRatingPostEmail ON PostRating (PostId, LOWER(Email)) WHERE Email IS NOT NULL`**; stored functions `UpsertPostRatingByEmail` (case-insensitive upsert, sticky verification) and `MarkRatingEmailVerified`; `PostRatingRepo` with `GetByPostAndEmailAsync/UpsertByEmailAsync/DeleteByPostAndEmailAsync` and **verified-only** aggregates (`GetAverageByPost`, `GetCountByPost`, `GetStatsByPost`, `GetTopRatedPostIds` all filter `IsEmailVerified = TRUE`); `RatingSvc` with captcha + double opt-in; BRD-42 display already wired on the post page (`PostRatingPanel`) and in listings (`HomeLatestArticles` → `PostCard` `ShowRating`). **WHAT I ADDED:** no schema or service change was needed — the gap was proof, so I added **5 unit tests** pinning the constraint from angles the existing suite missed: `ChangedRatingUpdatesTheSameRow` (same `RatingId` survives a change — it is an in-place update, never delete-and-reinsert), `RepeatedChangesLeaveExactlyOneRating`, `DifferentAddressesEachKeepTheirOwnRating`, `SameAddressRatesEachPostSeparately` (the key is the pair, not the address), `ChangedUnverifiedRatingStaysASingleParkedRow`. **DB-level enforcement PROVEN, not assumed** (the acceptance asks for the database, not just code): direct `INSERT` of a second row for the same post+email was rejected with **`23505 duplicate key value violates unique constraint "idxpostratingpostemail"`**, and so was a **case-variant** (`CLUSTER-E@EXAMPLE.COM`) — the `LOWER(Email)` index holds. **Self-smoke 2026-08-07 (headless Playwright chromium, http://localhost:5405, `/post/the-markdown-kitchen-sink`, anonymous, no sign-in) — PASS:** rated 5 via the panel's accessible radio fallback + captcha question mode → average moved **4.8 · 4 ratings → 4.8 · 5 ratings**; then **changed the same address to 1** → **4.0 · 5 ratings**, i.e. the average moved but the **count did not**, and psql confirms **exactly one row** for that email on that post (`ratingid 49`, `UpdatedOn` stamped). **RENDER-TRUTH:** rendered `4.0` / `· 5 ratings` vs psql `avg(rating) FILTER (WHERE isemailverified) = 4.00`, `count = 5` — exact match, and the 3 unverified rows on other posts stay out of the public numbers. **VISUAL-TRUTH** 1280 + 390 px: panel screenshots inspected, no overlap/clipping, no horizontal body scroll, 0 page errors — `test-results/cluster-e-rating-panel-{1280,390}.png`. Build **0 errors**; unit suite **308/308 pass**. ⚠ The rating panel's `.tb-keyboard-fallback` fieldset reports a 662 px box at a 390 px viewport (UA `min-inline-size` on `<fieldset>` beats `width:1px`); it is genuinely clipped and invisible, but it inflates `documentElement.scrollWidth` — belongs to REQ-UI-027/REQ-UI-057, left alone ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — anonymous rating wrote isemailverified=f and public numbers did NOT move (verified-only aggregate). After /verify/{Rating token}: count 5->6, avg 4.0->4.2, UI showed 4.2/'6 ratings'. Re-rating from the SAME address UPDATED the row in place (still exactly 1 row for that post+email), count stayed 6, avg 4.2->3.5, fresh load rendered 3.5 - exact psql match · screenshot: .verify/shots/engage/post-rating-390.png** | [view](#d-req-fn-023) |
