| REQ-NFR-027 | Move the hardcoded JWT signing key and AES key out of source | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-NFR-027] 2026-08-08 (later same day) — the carried-forward MAUI gap below is now CLOSED.** The residual noted that `source/BlogApp` never called `AppSecrets.Initialise` and would throw at first use of `AppEncrypt`; it did. **What changed:** `ConnectionSettings` gains `JwtSigningKey` + `AppEncryptionKey`, persisted through the **existing DPAPI/SecureStorage `ConnectionStore`** that already holds the database password — deliberately **not** the embedded `appsettings.json`, which ships inside the distributed binary and would put a live site key in every copy. `MauiProgram.ApplySiteSecrets` publishes both onto `IConfiguration` under `AppSecrets.JwtSigningKeyPath` / `EncryptionKeyPath` and calls `Initialise`. The call is **guarded, not unconditional**: a first run has no stored secrets and `Initialise` throws by design, so an unguarded call would abort composition before the setup screen could be shown — leaving the operator no way to supply the values that would fix it. `ConnectionSettings.IsComplete` now requires `HasUsableSecrets()` (presence + the same minimum lengths `AppSecrets` enforces, reusing its constants rather than duplicating the numbers), so a pre-existing blob reads as incomplete and reopens setup — the intended upgrade path. The retired-literal blocklist is deliberately **not** duplicated on the client; it stays owned by `AppSecrets` and rejects at startup. `SaveAndContinue` also refuses before the DB probe, so a secret-less blob can never be stored and bounce the restarted app back to setup unexplained. **Operator note:** both values must be **byte-for-byte the website's** — both heads read the same encrypted `SiteSetting` rows (27 present) and there is no key versioning, so a mismatch fails silently rather than loudly; the setup screen says so. **SMOKE (mine, `blogapp-smoke/nfr027.mjs`, app launched by the harness and bound by PID to its own WebView2 CDP :9333, all interaction element-scoped):** (1) app **launches** instead of throwing; (2) setup screen renders both new masked fields — `nfr027-2-filled.png`; (3) Save **enabled** with both secrets, **disabled** with the JWT key blank, **disabled** with it too short — length gate, not just presence; (4) save → process restarts → restarted app does **not** bounce to setup, lands on `/login`; (5) signed in as `Ravi@techieblog.com` → `/change-password`, **no "secrets have not been loaded" error** — `AppEncrypt` runs on the sign-in path, so this is the positive proof `Initialise` ran (`nfr027-6-after-login.png`). Build `0 Error(s)`. **Residual 5% (unchanged, OWNER ACTION):** the original values are in git history, so every token and every `SiteSetting` ciphertext issued under them stays compromised — rotating `AppEncryptionKey` makes existing encrypted rows permanently undecryptable (no key versioning) and the owner must re-enter them. Verifying the JWT signature on read still belongs to whoever owns `SvcUtils`/`AuthSvc`. ORIGINAL ENTRY FOLLOWS. **both secrets removed from source and supplied from configuration; startup now fails loudly without them; rotation proved to sign every existing session out.** **What changed:** new `source/BlogModel/Common/AppSecrets.cs` loads `JwtSigningKey` and `AppEncryptionKey` from `IConfiguration` once, in `Program.cs`, before any service is registered. Key names are flat PascalCase to match the existing `AppDbConString` precedent, so the same name works verbatim as an environment variable per Coding Standards §Environment Variables. `AppConstants.JWTTokenGenKey` and `AppConstants.AppSalt` are **deleted** — a reflection test (`CommittedLiteralsAreGoneFromAppConstants`) fails if either name comes back. Consumers repointed: `AuthSvc.GenerateJWToken` (the one line owned by this cluster, also switched `Encoding.ASCII` → `Encoding.UTF8` so a random key keeps its entropy instead of having every byte >0x7F folded to '?') and the three `AppEncrypt` sites. The retired literals are blocklisted **by SHA-256 digest, not by value**, so the blocklist itself does not put the secrets back in the repository. Banned prefixes in `AppEncrypt.cs` (`vResult`, `sResult`, `vInitTokenVal`, and the PascalCase parameter `GenDate`) fixed to bare camelCase. **Rotation invalidates sessions:** the JWT signature is never verified anywhere in this app (`SvcUtils.GetUserIDFromToken` uses `ReadJwtToken`; session validity is DB-backed against `UserLogin`), so rotating the key would NOT have invalidated anything on its own. `AppConstants.AccessKey`/`RefreshKey` therefore now carry an 8-hex one-way fingerprint of the signing key (`AccessToken-<fp>`), so a rotation moves the browser storage slot and the old token can never be presented again. **SMOKE (mine, headless Chromium + curl, Postgres `techieblog-pg`):** (1) boot with no secret → `[FTL]` + stderr `FATAL: TechieBlog failed to start. Required secret 'JwtSigningKey' is not configured…` and **exit code 1**; (2) boot with secrets from the environment → starts, `/health` 200; (3) signed in as Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` → redirected to `/change-password` (the `MustChangePassword` flag), `localStorage` holding `AccessToken-6fa477ae` / `RefreshToken-6fa477ae` with real `eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIs…` JWTs, and `6fa477ae` verified equal to `sha256(signing key)[0..8]`; (4) **rotation**: same browser profile, same origin, host restarted with a new signing key (`ec00b386`) — the still-valid stored session no longer authenticates and `/admin` lands on `/login?ReturnUrl=%2Fadmin`, screenshot `test-results/cluster-d/after-key-rotation-1280.png`; under the un-rotated key the identical stored session reached `/change-password`; (5) **no secret in any log** — grepped both live keys and the retired literals across the console capture and every `logs/techieblog-20260808*.log`: 0 hits. **Tests:** `tests/unit/Security/AppSecretsTests.cs` (12) + `tests/unit/Security/TestSecretsBootstrap.cs`; suite 380 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed. **Residual 10% / carried forward:** the values are in git history, so every token and every `SiteSettings` ciphertext issued under the old keys must be treated as compromised — rotating `AppEncryptionKey` makes existing encrypted settings rows permanently undecryptable (no key versioning), so the owner must re-enter them. The **MAUI head `source/BlogApp` does not call `AppSecrets.Initialise`** and is not owned by this cluster: it will now throw at first use of `AppEncrypt`/`AppConstants.AccessKey` until its `MauiProgram` wires the same call. Verifying the JWT signature on read (which would make rotation invalidate at the token layer rather than the storage-key layer) belongs to whoever owns `SvcUtils`/`AuthSvc`. ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS FOLLOWS. ⚠ **SECURITY.** Added 2026-08-07 by *build-phase, found by the BlogModels documentation pass. `AppConstants.JWTTokenGenKey` (`source/BlogModel/Common/AppConstants.cs:112`) is the **JWT signing secret, hardcoded in source**. Anyone with repository access can forge a token for any user, including an Admin — and the repository is the same one whose committed PAT is already REQ-NFR-025, so the exposure set is not hypothetical. `AppConstants.AppSalt` is a hardcoded **AES key** with the same objection (and is misnamed — it is a key, not a salt). Both directly violate the Coding Standards' "Never hardcode credentials". Note this is the same class of problem as REQ-NFR-025: because the values are committed, changing them in the file is not sufficient on its own — any token or ciphertext already issued under the old key must be treated as compromised. Acceptance: both secrets are supplied from configuration/environment (never a literal in source), absent values fail startup loudly rather than falling back to a default, existing sessions are invalidated on rotation, and no secret is written to a migration script or a log. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — static-only sweep + runtime fail-fast: ZERO key literals in any .cs or .json under source/; appsettings.json documents their absence; the host is up and serving, so user-secrets provisioning works** | [view](#d-req-nfr-027) |
| REQ-NFR-028 | Configure forwarded headers so rate limiting keys on the real client | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-NFR-028] 2026-08-08 — `UseForwardedHeaders` added with an explicit allow-list, and proved at runtime in both directions with real `X-Forwarded-For` requests.** **What changed:** new `source/TechieBlog/Middleware/ForwardedHeadersSetup.cs` builds `ForwardedHeadersOptions` from `ForwardedHeaders:KnownProxies` / `KnownNetworks` / `ForwardLimit`; `Program.cs` registers it and calls `app.UseForwardedHeaders()` as the **first** middleware, ahead of the correlation id, request logging and the rate limiter. The framework's implicit IPv6-loopback trust is cleared, `0.0.0.0/0`-style blanket entries are refused outright, and a malformed address or CIDR stops startup rather than silently shrinking the allow-list. `ForwardLimit` defaults to a single hop. **⚠ Non-obvious defect found and fixed during the smoke, not by inspection:** `ForwardedHeadersMiddleware` only consults its allow-list when it has at least one entry (`KnownNetworks.Count > 0 ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — static-only + unit test: ForwardedHeadersSetup builds options from KnownProxies/KnownNetworks/ForwardLimit; UseForwardedHeaders is the FIRST middleware (Program.cs 307) ahead of correlation id and rate limiter; ForwardedHeadersSetupTests passes. Not driven at runtime - would trip the shared auth limiter** \|\| KnownProxies.Count > 0`), so an **enabled middleware with two empty lists trusts EVERY caller**. The first runtime probe confirmed it — curl straight from loopback with no proxy configured successfully forged its own rate-limit identity (`Rate limit rejected GET /login from 203.0.113.9`). The setup now sets `ForwardedHeaders.None` when the allow-list is empty, which is what actually makes the middleware a no-op. **SMOKE (mine, curl against the booted host, REQ-NFR-005 limiter at 10/60 s):** (a) **known proxy** — host started with `KnownProxies = 127.0.0.1, ::1`; 12 requests to `/login` carrying `X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.9` → `200×10` then `429 429`, log `Rate limit rejected GET /login from 203.0.113.9` (the real client, not the proxy); immediately after, 5 requests as `198.51.100.22` through the same proxy → `200×5`, i.e. a **fresh per-client budget**, which is exactly the site-wide-collapse this REQ exists to prevent; (b) **no proxy configured** — same 12 requests, allow-list empty → still `200×10` then `429 429` but the log now reads `from ::1`, the header ignored and the transport address used unchanged. The captcha limiter's `CaptchaClientKeyProvider` was **not** modified: it still keys on transport `RemoteIpAddress` and still refuses to read `X-Forwarded-For` itself, which is now correct because the middleware makes that address true. **Tests:** `tests/unit/Ops/ForwardedHeadersSetupTests.cs` (10, including the empty-allow-list case), linked into `TechieBlog.Tests` as source like `GlobalExceptionLogging`; suite 380 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed. **Residual 5%:** verified through the REQ-NFR-005 auth limiter only — the REQ-NFR-024 captcha limiter shares the same `RemoteIpAddress` input and needs no change, but was not separately exercised behind a proxy; and the proof used loopback as the trusted proxy rather than a real nginx hop. ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS FOLLOWS. ⚠ **SECURITY.** Added 2026-08-07 by *build-phase, raised by the captcha rate-limiting cluster and deliberately not fixed unilaterally because it is a host-level change affecting two REQs at once. `source/TechieBlog/Program.cs` configures **no `UseForwardedHeaders` and no `ForwardedHeadersOptions`**. Behind nginx or any load balancer, both REQ-NFR-024's captcha limiter and REQ-NFR-005's already-`Implemented` auth limiter (`AuthRateLimit.BuildPartitionKey`) therefore see only the **proxy's** address, so every per-client cap silently becomes site-wide: one attacker exhausts the bucket and locks out every legitimate user. The captcha limiter deliberately keys on transport `RemoteIpAddress` and refuses to trust `X-Forwarded-For`, which is correct in the absence of this configuration — a client-written header would let an attacker mint a fresh identity per request, which is worse than no limiter. Acceptance: `UseForwardedHeaders` configured with an explicit `KnownProxies`/`KnownNetworks` allow-list (never a blanket trust), both limiters verified to key on the real client address behind a proxy, and the behaviour unchanged when no proxy is present. | [view](#d-req-nfr-028) |
| REQ-NFR-029 | Serilog rolling file ignores its size cap and lacks shared-write | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-NFR-029] 2026-08-08 — `shared: true` added; the size cap proved by writing past it on the running host, and the shared/rolling interaction settled empirically.** **Root cause of the 341 MB file: it predates the size cap, which is already working.** Evidence on disk before any change this pass: `techieblog-20260807.log` = 341,635,188 bytes (written by a build with no `fileSizeLimitBytes`), while the very next file `techieblog-20260807_001.log` = **52,428,823 bytes — the configured 52,428,800 cap plus the one event that straddled it** — and `techieblog-20260808.log` = 52,429,076, the same. So the cap rolls exactly where configured; the oversized file is a pre-cap artefact, not a live defect. **`shared` × `rollOnFileSizeLimit` are NOT mutually exclusive in Serilog.Sinks.File 7.0.0** — `SharedFileSink`'s constructor takes `fileSizeLimitBytes`, so a shared sink still overflows and rolls. The pairs this sink version does refuse are shared+buffered and shared+lifecycle-hooks, neither of which this head uses, so nothing had to be dropped. Verified by test, not by reading release notes. **What changed:** `Program.cs` file sink now sets `shared: true`, and the cap / retention / shared flag are driven from a `LogFile` configuration section so they can be exercised without a rebuild. **SMOKE (mine, booted host, cap driven down to 16,384 bytes for the run):** 400 requests wrote past the cap repeatedly and produced `…_004.log` 16,441 · `…_005.log` **16,385** · `…_006.log` **16,385** · `…_007.log` 14,210 (still filling) — the file rolls at the byte the cap names. **Shared write across real hosts:** with several instances of the head running at once, `/proc/*/fd` showed **three separate PIDs (51072, 51479, 51732) holding the same `techieblog-20260808_002.log` open for write simultaneously** — the divergence-to-`_NNN` this REQ describes no longer happens. **Tests:** `tests/unit/Ops/RollingLogFileTests.cs` (5) — every one writes past the limit against a real sink on a real directory and then reads the bytes back; includes a shared-mode roll and a no-events-lost-across-a-roll check (that test raises `retainedFileCountLimit` out of the way, because the head's 7-file retention deletes early files and makes a working sink look lossy — worth knowing before anyone re-runs this). Suite 380 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed. **Residual 5%:** the multi-process shared-write proof came from concurrently running sibling instances rather than a purpose-built two-host harness, and the retention policy still means bounded disk = cap × retained count (7 × 50 MB ≈ 350 MB), which nobody has signed off as the intended ceiling. ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS FOLLOWS. Added 2026-08-07 by *build-phase, found by the observability cluster. `source/TechieBlog/logs/techieblog-20260807.log` reached **341 MB** despite a configured 50 MB `fileSizeLimitBytes` with `rollOnFileSizeLimit` — the size cap is not taking effect, so a long-running host can fill its disk. Separately, the web head's sink lacks `shared: true` (the BlogApp head's has it), so a second instance silently rolls to a `_NNN` file instead of sharing the write. Acceptance: the size cap demonstrably rolls the file at the configured limit (proved by writing past it, not by reading the config), and two concurrent hosts write to one shared file without silent divergence. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — runtime on-disk evidence from the running host: logs/techieblog-20260808_009.log = 52,428,827 B and _010.log = 52,428,972 B - both the configured 52,428,800 cap plus the straddling event - with _011.log filling. Cap honoured, file rolls. shared:true, fileSizeLimitBytes, rollingInterval Day all set** | [view](#d-req-nfr-029) |
| REQ-FN-051 | Login audit trail cannot record a failed sign-in | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-051] 2026-08-08 — fixed end to end.** `LoginLogRepo`'s INSERT no longer hard-codes anything: it binds `userid, attemptedemail, success, ipaddress, useragent, attemptedon` from the entity, maps a missing/non-positive user id to SQL `NULL` (the FK is nullable, so an attempt against an address with no account is recorded instead of rejected), and clips each value to its column width so a hostile 5 KB user agent truncates rather than losing the whole audit row to a 22001. `LoginLog` (BlogModels) gained `Success`, `AttemptedEmail` and `UserAgent`, and `LoginUserId` became `long?`; `ILoginLogRepo` gained `GetRecentByAttemptedEmailAsync` (case-insensitive, capped) because the attempted address is the only key that spans a brute-force run. **The repo had no call sites at all** — the sign-in path wrote no audit row — so `AuthSvc` now takes `ILoginLogRepo` (registered in `BlogSvcInitializer`) and writes exactly one row on every arm of `AppLoginAsync`: throttle-refused, wrong password, unknown address and success. `AuthenticateAsync` now returns a private `LoginAttempt` record so a refused attempt against a real account is still attributed, without leaking account existence to the caller. Client IP + user agent are stamped onto the envelope by the host adapter from `IHttpContextAccessor` (new `SvcData.ClientIP` / `ClientUserAgent`). The attempted **password** is never a parameter of the audit path. An audit-write failure is logged and swallowed — a logging outage must not become a sign-in outage. Migration `021-LoginAuditAndForcedChange.sql` PART A guards the columns idempotently, backfills+NOT NULLs `Success`, drops NOT NULL on `UserId` and adds `IdxLoginLogAttemptedEmail`. **Smoke 2026-08-08 on :5411 against live PostgreSQL — PASS, proved from the DB not the code:** five wrong passwords for `Ravi@techieblog.com` wrote logid 15,16,17,19,20 all `success = f` with the attempted address (16-17,19 attributed to userid 1; 20 is the throttle-refused attempt, correctly userid NULL because REQ-NFR-005 refuses before the credential lookup); `attacker@nowhere.invalid` wrote logid 21 `success = f`, userid NULL; `editor@techieblog.test` wrote logid 22 `success = t`. The brute-force run is legible as a run of failures against one address in a 10-second window — and logid 18 (a concurrent cluster's genuine admin sign-in, `success = t`) sits inside that window, which is exactly the discrimination the old always-true column destroyed. 9 new unit tests in `tests/unit/Security/LoginAuditTests.cs`. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — VERIFIER-CONFIRMED independently: loginlog holds 23 rows with success=false alongside 236 success=true, each carrying attemptedemail, ipaddress and attemptedon, with userid NULL for unknown addresses (nullable FK). Failed and successful rows interleave so a brute-force run is distinguishable. The defect that made failed sign-ins unrecordable is genuinely fixed** | [view](#d-req-fn-051) |
| REQ-FN-052 | `svctoken` table is queried but never created by any migration | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-052] 2026-08-08 — Implemented. DECISION: DELETE, not migrate.** The whole `SvcToken` data-access stack is removed as dead code: `source/BlogEngine/DbAccess/SvcTokenRepo.cs`, `source/BlogModel/Interfaces/ISvcTokenRepo.cs` and `source/BlogModel/Models/SvcToken.cs` are gone. **Deciding evidence, in order of weight.** (1) **No caller exists anywhere.** A solution-wide sweep of every `.cs` and `.razor` under `source/` and `tests/` for `SvcToken` / `ISvcTokenRepo` / `SvcTokenRepo` returned hits in exactly three files — the repository, its interface and its model — each referring only to itself. Not a single service, page, host or test touched them. (2) **The DI claim in the original defect note was stale — it was never registered at all.** `BlogSvcInitializer.cs` and `EngagementSvcInitializer.cs` are the only registration entry points; their only token registrations are `IPasswordResetTokenRepo` and `IEmailVerificationTokenRepo`. There was therefore no DI line to delete, and the type was unreachable by construction, not merely uncalled. (3) **No live requirement needs it.** `docs/TechieBlog-BRD.md` records the "removal of the REST API layer" and `docs/TechieBlog-Architecture.md` ADR-002 states "No REST API layer — the `BlogSvc` API project was deleted" (REQ-FN-002). A service-token table backs service-to-service API authentication; with the API head gone, the feature it served no longer exists. A BRD/architecture grep for service token / service-to-service / machine-to-machine / API key / service account found nothing. `UserLoginRepo` owns interactive token persistence (migration `017-SecurityAndTokenPersistence.sql`). (4) **The missing table was confirmed against the live database, not just by reading scripts:** `SELECT to_regclass('public.svctoken')` returns NULL after all 21 migrations have run. Writing a migration would therefore have created a table for a feature that was deleted, adding schema and maintenance burden to make dead code reachable; deleting removes a latent `42P01` runtime landmine instead. **Proof nothing referenced it:** full non-incremental `~/.dotnet/dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx` → **Build succeeded, 0 errors**, with BlogUI genuinely compiled in that run (206 BlogUI.csproj diagnostic lines, BlogEngine 22, TechieBlog 4) — not a build that failed before reaching the consumers. `dotnet test` (integration excluded) **380 passed / 3 skipped / 0 failed**. No test referenced the deleted types, so none was added or removed; the deletion's regression test is the smoke below. **Self-smoke (`tests/verify/cluster-f-svctoken-removal.spec.ts`, 2/2 passed):** the host was booted by this agent and **started cleanly** — DbUp reported "No new scripts need to be executed", Kestrel listened on :7373, and the DI container resolved with no missing-registration failure, which is the exact failure mode a wrongly-removed registration would have produced. `/` rendered real article cards read from PostgreSQL (Getting Started with Blazor Server, Writing a Technical Talk That Lands, Dapper Patterns That Scale, with authors, dates and star ratings) cross-checked against a live `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM BlogPost WHERE Published = TRUE` = 11; `/admin` rendered the dashboard for the documented seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` with real aggregates (13 posts, 4 users, 20 comments / 11 pending, 23 subscribers, Recent Activity, Popular Posts). RENDER-TRUTH and VISUAL-TRUTH checked at 1280 and 390 — no horizontal page scroll at either width, no overlaps, nothing off-viewport; screenshots in `test-results/cluster-f/` (masters kept in the session scratchpad because Playwright wipes `test-results/` at the start of every run). No round-trip smoke was owed: that half of the acceptance applies only to the migrate branch. **DB left as found** — the seeded Admin's `MustChangePassword` flag was cleared to reach `/admin` and **re-armed to TRUE** afterwards (verified by re-select); no password hash was altered, no account invented, no fixture rows written. **Two smoke-harness traps found and documented in the spec, neither a product defect:** Blazor Server's prerender-then-interactive swap leaves two headers and two footers in the DOM for ~3s, so the spec waits for a settled render; and headless Chromium in this WSL environment composites a **stale surface for `fullPage` screenshots** — it produced an image of the home page showing a doubled layout and a loading spinner while `getBoundingClientRect` at that same instant reported one header, one footer and three laid-out post links, so the spec captures viewport-sized shots at two scroll offsets instead. **Note for the schema-versus-code pattern:** this was the third missing-schema defect of the phase but the first that resolved to deletion rather than a migration — the other two backed live features, this one did not. Original finding follows. Added 2026-08-07 by *build-phase, found by the BlogModels documentation pass. `SvcTokenRepo` issues `SELECT * FROM svctoken`, but **nothing in `source/BlogDb/PostgresScripts/` creates that table**. The repository compiles and is registered in DI, so any call reaches the database and throws at runtime. Currently **latent, not live** — no code path appears to invoke it today, which is the only reason it has not surfaced. Note this is the third missing-schema defect found in one build phase (the `SiteSetting` table was absent behind a 015→017 numbering gap, and `InsertPasswordResetToken` existed but with an unmatched signature), so the schema-versus-code gap is a pattern here rather than a one-off. Acceptance: either a migration creates `svctoken` with the columns the repository projects and a smoke exercises one round-trip, or the repository, its interface and its DI registration are deleted as dead code — decided deliberately, not left latent. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — to_regclass('public.svctoken') is NULL and the app serves every page normally; no SvcToken type remains in source/ or tests/ (3 matches are XML-doc notes recording the deletion)** | [view](#d-req-fn-052) |
| REQ-FN-053 | Manage Profile silently erases the site owner's resume on save | Verified | 100% | ⚠ **DATA LOSS — found and fixed 2026-08-08 by \*build-phase (BlogModels documentation pass, cluster P1).** **The defect:** `SelectBlogUserById` projected only **17 of BlogUser's 26 columns**, omitting `Username`, `IsSiteOwner`, `Title`, `Tagline`, `InstagramUrl`, `PhoneNumber`, `Location`, `CVFilePath` and `ResumeEnabled`. `ManageProfile.razor.cs:103` loads the signed-in user through `BlogUserRepo.GetSingle` → that function, binds the result into `ProfileFormModel`, and on Save writes the model back via `UpdateResumeFields` (`:247-258`). Every unprojected column therefore arrived NULL/false, rendered as an empty box, and was then **persisted as empty**. Opening `/admin/profile` and pressing Save — with no edits — erased the entire resume and switched `ResumeEnabled` off. On the site-owner row that is also the data source for the portfolio home (REQ-UI-049) and `/resume` (REQ-UI-036), so one Save blanked the public site. **The fix:** migration `022-FixBlogUserProjection.sql` drops and recreates `SelectBlogUserById` projecting **all 26 columns** (`DROP` first because PostgreSQL refuses to change a function's OUT list in place — 42P13). Deliberately projects the whole row rather than only the nine missing columns, so a future column cannot reopen the same defect. No C# change was needed: Dapper maps by name, so the widened result set flows straight into `AppUser`. **Evidence, measured not asserted.** BEFORE: `SELECT * FROM SelectBlogUserById(1)` returned 17 columns with `title`/`username`/`resumeenabled` absent, while the table held the owner's full resume (`ravi` / `Senior .NET Architect - Microsoft MVP` / `Hyderabad, India` / `+91 98765 43210` / `/uploads/cv/cv_1_ravi_kumar.pdf` / `resumeenabled=t`). AFTER: 26 columns (`pg_proc.proallargtypes` = 27 incl. the IN param). **Self-smoke `tests/verify/resume-projection-regression.spec.ts` — PASS.** Signed in as the documented seeded Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com`, opened `/admin/profile`, asserted RENDER-TRUTH (every resume input shows its stored value, `resume-enabled-checkbox` checked), pressed **Save with no edits**, then re-entered the page through the app's router to read persisted rather than retained state — all six fields and the checkbox survived. The owner row's md5 over the nine at-risk columns was **identical before and after** (`623549c6…72fc`). Downstream render confirmed: `/` and `/resume` both serve the live title, location and CV path. **COUNTERFACTUAL RUN (this is what makes the result meaningful):** the 17-column function was restored and the same spec re-run — it **FAILS** at the first render assertion with `Received: ""` on `title-input`, i.e. the field really is blank under the old projection; the fix is what makes the test pass, and the test is not vacuous. The failure lands before the Save click, so no blanks were written during the counterfactual. Fixed projection then re-applied and re-verified green. **Harness note:** the first counterfactual attempt *falsely passed* — `docker exec` without `-i` silently discards a heredoc, so the revert never ran. Re-run with `-i`; treat any counterfactual that passes as suspect until the revert is confirmed applied. **⚠ SYSTEMIC — this is the FOURTH instance of one bug class in this codebase:** a read projection that omits columns the write path persists. (1) `BlogPostRepo.GetAll/GetAllById` omitted `BlogWriter`/`PublishedOn`/`ScheduledPublishOn` (REQ-UI-017); (2) `SelectBlogUserById` omitted `MustChangePassword` (script 021, REQ-NFR-023); (3) `BlogPostRepo.SelectByIdSql`/`SelectBySlugSql` omitted `PublishedOn`/`ScheduledPublishOn`, so unpublishing erased a post's first-publication date (fixed 2026-08-08 under REQ-NFR-008); (4) this row. Instances 2 and 4 are the **same stored function**, fixed hours apart, because 021 added only the one column it needed. Unit tests and the compiler cannot see any of these — the `tests/unit/` fakes never execute the SQL — so only a live round-trip catches them. A projection-completeness gate is recommended and is NOT yet built. DB left as found; `MustChangePassword` re-armed `t` on all four seeded accounts. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-09] PASS — REGRESSION HOLDS - this is the data-loss defect where saving Manage Profile erased the site owner's resume. Opened /admin/profile as the site owner with every resume field populated and pressed Save with NO edits. md5 over the nine at-risk BlogUser columns was IDENTICAL before and after (ec6b0c7295ece185d0b819c571d6997c), and skills 13 / awards 3 / stats 4 / experience 3 all unchanged. Re-entered through the router (proving persisted, not retained, state): title, username and location still populated. Anonymous /resume afterwards still rendered 13 skill badges, 3 awards, 3 experience items. Repeated on two consecutive runs · screenshot: .verify/shots/resume/req-fn-053-resume-after-save.png** | [view](#d-req-fn-053) |

Smoked through the REAL `/admin/category` form against PostgreSQL as `Ravi@techieblog.com`: three categories saved with the SAME author-supplied slug persisted as `a1-smoke-collide`, `-2` and `-3`, proving the supplied slug survives attempts 2 and 3 (the old defect would have produced `a1-smoke-beta-totally-different`); a non-Latin name `日本語テスト` with the slug field blank persisted as `category-4b09dffafb42`, the hash fallback, not an empty string. All four smoke categories deleted afterwards. ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase (cluster M, found by new unit tests; NOT patched — production code was deliberately left alone so the finding stays visible).** TWO defects in one code path. (1) In every slug-collision retry loop the FIRST candidate derives from the author-supplied slug but every LATER candidate re-derives from the title/name, so an author's chosen slug is silently discarded from attempt 2 on — 12 loops: `BlogSvc.cs:347,427,1134,1201`; `CategorySvc.cs:192,258,482,542`; `TagSvc.cs:479,545,612,681`; same shape at `SeriesSvc.cs:561,637,713,784`. (2) `SlugGenerator.GenerateSlug` returns `""` for punctuation-only or non-Latin titles and **its own XML doc states callers must substitute an id-based URL** — no service does, so the row persists with an empty slug and becomes unaddressable by URL. Same call sites plus `TagSvc.cs:387,434`. Acceptance: a supplied slug survives every retry attempt; an empty generated slug never reaches the database. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — 10/10 slugs non-empty, unique and URL-safe; a hand-written slug is not overwritten by a title-derived one; the editor generated a correct slug live. The collision-retry branch needs a save and is covered by the SlugGenerator unit tests rather than by this run. | [view](#d-req-fn-054) |

| REQ-FN-057 | Latent read-projection omissions registered by the new completeness gate | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-057] 2026-08-10 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verify-phase run this session), with a follow-on fix the audit caught.** (a) `SelectPublishedSql`/`SelectFeaturedSql`/`SelectByCategorySql`/`SearchSql` now project `p.PublishedOn`, pinned by `PublicPostReadsProjectPublishedOn`; (b) `SelectPagedSql` is now a column-for-column twin of `SelectAllSql` with the author join, differing only by LIMIT/OFFSET, and its absent `Published` filter is REGISTERED as a deliberate admin-read narrowing enforced in BOTH directions; (c) all six `BlogSeriesRepo` reads compose the computed `PostCount` from one shared fragment, so `SelectByIdSql` can no longer drift from `SelectBySlugSql` — that repo now has NO declared narrowings. **The audit found the widening was only half-delivered:** every public listing still RENDERED `post.CreatedOn`, so the wider projections were invisible to readers. `Home.razor`, `HomeLatestArticles.razor`, `CategoryArchive.razor`, `SearchResults.razor` and `RssFeed.razor` now read `PublishedOn ?? CreatedOn`; a second cluster then finished `PostView.razor` and `TagArchive.razor`, the latter requiring `BlogTagRepo.SelectPostsByTagSql` to be widened first because it projected no `PublishedOn` at all (so fixing the renderer alone would have silently always fallen back). Smoked against a REAL divergence (post 1 `createdon` 2026-06-24 vs `publishedon` 2026-08-08/09, host restarted to defeat the new cache): RSS `pubDate`, sitemap `lastmod`, home featured, home latest, category archive, search, post detail and tag archive all date by `PublishedOn`; `/series` renders "3 Parts"/"2 Parts" matching PostgreSQL — the exact surface that showed "0 Parts" in REQ-FN-019. DB reverted; 0 diverged rows. **Residual filed separately as REQ-UI-059:** listings now DATE by `PublishedOn` while still `ORDER BY p.CreatedOn DESC`, so a back-dated post renders "Aug 09" yet sorts third behind Jul cards (visual proof captured). ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase; consolidates the LATENT instances found by clusters G and M and now guarded by `tests/unit/DataAccess/ProjectionCompletenessTests.cs`.** This project's #1 recurring defect class — 8 known instances, two of which shipped as real bugs (REQ-FN-015 leaked drafts anonymously; REQ-FN-053 NULLed nine resume columns on a no-edit save). Still open, all currently harmless but registered so they cannot drift: (a) `BlogPostRepo` `SelectPublishedSql` / `SelectFeaturedSql` / `SelectByCategorySql` / `SearchSql` omit `PublishedOn`, so every public listing — and the RSS `pubDate` and the sitemap — dates posts by `CreatedOn` rather than by when they went live; (b) `BlogPostRepo.SelectPagedSql` has no author join and NO `Published` filter and would leak drafts if ever wired to a public page (verified to have no caller today); (c) `BlogSeriesRepo` `SelectAllSql` / `SelectByAuthorSql` / `SelectByIdSql` / `SelectPagedSql` omit the computed `PostCount` that `SelectBySlugSql` provides — the exact shape of the already-fixed REQ-FN-019 "0 Parts" defect. | REQ-FN-058 | Deep-linking into an admin route bounces to the home page despite a valid session | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-058] 2026-08-10 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verify-phase run this session), fix extended by the audit.** `source/TechieBlog/Authentication/SessionCookieAuthenticationHandler.cs` is registered as the default authenticate AND challenge scheme (`Program.cs:331-340`); it reads the mirrored session cookie (named from `AppConstants.AccessKey`, so it rotates with the JWT signing key), resolves it through the SAME `IAuthService.GetUserByAccessTokenAsync` the circuit uses, and builds the principal via the shared `CustomAuthStateProvider.BuildIdentity` so HTTP request and circuit agree claim-for-claim. Failures return `NoResult` (never a 500); challenge 302s to `/login?returnUrl=…`, forbid to `/access-denied`. **Proven by a full document load with NO JavaScript at all:** raw `curl` carrying only the session cookie returned HTTP 200 and 76–93KB of PRERENDERED admin HTML for `/admin`, `/admin/analytics` and `/admin/subscribers` (the subscribers document contains the real emails and consent badges); the identical `curl` WITHOUT the cookie returned 302 → `/login`. Playwright hard `page.goto` plus a browser reload on all three ended on the requested route with live data (Analytics: Views 8, Unique visitors 4, Avg rating 4.2, Comments 12). **Negative direction holds:** an Author hard-loading the same three routes ends at `/access-denied` with 0 subscriber rows. **GENUINE GAP FOUND AND FIXED:** the cookie carries the 60-minute access token while the cookie lives 7 days and the refresh window 14 — so a bookmark opened over an hour later was challenged, the circuit renewed from the refresh token, and `LoginPage.OnInitializedAsync`'s already-authenticated branch called `NavigateTo(PublicHome)`, DISCARDING the returnUrl. Measured: the probe landed on `/` with title "TechieBlog". `LoginPage.razor.cs` now honours the validated return URL in that branch via `ResolveAlreadySignedInDestination()` — forced password change still wins (REQ-NFR-023), site-relative only (an absolute `https://evil.example.com` returnUrl was confirmed still rejected), and a fresh sign-in with no returnUrl still lands on the role route. ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase (found by cluster B while smoking REQ-FN-035).** A FULL page load of a protected route such as `/admin/analytics` prerenders UNAUTHENTICATED and redirects to the home page even when the visitor holds a valid session; only client-side navigation reaches the page. Cluster B's smoke had to sign in and then use `Blazor.navigateTo` to reach admin at all, and cluster L independently had to assert landing URLs for the same reason. Impact: bookmarks, shared admin links and browser refresh on any admin page all fail. Related to REQ-FN-008's session work (cluster J, this pass) but distinct — the session is valid; the prerender pass cannot see it. A full page load of an unauthenticated deep route correctly routes to `/login?returnUrl=...`, preserving the destination. | [view](#d-req-fn-058) |
| REQ-FN-059 | Subscriber consent model — IsConfirmed conflates 'never confirmed' with 'opted out'; unsubscribe tokens never expire | Verified | 100% | **[REQ-FN-059] 2026-08-10 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verify-phase run this session).** Migration `024-SubscriberConsentAndTokenLifecycle.sql` is CONFIRMED APPLIED in the live database (`schemaversions` row 2026-08-10 17:54:53) with `confirmedon`, `unsubscribedon`, `isconsentunknown`, `unsubscribetokenissuedon`, `unsubscribetokenusedon`, two partial indexes and trigger `trgsubscriberconsentchange` present on `public.subscriber`. Consent state is DERIVED, never stored (`Subscriber.ConsentState`), and `IsConfirmed` is deliberately left as the single mailability bit so no send query changed. The backfill is conservative — it never invents a `ConfirmedOn` (fabricated consent) nor an `UnsubscribedOn` (fabricated data-subject action), marking uninterpretable legacy rows `isconsentunknown` instead. **Withdrawal is now distinct from never-confirmed at the data level:** an unsubscribed row KEEPS its `confirmedon` and gains an `unsubscribedon`; a pending row has neither; `/admin/subscribers` renders all four derived states with dated detail text ("left Aug 10, 2026 · opted in Aug 10, 2026") beside the old Status bit, which shows Inactive for BOTH Withdrawn and Pending — the whole point. **Token lifecycle proven end to end on the real `/unsubscribe/{token}` route:** a fresh token unsubscribes and is burned in the SAME guarded UPDATE (`AND UnsubscribeTokenUsedOn IS NULL`, so a concurrent double redemption cannot double-write) with `confirmedon` preserved; a replay reports "already unsubscribed" without rewriting the instant; a 500-day-old token (400-day cap) and a burned token are both refused; a legacy token with NULL issuance is still honoured so delivered mail is never stranded; an unknown token gives the same non-disclosing message. Re-consent through the admin grid's own UPDATE fires the trigger and rotates the token with a fresh issuance and cleared burn, after which the old token matches 0 rows. Tokens come from `RandomNumberGenerator`, not `md5(random())`. **Sync/async divergence resolved (found by the async cluster):** `SubscriberSvc.Subscribe` only called `UpdateStatus(id,true)` while `SubscribeAsync` recorded consent and rotated the token — the two paths would have written different consent records. `ISubscriberRepo.RecordConsent` (sync) + a real `SubscriberRepo.RecordConsent` over the SAME `RecordConsentSql` constant were added, and `Subscribe`'s reactivation branch now matches its twin line for line; blocking on the async member was rejected per pattern-doc trap 7. All seeded smoke rows deleted afterwards (subscriber count back to 11). **Documented follow-up filed as REQ-FN-060:** `NewsletterSvc.BuildMessage` still reads the stored token rather than calling `IssueUnsubscribeTokenAsync`, so rotation is per re-consent rather than per issue. ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase (cluster H, while implementing REQ-FN-032's unsubscribe route).** Now that double opt-in is genuinely enforced (REQ-UI-056, cluster E, this pass), `Subscriber.IsConfirmed` carrying two meanings is the weak link in the consent chain: unsubscribing sets it false, which ERASES the proof of consent rather than recording a withdrawal, leaving an opted-out address indistinguishable from one that never confirmed. Separating them needs a migration (e.g. an explicit `UnsubscribedOn` / status column). Recorded alongside it: unsubscribe tokens are never rotated and never burned, so the same token ships in every issue a subscriber ever receives — a credential with unlimited lifetime, currently documented as an accepted trade on the Subscriber model. Acceptance: withdrawal is recorded distinctly from never-confirmed, and unsubscribe tokens are scoped or expirable. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — SubscriberConsentTests pass; the `unsubscribetoken` table carries the expiry/burn lifecycle. | [view](#d-req-fn-059) |

No horizontal overflow at 390. ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase (cluster F, while fixing the global 404 in REQ-UI-012).** `/post/<nonexistent-slug>` answers HTTP **200** and renders PostView's own not-found state. This is the same soft-404 class that cluster F fixed globally this pass with `UseStatusCodePagesWithReExecute("/404")` — but PostView short-circuits it by matching the route and rendering successfully. Search engines will index the miss as a live page. Acceptance: an unresolvable slug yields a 404 status with the rendered not-found page, matching the behaviour now proven for unmatched routes. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — FIXED and proven both ways: `GET /post/{unknown}` returns HTTP **404** off the wire AND through the browser, with a 43KB rendered 'Page not found' body. All five `BaseUrl` consumers still read the same key, so the single startup gate covers every one. **Proven against a real process, four ways** (running `TechieBlog.exe` directly — see the toolchain trap below): (1) Production, both absent → REFUSED TO START, exit 1, "FATAL: … Deployment configuration is invalid for environment Production" naming BOTH settings with their fix commands; (2) Production with `BaseUrl=https://localhost:5001` + a valid 42-char salt → still REFUSED, message "SiteSettings:BaseUrl is still the development value" and NO salt complaint, so the dev-default detector fired rather than the null check; (3) Production with `https://blog.example.com` + valid salt → STARTED, DbUp clean, `GET /` = 200 — the gate is not a blanket block; (4) Development with the repo's own settings → exactly one warning, host started, `GET /` = 200. The offending configuration was supplied purely through process environment variables, so no file needed restoring. 13 tests in `tests/unit/Configuration/DeploymentConfigurationTests.cs`. **TOOLCHAIN TRAP recorded:** `dotnet run` CANNOT test a non-Development environment — `Properties/launchSettings.json` hard-sets `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Development` and silently overrides `set ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Production`; the first attempt booted in Development and looked like the gate had failed to fire. Use the built exe or `--no-launch-profile`. ‖ **Added 2026-08-09 by \*build-phase (clusters H and B).** Two settings that fail SILENTLY and only in a real deployment. (1) `SiteSettings:BaseUrl` in appsettings.json is still `https://localhost:5001`. It is read once at construction and every unsubscribe URL mailed from a real deployment inherits it — producing a mailing nobody can opt out of, with no error raised. Directly undermines REQ-FN-032, which was implemented this pass. (2) `Analytics:VisitorSalt` is NOT configured, so `PostViewTracker` logs a warning and falls back to a built-in default salt; per the code's own privacy documentation the salt is load-bearing, because an unsalted IP hash is invertible over the IPv4 space. It is also write-once — rotating it resets every stored visitor pseudonym. Acceptance: both are required-at-startup deployment secrets that fail fast when absent or left at their development defaults. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — DeploymentConfigurationTests pass. | [view](#d-req-nfr-030) |

| REQ-UI-059 | Public listings date posts by PublishedOn but still sort by CreatedOn, so back-dated posts appear mis-ordered | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-10 by \*build-phase (clusters A1 and E, while completing REQ-FN-057; NOT patched — deliberately out of that row's acceptance).** REQ-FN-057 widened the public read projections and moved every listing renderer onto `PublishedOn ?? CreatedOn`. The `ORDER BY p.CreatedOn DESC` on those same listings was left untouched, so a post's displayed date and its sort position now come from DIFFERENT columns. **Visible, with live proof:** in `tests/.artifacts/e-tag-archive-date-desktop.png` a post dated "Aug 09" sits THIRD, behind cards dated Jul 08 and Jul 01. Any post whose `PublishedOn` differs materially from its `CreatedOn` — a long-lived draft, a back-dated import, a scheduled publish — reads as mis-sorted to a visitor. Changing the sort touches paging on `/`, category, tag, series and search, which is why it was filed rather than folded into REQ-FN-057 mid-pass. Acceptance: public listings sort by the same column they date by, and paging remains correct across all five surfaces. ‖ **[REQ-UI-059] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** Public listings now sort by the column they date by. ORDER BY changed from `p.CreatedOn DESC` to `COALESCE(p.PublishedOn, p.CreatedOn) DESC, p.PostID DESC` on the five public reads: `BlogPostRepo.SelectPublishedSql` (home/RSS/sitemap), `SelectFeaturedSql` (home hero), `SelectByCategorySql`, `SearchSql`, and `BlogTagRepo.SelectPostsByTagSql`. **The PostID tiebreaker is load-bearing, not cosmetic** — COALESCE ties are the normal case and PostgreSQL picks tied-row order per execution, so LIMIT/OFFSET paging without it can show one post on two pages and drop another. **Deliberately NOT changed, each checked per call site rather than blanket-edited:** `SelectAllSql` / `SelectAllByUserSql` / `SelectPagedSql` stay on `CreatedOn` because they are ADMIN reads carrying drafts whose `PublishedOn` is NULL (confirmed live: `/BlogsList` still lists authoring order, with the back-dated post last); the two series reads stay on `SeriesPartNumber`; the two scheduled reads stay on `ScheduledPublishOn`. No service-layer sort leak (`BlogSvc`/`TagSvc`/`SeriesSvc` do no in-memory ordering of these listings). **Migration `026-PublicListingSortIndex.sql`** adds `IdxBlogPostPublicListing (Published, (COALESCE(PublishedOn,CreatedOn)) DESC, PostID DESC)` because the pre-existing `IdxBlogPostPublished (published, createdon DESC)` — created in 001 for the old expression — **cannot serve a COALESCE sort**, so without it the fix would have cost a sequential scan on every public listing; the old index is kept for the admin reads. Applied and verified in `schemaversions` + `pg_indexes`. New gate `tests/unit/DataAccess/PublicListingSortTests.cs` (11 cases) pins the sort key and tiebreaker per listing and adds a **self-wiring** assertion that no `Published = TRUE` read anywhere in `BlogEngine.DbAccess` ranks by `CreatedOn`, so a listing added later inherits the rule. EVIDENCE: full solution build rung #4 0 errors; suite 1411 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped. Live smoke with post 1 temporarily back-dated to `PublishedOn` 2026-08-09 (`CreatedOn` Jun 24): rendered order EQUALS the SQL order on `/`, `/category/web-development`, `/tag/blazor`, `/search?q=blazor` at 1280 and 390; `/series` stays in part order; home hero became the back-dated post; no console errors, no horizontal overflow (`tests/.artifacts/a-req059-*.png` — the tag archive now leads with Aug 09 instead of burying it third, the exact inverse of `e-tag-archive-date-desktop.png` the row was filed on). **Paging proved** via LIMIT 2 at offsets 0/2/4/6 through the real `BlogPostRepo`: pages pairwise disjoint, union equals the full ordered set, and still so under a **deliberately created COALESCE tie**, stable over 10 repeats. Test data restored to exact original values and confirmed by SELECT; host killed by PID, port 5401 free. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS.** Rendered order on /, /category/web-development, /tag/dotnet, /search and /feed.xml equals `ORDER BY COALESCE(publishedon,createdon) DESC, postid DESC` from psql; dates descend monotonically; /series correctly stays in ascending part order; featured = newest published. **HONEST CAVEAT ON THE EVIDENCE:** this seed has `publishedon == createdon` on every published row, so rendering alone cannot discriminate the two columns — the verifier corroborated via the psql sequence and the covering index `idxblogpostpubliclisting btree (published, COALESCE(publishedon, createdon) DESC, postid DESC)`. That gap is closed from the other side by the build pass, which temporarily back-dated post 1 to `PublishedOn` 2026-08-09 and observed the tag archive move from third position to first. Neither piece of evidence suffices alone; together they do. | — |
| REQ-FN-060 | Unsubscribe tokens rotate on re-consent only, not per newsletter issue | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-10 by \*build-phase (cluster A2, while verifying REQ-FN-059; self-declared limitation, documented in the code's own XML docs).** REQ-FN-059 gave unsubscribe tokens a real lifecycle — burned on use, 400-day expiry, rotated on re-consent — which satisfies its "scoped or expirable" acceptance. But `NewsletterSvc.BuildMessage` still reads `subscriber.UnsubscribeToken` directly rather than calling `SubscriberSvc.IssueUnsubscribeTokenAsync`, so the SAME token ships in every issue a subscriber receives until they re-consent. The credential's blast radius is therefore still "every mail ever sent to this address" rather than one issue. Acceptance: each issue carries a token scoped to that send, and a token from an older issue is refused once a newer one is issued (or an explicit decision is recorded that per-issue rotation is not wanted, with the reasoning). ‖ **[REQ-FN-060] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** Per-issue unsubscribe tokens. `NewsletterSvc.DeliverAsync` no longer reads `subscriber.UnsubscribeToken`; it calls `SubscriberSvc.IssueTokenForNewsletterAsync` per recipient, inserting a row into the new `UnsubscribeToken` table (migration `027-PerIssueUnsubscribeToken.sql`) keyed to `(SubscriberId, NewsletterId)`. Blast radius drops from 'every mail ever sent to this address' to one issue. **DESIGN CHOICE, recorded because the acceptance explicitly permits a reasoned alternative: PER-SEND TOKEN ROWS, not a single superseding token.** The literal reading ('a token from an older issue is refused once a newer one is issued') would refuse a subscriber who receives issues #1 and #2 and then clicks Unsubscribe in the OLDER mail — a CAN-SPAM/GDPR-shaped failure strictly worse than the over-broad credential being narrowed, and the same reasoning REQ-FN-059 used to pick a 400-day expiry over a short one. So **an older issue's link STILL UNSUBSCRIBES**; every issued token stays valid until used, expired (400 days, unchanged), or superseded. Rotation therefore happens on re-consent only — the requirement's own title — enforced **read-side** by `IssuedOn < Subscriber.ConfirmedOn`, so no writer can forget to revoke; no revocation column and no trigger. Redemption is one CTE statement that burns the token row and records the withdrawal together, guarded on `UsedOn IS NULL`, leaving `ConfirmedOn` intact. Resolution tries the token table first and falls back to `Subscriber.UnsubscribeToken`, so every link already in a delivered inbox keeps working; that column is retained as the fallback and is NOT dropped. Send-time issuance failure falls back to the row-level token rather than mailing a footer with no link. **BUG FOUND AND FIXED MID-BUILD:** the first cut applied the supersede rule to both token stores and broke `SubscriberConsentTests.TokenInsideTheLifetimeIsAccepted` — a row-level token whose issuance merely predates a consent instant is legitimate, and refusing it would strand a subscriber holding a working link; the rule is now per-issue-only (the row-level token rotates by physical overwrite and needs none). EVIDENCE: whole-solution build 0 errors; suite 1411 passed / 0 failed (3 Testcontainers skipped) incl. 13 new tests; live smoke on :5404 as `Ravi@techieblog.com` sent two real issues to a segment-filtered subscriber → two distinct 64-hex tokens on distinct NewsletterIds; **OLDER issue's link unsubscribed** (only its own row burned, `ConfirmedOn` preserved beside a new `UnsubscribedOn`); replay + newer issue's link reported already-unsubscribed with no writes; re-consent via the real `/admin/subscribers` roster moved `ConfirmedOn` forward and rotated the row-level token; both per-issue tokens then refused as superseded and **indistinguishable from a bogus token**; rotated row-level token still worked. Screenshots at 1280/390 inspected, no overflow, refusal page degrades to a human contact route not a 404. All mutated rows restored and proven by SELECT (11 subscribers / 7 active, `UnsubscribeToken` and `Newsletter` empty, all pre-existing timestamps byte-identical). NOT DONE deliberately: no backfill of past sends, `Subscriber.UnsubscribeToken` not dropped, no unsubscribe-page markup change (its invalid state already offers a manual-removal route), `NewsletterSvc.UnsubscribeAsync` left in place but documented as superseded (row-level only, and its write erases the consent record). ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — `unsubscribetoken` exists with a unique `idxunsubscribetokentoken` plus a partial `idxunsubscribetokennewsletterid ... WHERE newsletterid IS NOT NULL`; all 13 PerIssueUnsubscribeTokenTests pass, including `AnOlderIssuesLinkStillUnsubscribes` (the recorded design deviation) and `ALegacyRowLevelTokenStillUnsubscribes`. Read-only throughout. | — |
| REQ-FN-061 | Unsaved theme selection on /settings mutates the server-side SiteSettings cache for every user | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-11 by \*build-phase (cluster E, found while removing the TR-049/058 Select workaround on `/settings`; out of that cluster's scope, deliberately not fixed there).** Selecting a theme in the Site Settings Theme tab **without pressing Save** mutates the process-wide cached `SiteSettings`, so a later fresh navigation by **any** user — including anonymous public visitors — renders the unsaved theme. This is a cross-user state leak from an abandoned form: an admin who opens the picker, previews a theme and navigates away has silently re-themed the live site until the host restarts. **Measured, not inferred:** `sitesetting.Theme.SiteTheme` stayed `trblaze-modern` in psql throughout, while the page served `Minimal Clean` until the host was restarted, after which it served `TrBlaze Modern` again — i.e. the DB was never written and the divergence lived entirely in the cache. Cluster E changed no DB rows. **Acceptance:** changing the theme selector without saving must not affect any other request or user — either bind the picker to page-local state until Save commits, or scope any live preview to the current circuit/session only; the cached `SiteSettings` is mutated only by a successful Save. Add a regression test that reads the effective theme on a second, independent connection after an unsaved change and asserts it is unchanged. Check the same tab's other settings (and any sibling admin form that edits cached configuration) for the identical pattern rather than fixing the theme field alone. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14] PASS — built and verified this pass, row CLOSED.** **Root cause was WIDER than this row's title.** `Settings.razor` bound its form to the aggregate returned by `GetSettingsAsync()`, which is the singleton's process-wide **cached instance** — so not only the theme picker but the site title, tagline, admin email, pagination, moderation switches, SMTP host/password and storage paths all wrote through to live site configuration on change, with no Save. Fix: `SiteSettings.Clone()` (deep — it clones `Smtp` and `Storage`, because a member-wise copy would leave the Mail and Storage tabs leaking), a new `ISiteSettingsService.GetEditableSettingsAsync()` that the editor binds to, and copies also returned from `SaveSettingsAsync`, `GetSmtpSettingsAsync` and `GetStorageSettingsAsync`. `GetSettingsAsync` still returns the shared instance because the layout calls it every render, and is now documented read-only. **Proved by live counterfactual, not asserted:** with the page reverted to `GetSettingsAsync()` and the host rebuilt, an anonymous request on its own connection was served `data-site-theme="minimal"` and `<title>LEAKED TITLE — never saved</title>` while psql still held `trblaze-modern`/`TechieBlog`; with the fix restored the same probes read the seeded values through an unsaved preview and after abandoning the form. Save path re-proved in the same run (saved `developer` -> anonymous sees `developer` -> restored to `trblaze-modern`), so detaching the model did not detach the Save button. Visual clean 1280+390. 11 new unit tests (`SiteSettingsAliasingTests`, `SettingsEditorBindingScanTests`); both counterfactualled — reverting the copy fails 3, a SHALLOW copy fails the nested test plus the `NotSame` assertion, and pointing the page back at `GetSettingsAsync` fails the source scan. | REQ-NFR-034 | PostViews INSERT and COUNT(DISTINCT) aggregate run on every post render — an unbounded per-render write and scan | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-10 by \*build-phase (cluster D, found by direct query-log capture during the REQ-NFR-001 re-measure).** With `log_statement='all'` scoped to the TechieBlog database, a warm `/post/{slug}` render was shown to issue 4 queries, two of which are view tracking: a `PostViews` INSERT — **a write on a read path, on every render** — and a `COUNT(*)` / `COUNT(DISTINCT VisitorHash)` aggregate over the same table. `PostViews` holds 12 rows today so the aggregate is effectively free and this is NOT currently a defect; it is a scaling landmine, because the table is unbounded and the aggregate is per-render with no cache (the per-post rating aggregates are cached under `CacheTags.Content`, but view counts deliberately are not, since they must move). Acceptance: view-count reads do not scan the full `PostViews` table per render (a maintained counter, a rollup table, or a bounded cache), and the write path does not block the render. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-034] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** Chose a maintained **ROLLUP TABLE** (migration `028-PostViewCountRollup.sql` → `PostViewCount`, PK=`PostId`, `TotalViews`/`UniqueViews`/`UpdatedOn`) over counter columns on `BlogPost` (would widen every `BlogPost` projection, including hot listing queries, for a number only the detail page shows) and over a short-TTL cache (does not remove the scan, only makes it less frequent and unpredictable, and fights the row's own 'view counts must move' constraint). **BEFORE** (statement log, warm `/post/blazor-render-modes-explained`): 4 queries, 2 of them view tracking — `INSERT INTO PostViews ... WHERE NOT EXISTS` followed by `SELECT COUNT(*), COUNT(DISTINCT VisitorHash) FROM PostViews WHERE PostId=$1`; `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` of the aggregate = `Aggregate → Sort (Sort Key: visitorhash) → Seq Scan on postviews`. **AFTER:** the render issues `SELECT PostId, TotalViews, UniqueViews FROM PostViewCount WHERE PostId=$1`, `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` = `Index Scan using postviewcount_pkey`, rows=1, **constant work at any table size**. `COUNT(DISTINCT)` is maintained incrementally by an `EXISTS` probe on `(PostId, VisitorHash)` inside the write statement — covered end-to-end by the pre-existing `IdxPostViewsPostIdVisitorHash`, so an index point lookup on the (now off-render) write path, not a scan. **WRITE OFF THE RENDER PATH:** `TrackCurrentVisitAsync` snapshots ip/user-agent from the live `HttpContext` (both gone once the response completes) and `TryWrite`s to a bounded `Channel`; `PostViewWriter`, a hosted `BackgroundService` registered in `BlogSvcInitializer`, drains it with a fresh `IServiceScopeFactory` scope per item and **every exception observed and logged** — explicitly NOT an unobserved fire-and-forget `Task`. **PROOF, by construction:** held `EXCLUSIVE` (not `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`, which also blocks readers and made the first attempt measure the read at 11.9 s — a measurement artefact, caught) on `PostViewCount` for 12 s, stalling the view write; a new-visitor render of `/post/{slug}` returned in **0.111 s** vs 0.216 s unlocked, and the write landed once the lock released. Under the old code the render awaited that INSERT and would have taken the full 12 s. The insert + counter upsert are ONE statement (CTEs Seen/Written/Rolled), so the rollup cannot drift from its source. **BACKFILL PROOF:** 028 backfills with the EXACT aggregate expressions the old per-render query used; a FULL OUTER JOIN of `PostViewCount` against a live aggregate over `PostViews` returned MATCH on all 5 posts (1:5/3, 2:4/4, 5:1/1, 8:1/1, 9:6/6) — no post's displayed numbers change at deployment; the backfill is an idempotent UPSERT that re-syncs rather than double-counts (verified by re-running it). SMOKE (headless Playwright, anonymous, host on 5402): byline rendered '15 views (13 unique)' at 1280 and '16 views (14 unique)' at 390, both screenshotted and inspected; value matched psql exactly; de-dup held on same-visitor reload; a new visitor moved both counters; the admin analytics dashboard (`Ravi@techieblog.com`) independently agreed at 16/14 for the post and 28/18 site-wide from `AnalyticsRepo`'s own unchanged aggregate. Build 0 errors rung #4; suite 1411 passed / 0 failed incl. 20 post-view tests. All 17 baseline `PostViews` rows restored, rollup re-synced, `log_statement` reset, port 5402 free. **DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED IN ITS OWN CODE:** `BoundedChannelFullMode.DropWrite` makes `TryWrite` return **TRUE while discarding the item**, so every dropped view would have been invisible — no counter, no warning, view tracking silently losing traffic; caught by `PostViewQueueTests.SaturatedQueueDropsRatherThanBlocking` and changed to `FullMode.Wait`, under which `TryWrite` still never blocks but returns false when full. DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: `AnalyticsRepo`'s admin-side `COUNT(DISTINCT)` queries and `PostViewRepo.GetSiteTotalViewsAsync` still aggregate `PostViews` directly — admin-only and not per-render, so out of scope; they also serve as the independent cross-check that the rollup is not drifting. `TrackViewAsync` (3-arg) unchanged and still reports the true write outcome; only `TrackCurrentVisitAsync`'s flag changed meaning from 'a row was written' to 'accepted for recording', documented on both the interface and the implementation. **One consequence to know:** the byline shows the count as it stood when the reader arrived, since the write is still queued when the figure is read. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS — no drift, checked twice independently.** Backend cluster: FULL OUTER JOIN of a live aggregate over `postviews` against `postviewcount` returned OK on every row, no MISSING-ROLLUP and no ROLLUP-ONLY. Admin cluster, later and on moved data (`postviews` went 17 → 27 mid-run): still zero drift across all 8 posts, rollup `SUM(TotalViews)=27` = raw `PostViews`=27, and the analytics popular panel reads the rollup exactly `[7,7,5,2,2,2,1,1]`. `PostViewWriter` is registered via `AddHostedService` (BlogSvcInitializer.cs:217) **and the running instance logged 'Post view writer started'** — hosted in fact, not merely registered. | — |

This matters more than it looks given the standing caveat on this project's accessibility position: axe reports 0/0 on public and admin, but only via an `App.razor` MutationObserver workaround (TR-054/063/064) and **no screen-reader pass has ever run**, so a missing document heading is exactly the class of defect 0 automated nodes will not catch. Acceptance: `/access-denied` exposes a single `h1` naming the page, and the heading order beneath it is contiguous. ‖ **[REQ-UI-060] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** `/access-denied` now exposes exactly one `h1`. Outline BEFORE: `[h3: Access Denied]` (no document heading at all); AFTER: `[h1: Access Denied]` with `data-testid="access-denied-heading"` — the page's only heading, so the order is trivially contiguous. **THE PAGE WAS AT FAULT, NOT THE LAYOUT:** `AuthLayout` contributes zero headings (brand mark is a `span`, `ThemeToggle` emits none) and was left untouched; a test now pins that. Cause was TrBlazeUI `CardTitle`, which hardcodes `<h3>` and exposes only `ChildContent`/`Class`/`AdditionalAttributes` (confirmed against the shipped assembly's XML docs, not guessed) — logged **TR-066**, worked around with a raw `<h1>` carrying CardTitle's own classes (`text-2xl font-semibold leading-none tracking-tight`), the same pattern as `VerifyEmail`/`Unsubscribe`/`_404Page`. **Side benefit — a second defect fixed:** `Routes.razor`'s `<FocusOnNavigate Selector="h1" />` matched nothing on this page before, so post-navigation focus management was a no-op for every denied visitor; it now works. Verified BOTH routes on the running host: the honest role-negative path (`contributor@techieblog.test` signs in, lands on `/`, requests `/users` [AdminOnly], bounced to `/access-denied`) and direct navigation — identical single-`h1` outline on each. axe = 0 violations at 1280x900 and 390x844 in light and dark. **CAVEAT, and it is the important part: the standard `wcag2a`/`2aa`/`21a`/`21aa` tag set CANNOT see this defect at all** — `page-has-heading-one` is a best-practice rule that is off by default, and `heading-order` only compares headings that exist, so a lone `h3` violates neither. That is exactly why the original 0/0 missed it. `page-has-heading-one`/`heading-order`/`empty-heading` were therefore run explicitly and are clean. The 0 is also still obtained under the standing `App.razor` MutationObserver workaround (TR-054/063/064), and **no screen-reader pass has ever run**. Visual: 3 themes x light/dark x 1280/390 = 12 screenshots inspected, zero sibling overlap, nothing zero-sized or off-viewport, no horizontal scroll; the `h1` resolves to a different oklch token per theme and per mode, so no hardcoded colour. `CardHeader` is a plain flex column with no `data-slot` grid dependency, so the swap is layout-neutral. **Negative control run: reverting to `CardTitle` fails 3 of the 4 new tests** (the 4th, the layout assertion, correctly stays green). DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED: `AuthLayout` (shared with LoginPage/ForgotPasswordPage/ChangePasswordPage; it is not the cause), the CardDescription/CardContent copy, and the REQ-UI-004 router hand-off logic. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — reached honestly (contributor → /users → /access-denied): headings in document order = exactly one `h1: Access Denied`, no gaps. axe run **explicitly** with `page-has-heading-one` + `heading-order` + `empty-heading` returned 0 violations — necessary because the default WCAG tag set structurally cannot see this defect. Anonymous /access-denied also has exactly one h1. ‖ **[REQ-UI-060] 2026-08-11 — Implemented. The plain `<h1>`/`<h2>` with hand-copied token classes in `Newsletters.razor`, `VerifyEmail.razor` (4 state headings) and `Routes.razor` (error boundary) are back on `TypographyH1`/`TypographyH2`/`TypographyP`: TR-020 gives them a `Size` that REPLACES the component's own size classes (so 24px is `Size="TypographySize.Xl2"`, never a `Class` override that loses on equal specificity) and TR-021/048 make them splat, so `newsletter-archive-title` / `newsletter-issues-heading` keep their testids on the component itself. h1 count asserted EXPLICITLY rather than trusted to axe: home 1, newsletters 1, post 1, /verify/{bad-token} 1, /404 1, /admin 1 · `tests/.artifacts/cluster-g/smoke-results.json`** | — |
| REQ-NFR-036 | Build and smoke output directories are not gitignored | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-10 by \*build-phase (clusters A2 and A3).** Two untracked output paths now appear in the owner's `git status` as build noise: `.smokeout/` at the repo root (the private per-agent `-p:OutDir` targets introduced this pass specifically to stop parallel clusters colliding on `source/TechieBlog/bin` with MSB3021/MSB3030 copy-lock errors — the convention should persist, so it needs an ignore entry rather than deletion), and `source/TechieBlog/logs/`, which currently holds **~349 MB across eight rolled 52 MB Serilog files** produced by the REQ-NFR-001 load runs. Related and still true: REQ-NFR-013's residual note that `fileSizeLimitBytes` does not bound total log volume — it rolls rather than caps — is confirmed by that 349 MB. Agents never run git (Hard rule 1), so the `.gitignore` edit and any cleanup are the owner's. Acceptance: both paths are gitignored, and a retention limit bounds `logs/` total size rather than only per-file size. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-036] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** **ALREADY TRUE, verified not re-done:** `.gitignore` already ignored both paths — `.smokeout/` (line 428) and `logs/` (line 411, plus `[Ll]ogs/` line 34 and `[Bb]in/` line 30); verified by **writing a pattern matcher** (agents cannot run `git check-ignore`) — all real paths match, and both are UNTRACKED so the entries actually take effect (no owner untracking needed). The web host was already volume-bounded by REQ-NFR-037: `LogFileSettings` anchors the path on `AppContext.BaseDirectory` and `SizeLimitBytes` 10485760 × `RetainedFileCountLimit` 10 = 104857600 B = **100 MB**, with `rollOnFileSizeLimit: true` — a legitimate total bound, arithmetic confirmed. **CHANGED — BlogApp was the real gap:** `MauiProgram.ConfigureLogging` set `retainedFileCountLimit: 14` with **NO `fileSizeLimitBytes` and NO `rollOnFileSizeLimit`**, so Serilog took its 1 GB default and, with rolling off, **SILENTLY STOPS WRITING** at the ceiling: worst case 14 GB *plus a log that goes deaf on the loudest day*. Added `fileSizeLimitBytes` 10 MB + `rollOnFileSizeLimit: true`, retention kept at 14 (the desktop head's deliberate history window), giving 10 MB × 14 = 146800640 B = **140 MB**, named as `LogFileWorstCaseTotalBytes` and logged at startup for parity with the web head. Combined worst case both heads = 251658240 B. Updated Coding Standards §Logging, whose prescribed snippet **still taught the exact anti-pattern both rows fixed** (bare relative `logs/techieblog-.log` + count-only retention); it now mandates an anchored path and all three sink args, and states the per-head arithmetic. SMOKE (Development, host on 0.0.0.0:5407, console captured to `/tmp` NOT `/mnt/c` to avoid the recorded 5.6 req/s throughput trap): startup line read `Log file sink writing to ...\bin\Debug\net10.0\logs - at most 10 files of 10485760 bytes, worst case 104857600 bytes total`; **content root was `source/TechieBlog` and logs did NOT land there, so REQ-NFR-037's two-folder regression stays fixed**; 6 anonymous page loads wrote to exactly ONE directory (444 KB, ~74 KB/request — consistent with the documented ~61 KB/request Development figure; Production is ~124 B/request). Bound behaviour proven directly on a throwaway path with the identical sink API: old config → 1 file, sink silent; fixed config → 3 files and total ≤ bound at 500/3000/20000 events. Host killed by PID, port 5407 free. NOT DONE ON PURPOSE: did not delete `.smokeout/` or any log file (siblings were building into them; existing volume cleanup is the owner's), did not touch `source/TechieBlog/Program.cs` (cluster C) — no change was needed there. BlogApp declares `LogFileWorstCaseTotalBytes` = 10 MB x 14 = 140 MB with `rollOnFileSizeLimit: true`. Both are TOTAL bounds, not per-file sizes. **The verifier caught a roll actually happening**: `techieblog-20260811.log` capped at 10,485,921 bytes and rolled to `_001` during the session, directory holding 3 files / ~19 MB. LogVolumeBoundTests pass including the negative control. | — |

SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verify-phase run).** **Root cause CONFIRMED, not assumed:** the Serilog sink path was the bare relative string `logs/techieblog-.log`, which Serilog resolves against the process **working directory** — `dotnet run --project source/TechieBlog/TechieBlog.csproj` sets cwd to the PROJECT folder (→ `source/TechieBlog/logs/`, **305 MB**) while the built exe launched from the repo root uses the repo root (→ `logs/`, **6.2 MB**). Same app, same day, two folders, and nothing in the log naming either. Note `IHostEnvironment.ContentRootPath` would NOT have fixed it — the content root defaults to `Directory.GetCurrentDirectory()`, the very thing that varies. **Fix:** new `TechieBlog.Configuration.LogFileSettings` resolves against **`AppContext.BaseDirectory`** (the folder the assembly loaded from, identical however the host is launched), overridable by `LogFile:Path` (a DIRECTORY; relative values resolve against the same anchor); the bootstrap `ConfigurationBuilder` now also `SetBasePath(AppContext.BaseDirectory)` for the same reason. **Proven by two runs** — `dotnet run --project` (cwd = the project dir) and the built exe with **cwd = `C:\`** in Production — both announced the same log directory; no `C:\logs` was created. **Volume bounded:** was 50 MB × 7 = 350 MB (and the observed 305 MB was that bound working exactly as written — capping a FILE is not capping a DISK); now **10 MB × 10 = 100 MB worst case per host**, stated once in `LogFileSettings.WorstCaseTotalBytes` and announced at startup. **Proven empirically, not from documentation:** a throwaway run with a 256 KB limit and 3 retained files was driven to sequence `_093` (~24 MB written) and settled at 3 files / 553,953 bytes, inside the 786,432-byte bound. **Container behaviour:** console is unconditional and primary (Docker captures stdout); the file sink is now conditional on `LogFile:Enabled`, which the Dockerfile sets to `false`, so nothing is written into the ephemeral layer — verified inside the running container (`/app/logs` does not exist). **Development verbosity is deliberately unchanged** (Debug on Components/SignalR, ~61 KB/request) with a comment recording that bounded retention is what makes it safe. `.gitignore`: `logs/` (line 411) is a bare directory pattern and matches both locations — verified; **`.smokeout/` was matched by nothing and was added**. The 311 MB of legacy logs was cleared centrally by the orchestrator after this landed. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14] PASS — re-verified, row CLOSED.** Both halves re-confirmed on the working tree and at runtime. **Anchoring:** the sink path is resolved through `LogFileSettings.Resolve(bootstrapConfiguration, AppContext.BaseDirectory)` (`Program.cs:83`), not a bare relative string, and the two-directory symptom is gone — a tree sweep finds **no `logs/` in the repo root and none under `source/TechieBlog/`**; the only log folders present are `.git/logs` and the gitignored per-agent `.buildout/*/logs` from earlier runs. **Volume bound:** all three Serilog settings are present together (`retainedFileCountLimit`, `fileSizeLimitBytes`, `rollOnFileSizeLimit: true` — `Program.cs:120-122`), which is the combination that stops the silent 1 GB stall; the leftover `.buildout/cluster-f/logs` measures 84 MB across 9 files (~9.3 MB each), i.e. the roll limit observably doing its job. Unit coverage green in the same run (`--filter FullyQualifiedName~RollingLogFile|LogFile`). | — |

new `BlogEngine.Storage.UploadsLocation` derives both from one value so writer and reader cannot disagree, and the pre-existing `/uploads` `PhysicalFileProvider` route was **repointed rather than duplicated**. **Credential handling:** the image build authenticates the private TrBlazeUI feed via a **BuildKit secret** (`RUN --mount=type=secret,id=nuget_pat`), writing and `rm`-ing the config in the SAME layer — never an `ARG`, never an `ENV`, never a committed `nuget.config` (REQ-NFR-025). Please try again." — the failure is **invisible server-side**; after `chown -R 1654:1654` the very next upload succeeded **with no restart**. Failure is transactionally clean (no partial file, no orphan `blogimage` row). **DataProtection key ring was NOT persisted** — fixed by a second bind mount `/srv/data/techieblog/dp-keys:/home/app/.aspnet/DataProtection-Keys` (+ `mkdir` in the workflow, + the same `chown`). And the deploy user is a full `(ALL : ALL) ALL` sudoer — the docs' "scoped to ensure-db only" was WRONG; only the NOPASSWD entry is scoped, which is what actually constrains CI, so the container-based chown remains necessary. **A defect in the recon script itself is recorded rather than hidden:** it reported "appuser CANNOT create tables — GRANTs needed" when the real error was `database "techieblog" does not exist` (expected — `ensure-db` creates it at deploy time), conflating connection failure with permission denial.
