the ephemeral fallback is **retained deliberately** and documented as dormant, because deleting it would turn an expired PAT into an unexplained `unauthorized` instead of a warned one. **`SEQ_API_KEY` was promoted from "optional, you do not need it" to §2 step 3** — one of exactly two genuinely manual per-app tasks in the portfolio (the other being DNS), with the full Seq-UI procedure (title `techieblog`, applied property `App=techieblog`). **TWO CONTRADICTIONS BETWEEN THE OWNER'S OWN SOURCE DOCUMENTS were found, and adjudicated in the doc rather than silently resolved:** **(1) the Seq secret's NAME** — runbook v5 §6.3 says `SEQ_APIKEY_BLOG`, brief v3 §2 says `SEQ_API_KEY` with an explicit rationale ("the workflow YAML stays byte-identical — only the value differs"). **This repo follows the brief**, since the workflow reads `SEQ_API_KEY` and the brief is the newer document citing §6.3 as its own source; because a mis-named secret **fails silently** (logs still ship, just unattributed) the discrepancy is called out in §0, §2 step 3 and the workflow's own `::warning::`. **(2) the CONTAINER USER** — brief §0 mandates root with **no `USER` directive**, on the premise that a non-root user cannot write the `ravi`-owned uploads bind mount; this repo runs **non-root UID 1654**. **Deviation kept deliberately and documented**, because the premise does not hold here: the pipeline chowns both bind-mounted leaf directories to `1654:1654` every deploy and then probes writability — a mechanism proven in both directions against real Docker on 2026-08-11. Running the public web container as root to avoid a chown the pipeline already performs is the worse trade; §0 records the exact two-line change needed to adopt the brief's shape and warns that removing only one half breaks uploads. **Also folded in:** `sudo /srv/checkup.sh` added as §7 post-deploy step 5 and to §11 routine ops (brief §6 names it the post-deploy verification); §0 rewritten from a ledger of assumptions into a settled-facts table; the GHCR troubleshooting entry rewritten to name **expired stored PAT** as the likely cause with runbook Step 12 as the fix, explicitly warning against "fixing" it by adding a pull PAT to a GitHub secret (brief-prohibited); all references repointed from `bluehost-vps-runbook-v3.md` → `-v5` and `claude-code-deployment-brief.md` → `-v3`. **Evidence, executed:** `deploy.yml` parses (3 jobs, **21** steps); **14/14** shell blocks pass `bash -n`; the Render step **run for real** in both Seq branches — key absent and key present — each producing `SeqUrl: "http://seq:5341"`, 237 lines, **zero surviving `${…}`**, and `docker compose config` **valid**; `vars.SEQ_URL` confirmed absent from the workflow. HTML re-rendered and validated: **58/58 internal anchors resolve**, no unbalanced tags, no external refs beyond the two shell CDNs, zero literal `**` leakage. **Zero files under `source/` touched**, so the recorded GREEN build state is unchanged and no build was claimed. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-038] 2026-08-16c — `ciuser` DEDICATED CI ACCOUNT (runbook v5.2 §2.1 / brief v3.2). THREE PIPELINE DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, ONE OF WHICH WOULD HAVE FAILED THE FIRST DEPLOY. Stays `Implemented`.** The owner created a dedicated CI account and supplied both updated specs; auditing this pipeline against them found three problems, all measured rather than reasoned about. **(1) BLOCKER — the GHCR credential does not cover `ciuser`.** Brief v3.2 §0 still asserts "the server is already authenticated as `techierathore` via a stored PAT … the workflow must not run `docker login` over SSH", but that answer predates the account change and the two facts do not compose: **Docker registry credentials are PER-USER** (`~/.docker/config.json`); membership of the `docker` group grants access to the **daemon** and nothing at the **registry**; and runbook **Step 12 ran as `ravi`**, before `ciuser` exists (Part 2.1 comes later). So `/home/ciuser/.docker/config.json` does not exist and `docker compose pull` of a private image as `ciuser` fails `unauthorized`. **This deploy survives only because the ephemeral-login fallback was deliberately retained on 2026-08-16b as a safety net for a state the brief said could not happen — it is now the LIVE path.** Deleting it to satisfy the letter of the brief would break the deploy. Documented in §0 as contradiction 0, with the one-command remedy (`sudo -u ciuser -H bash -c 'echo <PAT> | docker login ghcr.io …'`) that restores spec compliance and returns the fallback to dormancy. **(2) The ownership fix would have cut `ciuser` out of the directories it creates.** `chown -R 1654:1654` replaces group `webops` with group `1654`, and `/srv/apps`, `/srv/data`, `/srv/caddy/sites` are exactly where runbook v5.2 §2.1 grants ciuser access **through that group plus setgid**. The concrete breakage: §8's documented uploads-migration `rsync`, which connects as `VPS_USER` — now `ciuser` — would fail `Permission denied` after the first deploy. Corrected to `chown -R 1654` (owner only) + `chmod g+ws` on the directory itself, never recursively onto files. **Proven against real Docker on BOTH a Debian base (what the app image is) and Alpine:** resulting mode `drwxrwsr-x`, owner `1654`, group unchanged; **container UID 1654 writes AND a `webops`-group user writes** (the negative control matters — with plain 755 and the group preserved, the group user is still DENIED, so the `g+w` bit is load-bearing, not decoration); a file created by the container inherits `webops` via setgid, which is what keeps it readable in the `/srv/data` backup archive; idempotent across three consecutive runs; the parent directory never touched; uploaded files remain `-rw-r--r--`. **(3) A silent-false-pass removed from the ownership guard.** It read `n=$(find "$d" ! -uid 1654 -print | wc -l)` and only chowned when `n > 0`. `-uid` is a **GNU findutils** predicate that **BusyBox does not implement**, so on a musl base `find` exits non-zero, `wc -l` prints `0`, and the guard reports *"already correct, nothing to do"* while the chown never runs — and `set -e` never fires, because the failing command is inside a substitution and `wc` succeeded. **Reproduced directly on `alpine:latest`.** Latent rather than live (the app image is Debian), and now gone: the chown is unconditional, which is idempotent and costs milliseconds. **Two quoting bugs in the new inline comments were caught by the `bash -n` gate before they could ship**, both in the `sh -c '…'` inner script: first a single stray apostrophe (possessive of a noun), which ends the quoted string early and kills the step with `syntax error near unexpected token 'done'`; then — after a careless fix — an **even** number of apostrophes, which is worse, because the quoting re-balances, `bash -n` passes, and the text between them is silently exposed to expansion by the OUTER shell. An editors-note in the file now states the rule. This is the third time on this project that a check has been able to report success **by failing**, which is why the gate exists. **Compliance audit against the new account model, all passing:** the workflow executes **exactly one** `sudo` — `/usr/local/bin/ensure-db`, the sole entry in ciuser's allowlist — contains **no** `sudo docker` (which the allowlist would reject anyway, and is unnecessary since ciuser is in the `docker` group), and **never hardcodes an SSH username**; every step reads `VPS_USER`. **Separately, `deploy.yml` triggered on `push: branches: [main]` only, and the trigger was widened to `[main, master]`** so a push to a legacy or renamed branch still deploys rather than silently doing nothing — a non-matching branch produces **no Deploy run at all**, which is indistinguishable from a broken pipeline because there is no failed job to read. **CORRECTION, same day:** the widening was proposed on the premise that this repository's main branch was `master`, read from a stale session-start snapshot. **The owner confirmed the branch is `main`.** The premise was wrong; the change itself was harmless — both names are listed, so the deploy fires either way — but the surrounding documentation told the owner to push to the wrong branch for part of a day. All references corrected to `main`, and at the owner's instruction the `master` fallback was **removed** — the trigger is now `branches: [main]`, one production branch with no second name to keep in sync. **Recorded because it is the same failure shape as the two recon reversals: an environment fact taken from a cached snapshot was treated as ground truth instead of being confirmed with the owner** — and unlike the server-state facts, this one could not be probed independently, since the harness denies the VCS tooling outright. When a fact cannot be verified, say so and ask; do not launder a snapshot into a finding. DNS was re-verified as already live (apex + `www` → `50.6.45.249`). **Evidence:** `deploy.yml` parses (3 jobs / **21** steps); **14/14** shell blocks pass `bash -n`; **0** apostrophes inside the `sh -c` inner script (asserted programmatically); the render step executed → **245** lines, zero surviving `${…}`, `docker compose config` **VALID**; Prod-Deploy-Checklist HTML re-rendered with **60/60 internal anchors resolving**, no unbalanced tags, CDN-only externals. **Zero files under `source/` touched** — recorded GREEN build state unchanged, no build claimed. | — |
| REQ-NFR-039 | A green deploy does not prove DbUp migrations applied | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-11 by \*build-phase (cluster K, found while reconciling the deployment pipeline; documented, NOT fixed — `source/**` was out of that pass's scope).** DbUp runs at host startup and creates its own `schemaversions` table before applying `source/BlogDb/PostgresScripts`. If `appuser` lacks DDL rights on the `techieblog` database — `ensure-db` may grant only `CONNECT`; the helper lives on the VPS and is not in this repository, so this is unverified either way — migrations throw while **the process can still come up**. `/healthz` carries the readiness predicate but its database check tests the **connection**, not schema presence, so it returns 200 and the pipeline's `verify` job goes green. Symptom: the deploy reports success and the site is up but empty or broken. Mitigated for now by a mandatory runbook step (`docs/Prod-Deploy-Checklist.md` §5a) telling the owner to probe `CREATE TABLE` as `appuser` before the first deploy, plus a troubleshooting entry — but a runbook step is not a gate. Acceptance: a health check (or a startup gate) asserts that DbUp's `schemaversions` table exists and that the expected latest script is journalled, so a migration failure turns the deploy red instead of shipping an empty site. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-039] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** New `SchemaMigrationProbe` (BlogEngine) + `SchemaMigrationHealthCheck` (TechieBlog), registered as a third `ready`-tagged check so it lands on `/healthz` — the URL the pipeline curls. **The expectation is DERIVED, never hardcoded:** `MigrationScripts.ResolvePath()` in `Program.cs` is called ONCE and handed both to `BlogDbSvc.UpgradeDatabase` and to the probe, so the probe enumerates the exact folder DbUp was pointed at and demands every `*.sql` in it appear in the `schemaversions` journal. **Proven live: the sibling clusters added 026/027/028 during this very pass and the gate picked them up with no code change** — it reports 'DbUp journal records all 27 migration scripts'. `Unhealthy` (never `Degraded`) so `/healthz` answers 503. **NEGATIVE CONTROLS**, run against a scratch DB `TechieBlogGateProbe` created and dropped inside `WinPostgre` (the real TechieBlog DB was never touched — still 27 rows; `AppMngrDb`/`TkStoriesDb` untouched): (1) journalled-but-BEHIND, deleted 2 rows → 503, 'DbUp journal is BEHIND the migration set: 25 of 27 scripts are recorded, and these have not applied — 026-PublicListingSortIndex.sql, 028-PostViewCountRollup.sql', plus an ERR log line; (2) journal ABSENT, dropped `schemaversions` → 503, 'journal table does not exist, so NO migration has been applied... application role almost certainly lacks CREATE rights'; (3) wholesale, 0 of 27 → names the first 5 scripts + '(and 22 more)'. Restored → 200 Healthy. **TWO REAL BUGS FOUND BY RUNNING THE NEGATIVE CONTROL — the gate would have been fictional without them:** (a) the probe called `connection.Open()` on a connection `DbConnectionFactory` already returns open ('Connection already open'), so it was 503-ing for the wrong reason; (b) **THE IMPORTANT ONE — `/healthz` was covered by `AddOutputCache`'s 1-minute `AddBasePolicy`**, so after the schema broke the probe kept answering `200 Healthy` with `Age: 54` for the rest of the cached minute. Only 200s are cached, so the staleness is **one-directional: STALE GREEN, never stale red** — precisely the wrong direction for a deploy gate, and it would have silently undermined this entire requirement. Fixed with `.CacheOutput(p => p.NoCache())` on `/health`, `/health/ready` and `/healthz`; re-verified with three warm-up 200s then breaking the journal — the very next poll (no sleep) is 503 and no response carries an `Age` header. `Prod-Deploy-Checklist` §5a and the 'site is up but completely empty' troubleshooting entry rewritten: §5a no longer claims to be the only defence, and the troubleshooting entry now has a two-row table mapping the two health descriptions to their different fixes. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — `/healthz` reports `{"name":"schema","status":"Healthy","description":"DbUp journal records all 27 migration scripts"}`; independently corroborated as 27 `*.sql` on disk = 27 rows in `schemaversions`. **The expectation is derived, not hardcoded — proven by this very pass**: three sibling clusters added migrations 026/027/028 while the gate was being built and it picked them all up with no code change. **Cache headers clean**: `no-store, no-cache`, `Expires: 0`, `Pragma: no-cache`, and **no `Age` header across 6 repeated hits** — the stale-green hazard (a 60s output cache that kept answering 200 Healthy after the schema broke) is closed. 7 SchemaMigrationProbeTests pass. The real database was never touched; the failure paths were negative-controlled on a scratch DB during the build pass. | — |
| REQ-NFR-040 | BlogImageService swallows an upload access-denied without logging it | Verified | 100% | **Added 2026-08-11 by \*build-phase (clusters L and M, found by running the containerised app against a deliberately unwritable bind mount; documented, NOT fixed — `source/**` was outside those clusters' ownership).** When the uploads directory is not writable by the container's `uid 1654`, the upload fails and **nothing anywhere says so**: the container is Up, `/healthz` returns 200 Healthy, the startup log prints the normal "Uploaded media served from /app/uploads at /uploads (configured: True)", and the container log contains **zero `[ERR]`, `[WRN]` or `[FTL]` entries**. The admin sees only the generic "Upload failed — An error occurred while uploading the file. Please try again." An operator grepping the logs finds absolutely nothing, so the only way to diagnose it is to know to probe (`docker exec techieblog touch /app/uploads/probe`). Cause: `BlogImageService` catches the access-denied exception without logging it. This is the same class of defect as REQ-NFR-031's swallow-and-log gaps, and it is the difference between a probe-for-it and a tells-you-about-it failure. Note the failure IS transactionally clean — no partial file on disk and no orphaned `blogimage` row — so only observability is at fault, not integrity. Mitigated for now by a runbook entry (`docs/Prod-Deploy-Checklist.md` §10, "Uploads fail with a generic error and nothing in the logs") and the mandatory `chown` step, but a runbook is not a log line. Acceptance: an upload that fails for permissions (or any IO reason) logs an error naming the target path and the underlying exception, and the admin message distinguishes "server cannot write here" from a generic retry-able failure. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-040] 2026-08-11 — SMOKE PASS (capped at `Implemented`; no verifier run yet).** `BlogImageService.StoreAsync`'s bare `await storage.SaveAsync(...)` extracted into `WriteBytesAsync`, which catches `UnauthorizedAccessException` and `IOException`, **logs at Error with provider + storage-relative path + userId + the exception**, and rethrows a curated `InvalidOperationException`. **NO UI FILES NEEDED CHANGING** for the message to reach the admin: `ImagePicker` and `ManageImages` already render an `InvalidOperationException`'s message and fall back to the generic sentence for everything else, so the distinction arrives for free. **Two distinct messages by design** — a permissions refusal says 'The server cannot write to its upload location... Retrying will not help — the uploads directory needs to be made writable'; a transient IO failure invites a retry. An operator told 'please try again' would retry forever against a directory that will never become writable. **NEGATIVE CONTROL, end-to-end through the real admin UI** as the documented Admin `Ravi@techieblog.com` against a live host (published to a private folder, `Uploads__Path` pointed at a scratch dir, ACL flipped with `icacls /deny *S-1-1-0` since WSL `chmod` is a no-op on DrvFs): upload REFUSED → screen shows 'Upload failed / The server cannot write to its upload location...' (`tests/.artifacts/cluster-c/nfr040-upload-denied.png`) with **NO exception text, NO absolute path**, and the old generic sentence gone; the host log gained `[07:10 ERR] BlogEngine.Services.BlogImageService <correlationid> Upload REFUSED: Local storage cannot write uploads/profiles/profiles-1-...png for user 1...` followed by the full `System.UnauthorizedAccessException` naming the absolute server path. Permissions restored → a normal upload succeeds and the file lands on disk (`nfr040-upload-restored.png`). Both halves of the REQ-NFR-033 split (curated to the user, full detail to the log) proven in the same run. **Note for the verifier: the default upload category is `profiles`, not `blog` — the first negative control silently PASSED the upload because the wrong subdirectory had been denied**, which is itself a reminder that a happy-path smoke proves nothing here. Transactional cleanliness unchanged and unit-tested (no metadata row on a refused write). ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-11] PASS** — BlogImageUploadFailureTests pass; on the live admin surface the upload control renders, `upload-confirm` stays disabled until a file validates, and no `UnauthorizedAccessException` or 'Access to the path' text reaches the screen. No real upload was attempted during verification — the denied-directory negative control was already run end-to-end during the build pass, which is where the evidence for the log line and the distinguishing admin message lives. | — |
| REQ-NFR-041 | The coding-standards enforcement greps cannot match anything, so their zeros are not evidence | Verified | 100% | **Added and fixed 2026-08-14 by \*build-phase (found while running the §6b standards checks).** **The five patterns in `docs/TechieBlog-Coding-Standards.md` §Enforcement were structurally unmatchable, and every "all greps 0" claim since 2026-08-07 rested on them.** Root cause, proved not inferred: the 2026-08-07 correction replaced `\\w+` with the class `[\\w.<>,\\[\\]?]+`, but under POSIX bracket rules a backslash inside `[...]` is a literal backslash rather than an escape — so the `\\]` does not escape anything and **closes the class early**. The pattern parses as the class `[\\w.<>,\\[]+` followed by the literal characters `?` and `]`, and therefore only matches a line containing the text `?]`, which no C# field declaration contains. **Positive control: a two-line file holding exactly `private readonly IRepo repo;` and `private readonly ILogger<Foo> _logger;` scores 0 of 2 against the old pattern** — it cannot even see the violation it was written for. This is the SECOND time these greps have been blind (the first was the 2026-08-07 `\\w+`-vs-generics fix) and both times the zero read as a pass. FIX: patterns rewritten with POSIX classes only, using `private[^;=(]*[[:space:]]_[A-Za-z]` — `[^;=(]*` spans the whole type expression without enumerating its characters, so generics, arrays, nullables and qualified names are all covered without a bracket class. Each pattern now ships **paired with its positive control** in the doc, and the doc states the rule the incident yields: a zero from an enforcement grep is evidence only when the same pattern has just produced a non-zero on a known control. Also documented: this machine's agent shell aliases `grep` to `ugrep -G`, which changes the regex dialect — evidence-grade runs use `command grep`. **Re-run under the corrected patterns, real source is genuinely clean:** underscore fields 0 (control 259 real fields matched, and 2 of 2 synthetic violations caught, including the generic `ILogger<Foo> _logger` shape that defeated the pre-2026-08-07 pattern); test-method underscores 0 in real tests (control: the synthetic `tests/.artifacts/harness/PositiveControlTests.cs` matches); Hungarian prefixes 0; `a`/`v` prefixes 0; both `ex.Message` sink patterns 0 against 21 real `ex.Message` occurrences in `source/`, which is what shows those two patterns are correctly narrow rather than simply dead. Note `ex.Message` is additionally gated at build time by `tests/unit/Ops/ExceptionDisclosureTests.cs`, so patterns 5-6 were never the sole guard — **patterns 1-4 were**, and they have no build-time equivalent. Recommended follow-up: promote the field/parameter naming checks to a build-time source-scan test like the exception one, so the convention stops depending on anyone running a grep correctly. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14b] `Needs re-verify` — 5 of the 6 patterns are confirmed fixed, but PATTERN 2 IS STILL BLIND, and it fails the REQ's own stated positive control. This is the THIRD occurrence of this defect class.** Every pattern was re-run with `command grep` (the doc's own rule — this shell aliases `grep` to `ugrep -G`). **Confirmed working, each proved against a control before its zero was accepted:** pattern 1 (underscore fields) 0 in `source/`, control **259** real fields exactly as documented, and **5 of 5 synthetic violations caught** — plain, **generic** `ILogger<Foo> _logger`, **array** `string[] _names`, **qualified+nullable** `Foo.Bar? _q`, and **static** — while correctly ignoring the clean `private readonly IRepo clean;`; pattern 3 (Hungarian) 0 real, 1 of 1 synthetic caught; pattern 4 (`a`/`v` prefixes) 0; patterns 5-6 (`ex.Message` sinks) 0 against **21** real `ex.Message` occurrences, which is what shows they are narrow rather than dead. **PATTERN 2 (test-method underscores) DOES NOT WORK.** `[A-Za-z0-9]+_[A-Za-z0-9]+` permits **exactly one** underscore, because `[A-Za-z0-9]` cannot match `_`. So it matches `Login_Fails` but is **blind to `Login_WithBadPassword_Fails`** — the `Method_Scenario_Expected` form that is the dominant xUnit convention and precisely what §"Test naming: short PascalCase, NO underscores" bans. Proved by isolation, not inferred: a 5-line probe of one-underscore and two-underscore names scores **2 of 4 violations**, matching only the single-underscore pair. The doc's own control text — *"a synthetic file holding two deliberately-underscored test methods, must be non-zero"* — was rebuilt at `tests/.artifacts/harness/PositiveControlTests.cs` using the standard two-underscore convention and **scores 0 of 2**. The stated control therefore does not hold, which is the exact failure mode REQ-NFR-041 was raised to eliminate: a zero that reads as a pass. **The compliance conclusion is nevertheless sound, and is stated separately from the tooling defect:** a corrected pattern allowing N underscores — `[A-Za-z0-9]+(_[A-Za-z0-9]+)+` — catches **2 of 2** synthetic violations and still returns **0** over the real `tests/` tree, against a control of **1 218** real public test methods. So the test suite genuinely is clean; what is broken is the guard, not the code. **FIX (one character class):** replace `[A-Za-z0-9]+_[A-Za-z0-9]+` with `[A-Za-z0-9]+(_[A-Za-z0-9]+)+` in §Enforcement, and check the paired control in with the pattern rather than describing a file that lives under a wiped artifacts directory. The standing recommendation to promote these to a build-time source-scan test (as `ExceptionDisclosureTests` already does for patterns 5-6) is now the substantive fix: patterns 1-4 still have no build-time equivalent, and this is the third time a hand-written regex here has been silently unmatchable. ‖ **[REQ-NFR-041] 2026-08-14c — BOTH halves fixed by \*build-phase; status `Needs re-verify` → `Implemented`.** **(a) Pattern 2 rewritten** to `[A-Za-z0-9]+(_[A-Za-z0-9]+)+` plus an optional generic-argument group, so it now matches any number of underscores AND generic test methods (`Foo_Bar<T>(`), which the old pattern also missed because it required the name to run straight into `(`. **(b) The substantive fix shipped: `tests/unit/Ops/SourceConventionTests.cs`** — patterns 1-4 are now gated at BUILD time as .NET `Regex` scans, mirroring `ExceptionDisclosureTests` (which already gated 5-6), so the convention no longer depends on anyone running a grep correctly. Five `[Fact]`s: four tree scans plus a self-test asserting each regex matches a synthetic violation — that self-test is the specific guard against a fourth unmatchable-regex incident. **Evidence, orchestrator-run and independent of the builder's own numbers:** a control file written fresh by the orchestrator scored **4 of 5** — matching `Login_WithBadPassword_Fails`, `Foo_Bar`, `A_B_C_D<TFoo, TBar>` and `Order_Ships` while correctly REJECTING the non-violation `LoginWithBadPasswordFails`; real `tests/` tree **0** hits against a liveness control of **1 228** real test methods. **Mutation-tested end to end:** three real violations (`_leaked` field, `strName` Hungarian, `aInput` parameter) injected into `source/BlogModel/` made 3 of the 4 tree scans FAIL, each naming file, line number and line text; probe removed, suite back to **5/5**. Build rung #4 `0 Error(s)` 7/7; `SourceConventionTests` + `ExceptionDisclosureTests` **7 passed / 0 failed**. One design note: `SourceConventionTests.cs` lives under `tests/` and is therefore scanned by its own pattern-2 gate, so its control strings are assembled from fragments and no single line is itself a violation. ‖ **[VERIFY 2026-08-14c] `Verified` — chained verify-phase, scope REQ-NFR-041.** Gates run: `build` + `acceptance`. Render (§4a) and visual (§4b) are **N/A — this REQ owns no screen**; perf (§4c) is N/A — no `perf-budget:` declared. **The verifier did not accept the builder's evidence and re-derived it:** a positive-control file written by the verifier scored P1 **4/4** (underscore fields incl. generic, nullable-qualified and array-static shapes), P2 **4/5** — matching all four underscore forms while correctly REJECTING the non-violation `LoginWithBadPasswordFails` — P3 **2/2**, P4 both `aInput` and `vCount`; real-tree hits **0** on all four against liveness controls of **259** bare fields and **1 228** test methods. **Mutation test, run by the verifier:** three genuine violations injected into `source/BlogModel/` made 3 of the 4 tree scans FAIL, each naming file, line and text — proving the file walk is live and not merely that the regexes compile; probe removed, suite green. Full solution build rung #4 **0 Error(s) 7/7**; full suite **1 487 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped** (1 482 → 1 490 total, +5 new Facts). The three-occurrence regex-blindness cycle is closed at the level that matters: patterns 1-4 now fail the BUILD, so a future unmatchable regex cannot read as a pass. | — |
| REQ-UI-063 | BlogApp opens on the public blog home instead of the admin dashboard on every warm start | Implemented | 75% | **Added and fixed 2026-08-22 by \*fix-issues from owner UAT (issue 1 of 3). REPRODUCED LIVE before a line was read as authoritative, and the first diagnosis was WRONG and was corrected by the running app.** Owner's report: *"the desktop application shows the login screen only the first time, which is expected because I enabled remember password — but it's a Blog Admin Application, why does it show the Blog screen next time I open it and not the Admin Dashboard?"* **Repro** (BlogApp.exe launched by the harness and driven over its own WebView2 CDP, `C:\Users\srkra\blogapp-smoke\uat-repro.mjs`): a warm start with valid tokens in local storage lands on `pathname: "/"` rendering the PUBLIC surface — `public-header`, `primary-nav`, `resume-hero`, `h1: "S Ravi Kumar"` — while `localStorage` holds live `AccessToken-*`/`RefreshToken-*` pairs. Screenshot `test-results-blogapp/uat-1-landing.png`. **ROOT CAUSE — corrected mid-fix, and this is the part worth keeping.** The first diagnosis blamed `index.html`'s `<base href="/">`, i.e. "the WebView boots at `/`, which is the public home page". A `DesktopLanding` component was built on that theory, and it did not work: instrumenting it showed the app was **already on `/login` at its first render** (`blogapp-20260822.log`: *"DesktopLanding: first render, uri https://0.0.0.1/login"*). The base href was never the cause. The real chain is `MainPage.xaml.cs:38`, which set `blazorWebView.StartPath = "/login"` for a configured install → `LoginPage.OnInitializedAsync` (`LoginPage.razor.cs:79`) sees a remembered session and takes its already-signed-in branch → `ResolveAlreadySignedInDestination()` has no `returnUrl` to honour and returns `RoleLandingRoutes.PublicHome`. That is **correct for the website**, where `/login` is a page a reader wandered onto; it is wrong for a desktop admin tool, where `/login` was the front door. A theory that survives a code read and dies on the running app is exactly why the smoke policy exists. **FIX (entirely inside `source/BlogApp` — `BlogUI`, `BlogEngine` and `TechieBlog` are untouched, so the website's `/login` behaviour is unchanged):** new page `Components/Pages/DesktopStart.razor` at `/blogapp/start`, which `MainPage` now uses as the WebView start path. It owns one decision — signed in → `RoleLandingRoutes.ResolveFor(role)` (`/admin` for Admin/Editor, `/BlogsList` for an Author), anonymous → `/login` with NO returnUrl, because `LoginPage.ResolveDestination` already picks the role landing after a successful sign-in and can do it better once the role is known. Deciding it in a BlogApp page rather than a shared one means there is **no second navigation racing this one**, which the discarded `DesktopLanding` approach would have had. `ConnectionSetup`'s cancel target moved to the same route for the same reason: cancelling out of settings while signed in used to land on the public blog. **SMOKE — PASS, capped at `Implemented` (builder evidence, not a verifier run):** `uat-fix-smoke.mjs` phase A, harness-launched BlogApp bound by PID over CDP → `pathname=/admin`, `h1=["Dashboard"]`, admin shell present, public header **absent**, 2 access tokens still in local storage (so this is genuinely the remembered-session path, not a fresh sign-in). Screenshot `test-results-blogapp/fix-a-landing.png`. Full solution build rung #4 **0 Error(s)** 7/7. **Not covered by unit tests, stated plainly:** `source/BlogApp` targets `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` and is deliberately not referenced by `tests/TechieBlog.Tests` (net10.0), so this head's evidence is the live run. `RoleLandingRoutes` itself, which the fix delegates to, is already unit-tested by `tests/unit/Security/RoleLandingRoutesTests.cs`. | — |
| REQ-FN-062 | BlogApp writes uploaded images to the desktop machine, never to the server's uploads folder | Implemented | 75% | **ROUND 1 SHIPPED AND FAILED IN UAT; round 2 rebuilt on SSH/SFTP — read the 2026-08-22b entry at the end of this cell first.** ‖ **Added and fixed 2026-08-22 by \*fix-issues from owner UAT (issue 2 of 3). REPRODUCED with the owner's own failed upload still on disk.** Owner's report: *"the whole purpose of the desktop application was that I don't have to go to the server… but what about images? They are stored in a separate folder on the server, and the application should have a connection to that location. I tried to upload a company logo in Experience — it was not updated there, and it was not shown on the page either."* **Repro, physical evidence rather than inference:** the owner's logo from that morning was found at `C:\Users\srkra\AppData\Local\TechieBlog\BlogApp\wwwroot\uploads\logos\logos-1-20260822073822-2a37a986.jpeg` — on the desktop machine. The database row it created points at `/uploads/logos/…`, which resolves to nothing on the web server, so the public page renders a broken image. `/admin/images` inside BlogApp listed **zero** `<img>` elements and `fetch('/uploads/')` from the WebView threw, so the file was invisible in the desktop head too. **ROOT CAUSE, documented in the code that caused it:** `BlogSvcInitializer` registers `BlogEngine.Storage.FileStorageFactory`, which needs an `IWebHostEnvironment`; MAUI has no web host, so `MauiProgram.cs:275` supplies `DesktopHostEnvironment(ResolveAppDataRoot())` whose `WebRootPath` is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TechieBlog\BlogApp\wwwroot`. Site settings hold `Storage.ProviderName = Local` with `Storage.LocalRootPath` **empty**, so `BuildLocal` → `ResolveDefaultRoot()` → `UploadsLocation.Resolve(configuration, environment.WebRootPath, …)` returns that desktop folder. `DesktopHostEnvironment`'s own XML remarks stated the consequence outright, so this is a known-but-unbuilt gap — BRD-96 gave the desktop head a database connection and no media connection — not a defect introduced later. **Changing the shared `Storage.*` site settings is NOT an available fix:** the website reads those same rows out of the same database, so pointing `LocalRootPath` at a Windows path would move the SERVER's uploads to a path that does not exist there. **FIX (BlogApp-only; `BlogEngine`, `BlogUI` and `TechieBlog` untouched, which is what makes the website's storage behaviour provably unchanged):** (1) `ConnectionSettings` gains `MediaRootPath` + optional `MediaPublicBaseUrl`, stored in the same DPAPI-protected `ConnectionStore` blob as the database credentials — a JSON round-trip, so an existing blob simply reads them as empty. (2) The path is interpreted with the engine's OWN rule via `ResolveMediaStorageRoot()`: a trailing `uploads` segment means the folder itself is the served directory and its parent is the storage root, reusing `UploadsLocation.FolderName` so the two readings cannot drift. (3) New `MediaLocationProbe` proves the folder before the setting is stored — it creates the directory, writes a file, **reads it back and compares**, then deletes it, because a share that accepts a create and discards the bytes is a real failure mode a write-only check would score as a pass. (4) New `DesktopFileStorageFactory` decorates the engine factory and redirects only the *filesystem* providers, only when a media folder is configured; cloud storage passes straight through, and an unconfigured head gets today's behaviour unchanged. (5) The setup screen gains a Media storage section and its own result heading, so a media failure is never announced as "Connection failed". **(6) A pre-existing prefill gap fixed as a prerequisite:** `OnInitialized` copied the database password but NOT `JwtSigningKey`/`AppEncryptionKey`, so any visit to the settings screen — including one that only wanted to set the media folder — meant re-entering both site keys by hand, and `SaveAndContinue` refuses a blob without them. **SMOKE — PASS end to end, capped at `Implemented`:** `uat-fix-smoke.mjs` phases C/D against a stand-in server folder `C:\Temp\techieblog-media\uploads`. Media folder + probe render on the settings screen; the probe returned *"Media folder OK - uploads will be written to C:\Temp\techieblog-media\uploads"* and the folder was created; Save was **enabled without anyone typing a secret** (it is gated on `IsComplete()`, which requires both keys at their minimum lengths — rendered lengths 64 and 48), proving the prefill fix; then a real logo was uploaded through the Experience screen's `ImagePicker` and **landed at `C:\Temp\techieblog-media\uploads\logos\logos-1-20260822084211-7a6d6b03.png`, 70 bytes matching the fixture byte-for-byte, with ZERO new files under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`**, and the stored path came back as `/uploads/logos/…` — the same site-relative form the website writes. 14/14 assertions PASS. **Left as found:** the media setting was returned to blank and both smoke `blogimage` rows were deleted through the admin UI (the owner's own `ishir_logo.jpeg` row was identified and left alone); the scratch folder was removed. **Not covered by unit tests, stated plainly:** `source/BlogApp` targets `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` and is not referenced by the net10.0 test project, so this head's evidence is the live run; the engine-side path rule it mirrors is already covered by `tests/unit/Media/UploadsLocationTests.cs`. **⚠ OWNER ACTION:** set the Uploads folder on *Change connection → Media storage* to a path that reaches the server's uploads directory, and **re-upload `ishir_logo.jpeg`** — that file is still only on the desktop, and its existing row will stay broken until it is uploaded again. | — | ‖ **[REQ-FN-062] 2026-08-22b — ROUND 1 WAS THE WRONG FIX AND IT FAILED IN THE OWNER'S HANDS. Rebuilt on SSH/SFTP.** The owner followed the instructions, got a green "Media folder OK", uploaded five logos, and **none of them reached the site**. **What went wrong, precisely:** round 1 offered a folder box and assumed the server's uploads directory could be mounted from Windows. It cannot. The site is a Linux VPS and, measured from this machine, answers on **443 and 22 only — 5432 and every SMB path are filtered** — so no Windows path reaches `/srv/data/techieblog/uploads`. Asked for "the folder your site serves /uploads from", the owner reasonably typed the SERVER's path with a drive letter in front, `C:\srv\data\techieblog\uploads`. Windows created it, the probe found it writable, and five uploads landed on the laptop. **Two distinct defects, both mine:** (1) the transport was never verified to be achievable in this deployment — the design assumed a mount that does not and cannot exist, and a session note recording that the VPS is reachable over HTTPS rather than as a filesystem was on file and not applied; (2) **the probe converted a mistake into confidence.** It asked "can I write here?", which is true of any folder on one's own C: drive, and reported success. Writability is not reachability. That is the same silent-false-pass class as REQ-NFR-039 and REQ-NFR-041, and it is the third time this project has been bitten by a green check that could not fail. **ROUND 2 — the transport that actually exists between these two machines.** Port 22 is open and is the same access the owner already uses to reach the site's database (`localhost:5433` is the SERVER database, exposed locally — owner-corrected 2026-08-22, so the database half was never at fault). New in `source/BlogApp`: `MediaTransports` makes the destination an **explicit choice** (`None` / `Sftp` / `Folder`) instead of something inferred from a path, so "which machine do these bytes end up on" is a question the screen asks out loud; `SftpFileStorage : IFileStorage` writes over SSH.NET straight into the server's uploads directory, reusing `FileSystemStorage.NormalizeRelativePath` so the traversal contract is the engine's and not a second opinion; `DesktopFileStorageFactory` selects it; `UploadsUrlRewriter` + a host-page MutationObserver resolve stored `/uploads/…` paths against the site address **at display time only**, which is what fixes "the Experience page was not showing the images" without changing one byte of stored data. **The probe was rebuilt around the right question:** it now dispatches per transport, proves a real round trip (write → **read back** → compare → delete) against the actual destination, names that destination in the success message, and **refuses a local fixed drive outright, before creating anything** — so a mistyped server path now leaves no directory behind at all. **SMOKE — 11/11 PASS against a REAL SSH server**, a disposable container exposing the same `/srv/data/techieblog/uploads` path the VPS uses, with verification read back through `docker exec` so the assertion is about the server's filesystem rather than about anything the script wrote (`C:\Users\srkra\blogapp-smoke\uat-sftp-smoke.mjs`). A logo uploaded through the Experience screen's `ImagePicker` **arrived at `/srv/data/techieblog/uploads/logos/…`, 70 bytes matching the fixture, with ZERO new files on this machine**; the probe cleaned up its own test file; the rendered `src` came back as `https://techierathore.com/uploads/logos/…`, so a server-hosted image can display in the desktop app. **NEGATIVE CONTROL, which is what makes this pass mean anything:** the probe was re-run against **the owner's exact path `C:\srv\data\techieblog\uploads`** and against a fresh local path — both **REFUSED**, with a message naming the mistake, and the fresh path **was not created**. Build rung #4 **0 Error(s) 7/7**; suite **1 496** (1 493 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip). Test server and volume removed afterwards; `WinPostgre` untouched; the smoke's `blogimage` row deleted through the admin UI and the owner's six logo rows left alone. **⚠ OWNER — SIX EXISTING ROWS ARE STILL STRANDED, and no re-upload is needed to fix them.** Five files sit in `C:\srv\data\techieblog\uploads\logos\` and one in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\TechieBlog\BlogApp\wwwroot\uploads\logos\`; their names already match what the database rows point at, so copying them to `/srv/data/techieblog/uploads/logos/` on the server makes all six work as they are. ‖ **[REQ-FN-062] 2026-08-22c — the two frictions the owner hit next, both removed.** **(1) The `scp` recovery command was bad advice and failed on first use.** It carried a literal placeholder, `you@techierathore.com`, which was run verbatim; there is no account `you`, so sshd fell through to a password prompt for a user that does not exist. The owner's reasonable follow-up — *"why is a password needed when I am already connected to the server?"* — has two answers worth recording: the literal placeholder was the immediate cause, and separately, being connected to the site DATABASE on `localhost:5433` is a forwarded port, not an SSH session that `scp` could reuse. **The real defect was reaching for a shell command at all:** the app already holds credentials the operator has just proved with `Test`, and already knows the server's uploads path. Asking them to restate both in a terminal — correctly, with Windows path quoting — was work the app could do. New `MediaMigrator` + a **Send to server** button on the connection screen walks a local uploads folder and pushes every file over the SAME SSH connection, preserving the `uploads/{category}/{file}` layout. It writes NOTHING to the database, because nothing there is wrong: the pre-SFTP rows already point at `/uploads/…`, so landing the files repairs them in place. Overwrites by path, so a second run is a no-op rather than a duplication. **(2) The SSH private-key path was a bare text box.** A hand-typed path is a path nobody checked — the same shape of failure as the `C:\srv\…` incident — so it now has a **Browse** button, as does the migration folder (`FilePickerService`; Windows uses the WinUI folder picker bound to the app window, other platforms fall back to picking a file and taking its directory, since MAUI Essentials 9 ships no folder picker). **SMOKE — 7/7 PASS against a real SSH server** (`uat-migrate-smoke.mjs`): three stranded files under `uploads/logos/` and `uploads/awards/` were sent by the button alone with no shell involved, **all three arrived at the matching server paths with the layout preserved**, the migration folder defaulted to the head's own uploads folder, and **a second run left the file count unchanged** (3 before, 3 after). Verified by reading the server's filesystem through `docker exec`, not by trusting the screen. Build rung #4 **0 Error(s) 7/7**. **Automation limit stated plainly:** the native Browse dialogs are OS modals outside the WebView, so the smoke asserts the buttons render and are wired, not that the dialogs open — that is a manual check. Test server, volume and fixtures removed; `WinPostgre` untouched.
| REQ-UI-064 | Skill category order is fixed alphabetically and a skill's order is invisible on /admin/skills | Implemented | 75% | **Added and fixed 2026-08-22 by \*fix-issues from owner UAT (issue 3 of 3). Triaged from what was OBSERVED, which is narrower than the report in one way and wider in another.** Owner's report: *"On the Skill page there is no way to change the order of skills like there is in the Experience screen."* **What the live run showed (`uat-repro.mjs`, screenshot `test-results-blogapp/uat-3-skills.png`):** per-skill **Move up / Move down chevrons DO exist and work** — 13 `skill-row`s each carrying a visible, correctly-sized `move-skill-up`/`move-skill-down` button (36×36, `visibility: visible`), enabled and disabled correctly at the ends of each category. So "no way to change the order" is not literally true, and that half of the report is recorded as not reproduced. **Three real defects sit behind it, and together they explain it.** **(1) Category order could not be changed at all, and ignored the data that encoded it.** Both surfaces hard-coded alphabetical — `ManageSkills.razor.cs:142` and `ResumeSkills.razor:94` — so the screen rendered `Cloud and DevOps, Data, Frameworks, Languages, Practices` while the `DisplayOrder` values grouped into an authored sequence (Languages 1-3, Frameworks 4-6, Data 7-8, Cloud and DevOps 9-11, Practices 12-13) that neither surface honoured. **No sequence of the existing chevrons can move a skill past its category boundary**, so the owner's intended order was genuinely unreachable — which is the accurate reading of the report. **(2) The order was invisible.** `/admin/experience` has shown an `experience-order` badge on every row since REQ-UI-037 (observed: 1…10); `/admin/skills` showed no number at all, so position was real data with no visible handle and the chevrons read as decoration. **(3) Found while fixing, not reported: `SwapSkillOrder` was a silent no-op on tied values.** It EXCHANGED two `DisplayOrder` values, which is correct only while every value in the category is distinct. Ties are a state this data reaches on its own — a new skill is seeded from the category maximum and deletions leave duplicates — and on a tie the button was enabled, the click was accepted, two rows were written and nothing moved. That is indistinguishable from "there is no way to change the order". **FIX:** category groups are ordered by the LOWEST `DisplayOrder` they contain with the name as tie-break, in `ManageSkills.OrderCategories` **and** in `ResumeSkills`, so admin and the public resume agree and the order is data-driven with no new column and no migration; category Move up / Move down controls renumber whole blocks; a per-skill order badge restores parity with Experience; and both moves now run through ONE `ApplyOrder` renumbering pass that assigns 1..N over the whole arrangement, so the result is always gapless and tie-free and the no-op defect is unrepresentable rather than merely fixed. A `CategoryOf` helper was added because the move helpers compared `skill.Category` directly while the grouping maps null to `Uncategorized` — a null-category skill could not find its own neighbours. **SMOKE — PASS (6/6), capped at `Implemented`:** `uat-fix-smoke.mjs` phase B, live in BlogApp. Categories now render `Languages \| Frameworks \| Data \| Cloud and DevOps \| Practices` (the authored order); 13 order badges for 13 rows; 5 category up + 5 down controls with the first correctly disabled; moving the second category up really reorders it and the renumbering comes back `1,2,3,…,13` — gapless and tie-free; the move is reversible and **the owner's data was restored to the order it was found in**; and the PUBLIC resume renders the identical sequence. Screenshots `test-results-blogapp/fix-b-skills-{before,after}.png`, `fix-b-resume.png`. **UNIT TESTS — 6 added and MUTATION-TESTED, which is what makes the green mean something:** `tests/TechieBlog.Tests/Components/BlogUi/SkillCategoryOrderTests.cs` pins the minimum-order rule against data that is deliberately NOT alphabetical (so an alphabetical implementation cannot pass by coincidence), the case-insensitive name tie-break, the null-category grouping, the empty/null input, the intra-category order, and — the one that matters — **renders the real `ResumeSkills` component with bUnit and compares its headings against the real `ManageSkills.OrderCategories`**, rather than restating the rule a third time, because those two files are the exact pair that drifted. Counterfactual run: reverting ONLY the public resume to alphabetical fails **2 of 6**; reverting ONLY the admin helper fails **3 of 6**; restored, **6/6 pass**. **Scope note stated deliberately:** this changes the public resume's category sequence from alphabetical to authored order. That is the requested behaviour, not a side effect, and it is the only change outside `source/BlogApp` in this pass. | — |

## UAT Bugs

<!-- OWNER-FOUND DEFECTS, kept SEPARATE from the Requirements Status table above.

     These are things the owner hit while actually using the built application, not requirements
     anybody planned. They are logged here at the owner's explicit instruction (2026-08-22): a bug
     found in UAT is evidence that a shipped requirement did not hold, and filing it as a NEW
     requirement row would quietly restate a defect as a feature and inflate the requirement count
     every time something breaks. The owning REQ is named in each row instead, so traceability back
     to the Requirements Status table is kept without polluting it.

     ID scheme: UAT-nnn, allocated in the order the owner reported them. Next free id: UAT-014. -->

| ID | Reported | Defect | Owning REQ | Status | Fix summary |
|----|----------|--------|------------|--------|-------------|
| UAT-001 | 2026-08-22 | Deleted content kept appearing on the public site. The owner emptied `blogpost` / `blogseries` on the production database through the BlogApp desktop client, confirmed zero rows, yet `https://techierathore.com/` still rendered the Featured article and the Latest-articles cards. | REQ-FN-020 (featured post), REQ-NFR-018 (caching) | Fixed (self-smoked) | **Not a data bug — a cache-coherence gap, and the site had already self-corrected by the time it was investigated.** Re-fetching the live home page during triage returned `home-articles-empty` ("No posts yet") with no `home-featured` block and no `/post/` links at all, which proves both the delete and the query were correct. The public pages read through `MemoryCacheService`, whose entries carry `AbsoluteExpirationRelativeToNow = 10 minutes` (`MemoryCacheService.cs:96,152`), under the keys `content:posts:featured` and `content:posts:published:{n}:{0}` (`ServiceCache.cs:66,105`). Every write made *through the web application* calls `ServiceCache.InvalidateContent`, but a delete performed from the **desktop client straight against the same database** never enters the web host's process, so nothing evicted those entries and the site went on serving them until they aged out. The window is bounded at 10 minutes and closes on its own. **Fix:** a `Clear cached content` control on `/settings` → General → **Maintenance** (`Settings.razor`, `data-testid="clear-cache"`) that evicts the content, taxonomy and settings tags on demand, so an out-of-band database change can be reflected immediately instead of after a wait. Smoked live: control renders, clears without error, toast confirms. ⚠ Per-process — this clears the instance that served the click; a scaled-out deployment would need shared invalidation. |
| UAT-002 | 2026-08-22 | `/users` in the Admin module offered no way to **edit** a user and no way to **delete** one. Only "change role" and an activate/deactivate toggle existed, so a mistyped name or email address could only be corrected in the database. | REQ-UI-020 (Users list + add user) | Fixed (self-smoked) | Confirmed exactly as reported at `UsersList.razor:151-185` — the Actions column held two buttons and neither edited nor deleted. **Fix:** (a) the role-only dialog is replaced by a full **Edit User** dialog covering first name, last name, email and role, with in-dialog validation for blank names, malformed email, duplicate email (the database holds a case-insensitive unique index from migration 020) and last-admin demotion; (b) a **Delete** action with a confirmation dialog that states what deletion does. **Delete is a SOFT delete** — `BlogUser` is the target of 16 foreign keys and only 4 cascade, so a hard `DELETE` would be refused for any author who has posted and would silently take ratings and favourites with it for one who has not. Migration `030-UserAdminEditDelete.sql` adds `BlogUser.IsDeleted` and `SoftDeleteBlogUser`; the row survives so posts and comments stay attributed, while the account disappears from every list and every identity lookup. **Guards, all re-checked at click time and not merely rendered disabled:** cannot delete yourself, cannot delete the site owner (enforced in the database too — that row drives the public home page and `/resume`), cannot delete or deactivate the last active administrator. Each refusal is explained in the button's `title` rather than being a silently greyed control. Smoked live: 13/13 checks green, edit and delete both persisted across a reload, screenshots under `tests/.artifacts/harness/uat-users/`. |
| UAT-003 | 2026-08-22 | **Found during UAT-002 triage, not reported by the owner.** The Activate / Deactivate control that *was* already on `/users` had never worked. Clicking it showed "…has been deactivated", then the list reloaded and the badge silently reverted. | REQ-UI-020 (Users list + add user) | Fixed (self-smoked) | Three independent breaks, each of which alone would have made the feature inert. (1) **Never persisted:** `ToggleUserStatus` flipped `AppUser.IsConfirmed` and called `BlogUserRepo.Update`, which executes `UpdateBlogUser(...)` — a 13-parameter function that does not carry `IsConfirmed` at all (`002-CreateStoredFunctions.sql:73-107`), so the write was discarded without an error. (2) **Never enforced:** `AuthSvc.AuthenticateAsync` never consulted `IsConfirmed`, so even a correctly stored "Inactive" account could still sign in — the flag was decorative on both sides of the wire. (3) **Never set on creation:** `InsertBlogUser` does not set it either, so it fell to the column default of `FALSE` — meaning every account an administrator has ever created through `/AddUser` is stored Inactive while remaining able to sign in, the exact inverse of what the screen displays. **Fix:** migration 030 adds `SetBlogUserActive` (a narrow single-column write, deliberately not a widening of `UpdateBlogUser`, so an unrelated profile save cannot clobber activation), makes `InsertBlogUser` create accounts active, and **backfills every existing row to active before** `AuthSvc` starts enforcing the flag — without that backfill the first deployment would lock out every administrator-created account. The refusal sits *after* the password check so it cannot be used to enumerate disabled accounts, and still reports the candidate id to the REQ-FN-051 audit trail. Smoked live: deactivate now survives a reload. |

| UAT-004 | 2026-08-22 | The **desktop app's Statistics page broke with a missing-column error**, immediately after the `UAT-002` build. | REQ-UI-020 (soft delete), REQ-UI-052 (full admin surface in BlogApp) | Fixed (self-smoked) | **A regression introduced by the UAT-002 fix, four hours earlier — and a whole CLASS of defect, not a one-off.** `UAT-002` added `WHERE IsDeleted = FALSE` to the user projections. That column arrives with migration `030`, and **BlogApp deliberately never runs DbUp** — *"the website owns the schema; BlogApp connects to an already-migrated database"* (`MauiProgram.cs:14`). So a desktop build is routinely **newer than the database it points at**: production was journalling 28/28 scripts (confirmed live via `/healthz`) while the new binary demanded a 30th. **Reproduced exactly**, by dropping the column inside a rolled-back transaction: `ERROR: column "isdeleted" does not exist`. The Statistics page was merely the first one hit — **all six admin screens that load the user picker** (Statistics, Skills, Experience, Awards, Images, Users) called the same `GetAllAsync`, as did the public home page and `/resume` via `GetSiteOwner`. **Fix:** the soft-delete predicate moved out of the SQL and into C# (`BlogUserRepo.NotDeleted` / `LiveOrNull`). `SELECT *` returns whatever columns the database actually has and Dapper leaves an unmapped property at its default, so on a pre-030 database `IsDeleted` is false for every row and nothing is filtered — **which is the correct answer, not a fudge: a schema with no soft-delete column has no soft-deleted accounts.** On a migrated database the column maps and the filter behaves exactly as specified. Verified by running all four affected statements against a column-dropped schema (4 / 1 / 1 / 3 rows, no error) and by loading all six admin screens live. ⚠ **The general hazard remains and is now documented:** any future migration that a query hard-depends on will break the desktop head until the website is deployed. Ship order is website-first. |
| UAT-005 | 2026-08-22 | Owner request (enhancement, not a defect): add a **Blog-Archive** link on the home page pointing at the owner's earlier C# Corner writing. | REQ-UI-049 (portfolio landing page) | Fixed (self-smoked) | Added to the Latest-Articles intro line in `HomeLatestArticles.razor` (`data-testid="home-blog-archive-link"`) → `https://www.c-sharpcorner.com/members/s.ravi-kumar/top-articles`. Placed there rather than in the primary nav because it points at a body of work elsewhere, not a section of this site. It **renders whether or not any posts exist**, which matters while the database is empty — the sibling empty-state sits below it. As an off-site link it carries `target="_blank"` with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` (noopener denies the opened tab a `window.opener` handle back into this page; noreferrer also withholds the `Referer`), a visible external-link icon, and an `aria-label` naming the destination — the icon alone is invisible to a screen reader, and per REQ-NFR-007 / WCAG 1.4.1 the link stays underlined so it is not identified by colour alone. Smoked live: 7/7 checks (label, href, target, rel, accessible name, visibility). |

| UAT-006 | 2026-08-22 | Owner request (new feature): a public **Speaker Profile** page listing every speaking engagement — Past Sessions (Date, Event Title hyperlinked to the event page, Session Title, Session Details) and Future Sessions (the same plus a Registration Link) — with an **admin screen to add and edit** the data. | New — `REQ-UI-049` (portfolio surface) is the nearest owner | Fixed (self-smoked) | **Built on the EXISTING `UserEvents` table rather than a new one.** That table is already the generic timeline-entry store, discriminated by a `Type` column that had only ever held `'Experience'`, and it already carried event title, session title, event URL, date, description and display order. The model even documented the intent — `UserEvent.StartDate` reads *"Experience rows only: null on TALK rows"*. A parallel table would have duplicated eight columns, a repository and an interface to gain a narrower type check. Migration `031-SpeakingEngagements.sql` therefore adds exactly **one** column, `RegistrationUrl`, plus a `(UserId, Type, EventDate)` index for the page's query shape. **Past vs Future is DERIVED from the date, never stored** — a stored flag has to be maintained by something, and the day it is not the page advertises a talk that already happened; `UserEvent.IsUpcoming` compares against `DateTime.Today` (not `.Now`) so a session stays "upcoming" for the whole day it runs on, and an undated row counts as past. New: `EventTypes` constants (a mistyped discriminator makes rows invisible rather than failing), public `/speaker-profile`, admin `/admin/speaking` with add/edit/delete, both nav entries. The admin form shows which table a date will file the row under as live feedback, and only offers the Registration field once the date is upcoming. URL fields are validated as absolute `http(s)` — which also refuses `javascript:` — and every outbound link carries `rel="noopener noreferrer"` plus a destination-naming `aria-label`. Smoked live **22/22**, including a full add → appears in Future Sessions with its registration link → delete round trip; 6 new unit tests on the derived split. |
| UAT-007 | 2026-08-22 | Owner instruction: the four profile statistics were **demo data written during build testing**, were mistaken for real figures, and are contradicted by the owner's actual public record. Remove them, and the dummy articles, from the seed migration and from the local database. | REQ-FN-041 / BRD-73 (sample data set) | Fixed (self-smoked) | `019-SampleData.sql` **PARTS C–G removed**: blog series, ten sample posts, PostCategory/PostTag junctions, anonymous comments + verified identities + ratings, and the site-owner resume block (skills, experience, awards, and the four `UserStats` — *"20+ years"*, *"200+ articles"*, *"45 talks"*, *"12 products shipped"*). **PARTS A–B kept, by owner decision when the conflict was put to them:** the three staff accounts, because the UsageGuide test-user table, every smoke script and the verifier all resolve credentials from them — removing those breaks the test harness, not just the sample data — and the category descriptions, which only fill a description that is already empty. The local database was purged of the same content (`BlogPost`, `BlogSeries`, `PostViews`/`PostViewCount`, comments, ratings, `UserStats`, `UserSkills`, `UserAwards`, `UserEvents`); the four users were left intact. ⚠ **BRD-73 is deliberately NARROWED, not silently unmet** — the requirement asked for an evaluation-ready sample set and now gets only the accounts half; recorded in the file's own header and here rather than left as a quiet gap. ⚠ **Existing databases are unaffected:** DbUp journals by file name, so a database that already ran 019 will not re-run it — this edit governs FRESH databases only, which is why no cleanup migration was written (deleting content on a live site because a seed file changed is exactly the surprise a migration must not spring). |

| UAT-008 | 2026-08-22 | **"The site looks very different from the mockups."** | REQ-UI-049 (portfolio home), REQ-UI-005 (public shell) | Fixed (self-smoked) — full sweep clean; two items remain owner decisions | **⚠ THERE ARE TWO FOLDERS NAMED `mockups`, AND EVERY FUTURE READER NEEDS TO KNOW WHICH IS WHICH.** `docs/mockups/` is the **current, authoritative** set: newest file **2026-08-06**, and **39 of 39** files reference TrBlazeUI. `mockups/` at the repo root is a **different, much older** set: newest file **2025-12-16** (249 days), **0 of 35** files mention TrBlazeUI, and `styles.css` declares `--color-primary:#0078D4` with Segoe UI and a 4px radius — the pre-REQ-UI-048 Fluent design. `docs/TechieBlog-UIDesign.md` names `docs/mockups/*.html` as the visual contract. **This distinction caused a real misunderstanding in UAT:** an `@mockups/` reference resolves to the repo-root folder, so a comparison intended against the current set silently ran against the 2025 one. **Both this row and the fixes below are measured against `docs/mockups/` — the current TrBlazeUI set — and the owner has confirmed that is the set to follow strictly.** Nothing here treats the owner's recent mockups as outdated; they are the contract. **Token layer checked against that contract and it matches:** `--primary` `oklch(0.546 0.215 262.881)` ≡ `#2563eb`, `--radius` `0.625rem` — identical to `docs/mockups/trblazeui.css`. **But three genuine fidelity gaps were found against the real contract and fixed:** (1) **the hero headline** — the mockup renders one heading, `<h1>Hi, I'm <span class="accent">Name</span></h1>` at `clamp(30px,5vw,46px)/800` with the name in `--primary`; the build had a small `<p>Hi, I'm</p>` above a plain 36px black `<h1>`, losing both the two-tone treatment and most of the scale. Now one `<h1>` (also the correct document outline — "Hi, I'm" was never a heading). (2) **section headings** were left-aligned against `.section-title{text-align:center}` / `.section-sub{text-align:center}` — About and Latest Articles now centred at the mockup's 24px/700. (3) **social links** rendered as bare ghost buttons against `.social-row a{width:42px;height:42px;border:1px solid var(--border);border-radius:50%;background:var(--card);box-shadow:var(--shadow-sm)}` — now 44×44 bordered circles with the hover lift, `prefers-reduced-motion` respected. ⚠ **A trap worth recording: this project consumes TrBlazeUI's PREBUILT stylesheet and runs no Tailwind JIT pass.** The first attempt at (1) used `text-[clamp(30px,5vw,46px)]`, which is never generated — it rendered as an unknown class contributing nothing and the headline silently collapsed to the inherited size. Caught by measuring `getComputedStyle` on the running page, not by reading the markup. **Bespoke values must be real CSS rules** (`.resume-name`, `.resume-social a` in `resume.css`); only utilities already present in the shipped sheet may be used inline. Verified live: headline `46px/800`, social `44×44` `border-radius:50%`, `/resume` (which shares the hero) unaffected, no overflow. ‖ **[FULL SWEEP 2026-08-22] — all 38 contract mockups audited against the running app, and the sweep is now CLEAN.** Method: render each mockup and its live route, then diff *structure* (h1/h2 inventory) and *computed* typography/alignment — not pixels, because the database is deliberately empty after UAT-007 and every content region differs by design. Harness `tests/.artifacts/harness/uat-users/sweep.mjs`, findings `…/sweep/findings.json`. **27 web screens compared; 11 mockups skipped and named** (3 `blogapp-*` describe the MAUI desktop head, 8 need a slug/id/token the empty database cannot supply). **First pass: 14 screens with gaps. Three were real and are fixed:** (a) **public page titles rendered at 48px against the mockups' `clamp(24px,4vw,32px)`/800** — every public title in `docs/mockups/` carries that identical inline style, while TrBlazeUI's `TypographyH1` bakes in `text-4xl`/`lg:text-5xl`; fixed by `h1.page-title` in `layout.css` (element+class specificity `0,1,1` deliberately outranks the library's bare utility `0,1,0`, so it wins regardless of stylesheet order) applied to About, Search, Series, Category, Tag and RSS; (b) `/about`'s title was left-aligned inside a header the mockup centres (`page-title-centered`); (c) three headings said something different from the design — `Page not found`→`This page wandered off`, `My Profile`→`Manage Profile`, `Comments Management`→`Comment moderation`, `All Series`→`Article Series`. **Second pass: 11 gaps remain, and EVERY ONE was proved to be an empty-database artifact or a documented decision, not a defect.** The five "mockup has a table, page has 0" rows (`/BlogsList`, `/admin/series`, `/CommentsList`, `/admin/analytics`, `/admin/awards`) were verified to render a proper **empty state with no error** — and the control is that `/users` and `/CategoriesList`, the two admin lists that still HAVE rows, both pass. The remaining "missing heading" rows are mockup *content* the deleted seed data used to supply (series names, article titles, the newsletter body's "Hey there 👋", the post editor's already-existing post), plus two matcher artifacts: the mockup persona is "Ravi Rathore" and the site owner is "S Ravi Kumar", and the home page's contact block is headed "Contact" because REQ-UI-049 explicitly REUSES `ResumeContact` — and `10-resume.html` itself heads that section "Contact". **Net: zero outstanding style or structure defects against the contract.** |

He objected that his mockups were recent and TrBlazeUI-based. | — (record correction) | Fixed | **Both statements were about different folders, and the owner's was right about his.** Measured: `docs/mockups/` — newest file **2026-08-06** (16 days), **39 of 39** files reference TrBlazeUI; repo-root `mockups/` — newest file **2025-12-16** (249 days), **0 of 35** files mention TrBlazeUI, `--color-primary:#0078D4`. The owner's description ("prepared 10-20 days back", "created keeping TrBlazeUI in mind") matches `docs/mockups/` exactly. The mistake was taking the `@mockups/` path — which resolves from the repo root — as authoritative for what the owner meant, and then phrasing the finding in a way that read as dismissing his recent design work. Every comparison actually performed had already been made against `docs/mockups/`. Wording corrected in the checklist and both PROJECT-STATUS files; the false implication is gone. The provenance of the 2025 set was **not** rewritten — recording it as TrBlazeUI-based would have put something untrue in the project record and pointed a future agent at a Fluent design. |

| UAT-013 | 2026-08-22 | Owner request: put the photograph of him speaking (black t-shirt, from the old seed articles) on the Speaker Profile page as a **banner**. | — (UAT-006 follow-up) | Fixed (self-smoked) | The photograph was already in the repository as `source/BlogUI/wwwroot/images/SpeakingBg.jpg` — **an orphan referenced by nothing**, left over from the pre-TrBlazeUI design — so this revives a dead asset rather than adding one. **It is NOT served as-is:** the original is 5973×2029 / **1.51 MB**, roughly eleven times the bytes for pixels no viewport can display, and as the topmost element it would have been the page's LCP. Downscaled to 1920×652 / **141 KB** (−91%) via headless Chromium's canvas — the host has no ImageMagick, no PIL and no pip, and Playwright was already installed. Rendered as a masthead with the title overlaid on a bottom-up gradient scrim; the overlaid text is white in BOTH themes on purpose, because it sits on a photograph the theme cannot recolour, and `--muted-foreground` fails contrast there in light mode. `width`/`height` are declared so the box is reserved before the image lands and the heading below cannot jump. ⚠ **A real bug was introduced and caught during this change:** `aspect-ratio` combined with `min-height` let the HEIGHT become authoritative, so the browser derived width from it — 160px × 2.94 = **471px**, overflowing a 390px viewport by 121px. Fixed by pinning `width:100%` (making width authoritative so the ratio can only compute height) and dropping the `min-height`; narrow viewports now get a taller `16/10` crop so the subject and the overlaid text both have room. Found by measuring `scrollWidth − clientWidth` on the running page, not by reading the CSS. Smoked **28/28**, including six new banner assertions (loads, serves the downscaled copy, real alt, reserves its box, white overlay, zero overflow at 390). |

**Status values:** `Open` · `Fixed (self-smoked)` (code done + smoked by the builder — the builder's
ceiling) · `Verified` (an executed verify-phase run graded it) · `Won't fix` · `Not reproducible`.

**Note on UAT-001:** the underlying behaviour — a 10-minute cache that an out-of-band database write
cannot invalidate — is *inherent* to caching in a separate process, not a coding error. The control
added closes the gap the owner actually hit; it does not make the cache coherent with writers outside
the web host, and no in-process cache can be.

**⚠ Note on UAT-004 — the standing rule it leaves behind.** BlogApp and the website are two heads on
one database, and **only the website migrates it**. A query that hard-depends on a column added by
migration *N* therefore breaks the desktop head for the whole window between shipping the code and
deploying the site. Two consequences, both permanent:

1. **Deploy the website first**, then distribute a matching BlogApp build. Never the reverse.
2. **Prefer schema-tolerant reads in shared code.** `SELECT *` plus a C# predicate degrades safely on
   an older schema; a `WHERE new_column = …` cannot. This is the reason `BlogUserRepo` filters
   `IsDeleted` in C#, and that decision is load-bearing — the comment on `SelectAllSql` says so.

**⚠ Note on UAT-008 — the two open items are DECISIONS, not defects, and are deliberately not
"fixed".** Both are the loudest remaining differences from `docs/mockups/`, and both were left for
the owner because guessing either would be an unrequested change to a live, public-facing site:

1. **Dark vs light default.** Every mockup renders light; the site ships dark
   (`Theme.IsDarkModeDefault = True`, set deliberately by migration `025-DefaultToDarkMode.sql` for
   REQ-UI-033 / BRD-66). This is the single biggest reason the site "looks different" at a glance —
   and it is a stored **setting**, changeable from Settings → Theme without a deployment. Flipping it
   silently would undo a documented requirement.
2. **Brand and footer wording.** The mockups use the placeholder persona "● Ravi Rathore" as the brand
   and *"© 2026 Ravi Rathore · Built with TechieBlog"* in the footer; the site uses the configured
   site title, "TechieBlog". That is owner-controlled content in Settings, not markup.

Also unchanged by design: the **contact block** differs from the mockup's centred "Get In Touch"
email card because the owner decided on 2026-08-10 to publish LinkedIn instead of an email address
(the reason is recorded at `ResumeHero.razor`'s CTA), and the **stats band** is absent only because
UAT-007 removed the demo `UserStats` — `HomeStats` already collapses correctly when empty, so it will
reappear the moment real statistics are entered.

The same window applies to `AuthSvc`'s new `IsConfirmed` sign-in gate: migration 030 backfills every
row to active, so on a **migrated** database the gate is safe, but a NEW BlogApp build against a
**pre-030** database would refuse any account created through `/AddUser` (those rows default to
`FALSE`, because `InsertBlogUser` never set the column — that is UAT-003's third break). Website-first
deployment closes this too, since the code and the backfill ship together.
