### Page: Newsletter & verification — Phase 9 (added 2026-08-06)

<a id="d-req-ui-053"></a>
- **REQ-UI-053** — **OPEN** — Public newsletter archive at `/newsletters` (BRD-100): page header, prominent subscribe card (email + captcha + double-opt-in note), list of past issues newest-first with issue number, title, date and excerpt, and paging. *Mockup:* `docs/mockups/42-newsletter-archive.html`.
  - *Acceptance:* only *sent* issues appear; each links to its issue page; subscribing creates a **pending** subscriber until confirmed; no issues → TbEmpty.

<a id="d-req-ui-054"></a>
- **REQ-UI-054** — **OPEN** — Public newsletter issue view at `/newsletter/{slug}` (BRD-101): issue number, title, sent date, rendered body, previous/next issue navigation and a compact subscribe CTA. *Mockup:* `docs/mockups/43-newsletter-view.html`.
  - *Acceptance:* an unknown or unsent slug returns 404; prev/next resolve by send order and hide at the ends.

<a id="d-req-ui-055"></a>
- **REQ-UI-055** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07)** — Email confirmation landing page at `/verify/{token}` (BRD-98) with four states: confirmed, expired/invalid (offering a fresh link), already verified, and subscription-confirmed. *Mockup:* `docs/mockups/44-verify-email.html`.
  - *Acceptance:* a valid token confirms exactly once and the page states what was confirmed (comment, rating or subscription); a reused or expired token never confirms and explains why.
  - *Files:* `source/BlogUI/Pages/BlogPages/VerifyEmail.razor`, `source/BlogUI/Pages/BlogPages/VerifyEmail.razor.cs`, plus the subscription-loop fix in `source/BlogEngine/Services/EmailVerificationSvc.cs` (+ doc correction in `Services/IEmailVerificationService.cs`) and its tests `tests/unit/Engagement/FakeSubscriberRepo.cs`, `EmailVerificationSvcTests.cs`, `CommentSvcTests.cs`, `RatingSvcTests.cs` (the last two only to pass the new constructor argument).
  - *Design notes for the verifier:*
    - The token row is read via `IEmailVerificationTokenRepo.GetByTokenAsync` **before** any consume attempt. `EmailVerificationSvc.ConsumeAsync` collapses "used", "expired" and "unknown" into one message, which cannot satisfy the four-state acceptance on its own.
    - A row with `IsUsed = true` is never passed to `ConsumeAsync`, which is what guarantees a replayed link confirms nothing.
    - Redemption runs from `OnAfterRenderAsync(firstRender)`. `OnInitializedAsync` would run twice under the app's global `InteractiveServer` render mode (prerender + circuit) and the token would already be spent by the time the page went live.
    - All three purposes are now promoted inside `EmailVerificationSvc.PromoteTargetAsync`. The page holds NO promotion logic — it only picks the wording. The service's own XML docs previously claimed "the subscriber row is promoted by the newsletter service"; no such promotion existed, and that comment has been corrected rather than left to mislead the next reader.
    - The resend button is offered only for a row that expired **unused**; it re-issues from the stored email / purpose / target so the visitor retypes nothing.
  - *Verifier hooks:* `verify-page`, `verify-card`, `verify-loading`, `verify-confirmed`, `verify-subscribed`, `verify-already`, `verify-expired`, `verify-purpose`, `verify-success-alert`, `verify-already-alert`, `verify-expired-alert`, `verify-resend`, `verify-resend-result`, `verify-back-to-article`, `verify-browse`, `verify-actions`, `verify-footnote`.

<a id="d-req-ui-056"></a>
- **REQ-UI-056** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07)** — Captcha widget on every public write surface (BRD-99): challenge image, reload button and answer input, on the comment form, the rating step and the subscribe form. The image is stroke geometry, not text — see REQ-FN-049 for the 2026-08-07 fix that removed the readable answer from the payload. *Mockups:* `02-post-view.html`, `42-newsletter-archive.html`.
  - *Acceptance:* a wrong answer blocks submission with an inline error and issues a fresh challenge; the answer is never present in the page source; the control is keyboard reachable and labelled for screen readers.
  - *Files:* `source/BlogUI/Components/CaptchaWidget.razor` (new). No backend file was touched — the widget consumes the pre-existing, unit-tested `ICaptchaService` / `CaptchaSvc`.
  - *Public contract* (so other clusters can mount it): `<CaptchaWidget @ref="captcha" data-testid="captcha-widget" />` with `Task<bool> ValidateAsync()`, `void Reset()`, `string ChallengeToken`, plus `string Answer`, `bool HasAnswer` and `void ShowError(string)`. Parameters: `LabelText`, `HintText`, `Class`, and `CaptureUnmatchedValues`.
  - *Design notes for the verifier:*
    - **A challenge is SINGLE USE.** `CommentSvc` and `RatingSvc` validate the captcha themselves, so those two callers pass `ChallengeToken` + `Answer` straight through and must NEVER call `ValidateAsync()` — that would burn the challenge before the service could see it. `ValidateAsync()` exists for a surface whose service does not validate (the newsletter subscribe form: `NewsletterSvc`/`SubscriberSvc` contain no captcha step).
    - The challenge is issued from `OnAfterRenderAsync(firstRender)`, never `OnInitialized`. Under the app's global `InteractiveServer` render mode the init pass also runs during the static prerender, which would put a live challenge into the served HTML and burn a cache entry for a visitor who never connects. This is what makes the served page source challenge-free.
    - The SVG is rendered as a base64 `data:image/svg+xml` URI on an `<img>`, not inlined. `CaptchaSvgRenderer` emits the code as SVG `<text>` nodes, so inlining it would put the five characters into the DOM as readable text; encoding keeps them out of the document, out of `textContent` and out of a `grep` of the page.
    - Because a rejecting service has already consumed the challenge, `ShowError(message)` both surfaces the message and issues a NEW image — re-showing the old one would always fail with "expired".
    - **Known residual risk (backend, not this widget):** the glyphs still exist inside the encoded SVG payload, so a client that deliberately base64-decodes the `src` and parses the SVG can read the answer. That is inherent to `CaptchaSvgRenderer`'s text-based SVG and would need a rasterised image or a server-streamed image endpoint to close. The smoke harness uses exactly that decode to drive the "correct answer" path.
  - *Verifier hooks:* `captcha-widget`, `captcha-image`, `captcha-image-placeholder`, `captcha-reload`, `captcha-answer`, `captcha-hint`, `captcha-error`.

### Page: Engagement components

<a id="d-req-ui-027"></a>
- **REQ-UI-027** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07)** — Star rating widget showing average and count (BRD-40, BRD-42). ~~interactive for signed-in users and read-only otherwise~~ superseded 2026-08-06 by BRD-40/41 rev: **interactive for ANONYMOUS visitors, keyed by email, no sign-in gate.**
  - *Acceptance:* an anonymous visitor can rate; the widget shows the average and the count; a score counts only once the address has confirmed.
  - *Files:* `source/BlogUI/Components/PostRatingPanel.razor` (new), mounted in `source/BlogUI/Pages/BlogPages/PostView.razor`. No backend file was touched — the panel consumes the pre-existing, unit-tested `RatingSvc`. `Components/StarRating.razor` stays the read-only display used by `PostCard`/`SearchResults` and is unchanged.
  - *Design notes for the verifier:*
    - The stars are the visitor's INPUT and open empty; the community average is the number beside them (this is what mockup `02-post-view.html` specifies). Seeding the stars with the average was tried and reverted: TrBlazeUI's `Rating` does not raise `ValueChanged` when the already-selected star is clicked, so a visitor who agreed with the average could not reveal the identify step.
    - `selectedRating` returns to 0 after a successful submit for the same reason.
    - The average and count come from `RatingSvc.GetPostRatingStats`, which counts VERIFIED ratings only — submitting a score does not move the public numbers until the double opt-in link is clicked.
    - The panel never calls `CaptchaWidget.ValidateAsync()`; `RatingSvc` validates the captcha itself and would find the challenge already burned.
    - ⚠ **TR-031:** the star selection is not keyboard operable — TrBlazeUI renders each star as a non-focusable `<span role="radio">` with `aria-checked=""`. The email field, the captcha, the reload button and the submit button all are. Not fixable from application code.
  - *Verifier hooks:* `post-rating-panel`, `post-rating-stars`, `post-rating-average`, `post-rating-count`, `rating-identify-step`, `rating-email`, `rating-email-error`, `rating-submit`, `rating-form-error`, `rating-form-success`, plus the `captcha-*` hooks.

<a id="d-req-ui-028"></a>
- **REQ-UI-028** — Favourite toggle on the post page and post cards with a visual favourited state (BRD-43).

<a id="d-req-ui-029"></a>
- **REQ-UI-029** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07)** — Comment form and comment list on the post page (BRD-36, BRD-37). ~~signed-in only, with own-comment edit/delete~~ superseded 2026-08-06 by BRD-36 rev: **anonymous form — name + email + body, email never published, no sign-in prompt.**
  - *Acceptance:* an anonymous visitor can post a comment; the email address is never rendered; an unconfirmed or unapproved comment never appears on the page.
  - *Files:* `source/BlogUI/Components/CommentThread.razor` (new), `source/BlogUI/Components/CommentForm.razor` (new), both mounted in `source/BlogUI/Pages/BlogPages/PostView.razor`, which previously carried a dead placeholder (a hardcoded "No comments yet" `Empty` plus a `Disabled="true"` textarea and button). No backend file was touched — the components consume the pre-existing, unit-tested `CommentSvc`.
  - *Design notes for the verifier:*
    - Visibility is enforced server-side, not in the UI: `BlogCommentRepo.GetPostParentComments` / `GetPostChildComments` both filter on `moderationstatus = 'Approved'`, so an unconfirmed or unmoderated comment cannot reach `CommentThread` at all. `PostView.LoadComments` is deliberately re-run after a successful submit — the new comment correctly does NOT appear.
    - The form validates its three fields BEFORE calling the service, so a typo does not burn the single-use captcha challenge (the captcha gate runs after field validation inside `CommentSvc`).
    - `RenderedOn` is stamped in `OnAfterRenderAsync(firstRender)`, not during prerender, because `CommentSpamGuard` measures "too fast to be human" from it and a prerender timestamp would be stale. While unset the guard skips the check, so the fallback is safe.
    - The honeypot is a real bound `Input` inside a `display:none` wrapper with `aria-hidden` and `tabindex="-1"`; `CommentSpamGuard` blocks any submission that arrives with it filled.
    - The form never calls `CaptchaWidget.ValidateAsync()`; `CommentSvc` validates the captcha itself. Captcha-shaped failures are routed to the widget's inline error, everything else to a card-level alert; both paths re-issue a challenge because the service consumed it.
  - *Verifier hooks:* `comments-section`, `comments-count`, `comments-list`, `comments-empty`, `comment-item`, `comment-reply`, `comment-author`, `comment-date`, `comment-body`, `comment-form`, `comment-name`, `comment-email`, `comment-input`, `comment-honeypot`, `comment-submit`, `comment-verify-note`, `comment-form-error`, `comment-form-success`, `comment-name-error`, `comment-email-error`, `comment-body-error`, plus the `captcha-*` hooks.

## Functional requirements

<a id="d-req-fn-001"></a>
- **REQ-FN-001** — All five projects target `net10.0` and the solution restores and builds (BRD-85).

<a id="d-req-fn-002"></a>
- **REQ-FN-002** — The `BlogSvc` REST project is removed; `BlogUI` resolves `BlogEngine` services from DI with no HTTP hop (BRD-85).

<a id="d-req-fn-003"></a>
- **REQ-FN-003** — PostgreSQL schema created by numbered DbUp scripts, executed automatically at host startup (BRD-88).

<a id="d-req-fn-004"></a>
- **REQ-FN-004** — Fluent UI Blazor replaces Blazorise as the only component library (BRD-65).

<a id="d-req-fn-005"></a>
- **REQ-FN-005** — `AuthSvc.AppLogin` validates credentials and issues a JWT with `PrimarySid`, `Name`, `Email`, `Role`, recording the login (BRD-2).

<a id="d-req-fn-006"></a>
- **REQ-FN-006** — Registration creates a Reader-role account after enforcing email uniqueness and password strength (BRD-1, BRD-3).

<a id="d-req-fn-007"></a>
- **REQ-FN-007** — Password reset issues, validates and consumes a time-limited token (BRD-4, BRD-5).

<a id="d-req-fn-008"></a>
- **REQ-FN-008** — An expiring session token is refreshed without forcing re-login (BRD-6).

<a id="d-req-fn-009"></a>
- **REQ-FN-009** — Five roles and five authorization policies are registered and applied to every protected page (BRD-7, BRD-8).

<a id="d-req-fn-010"></a>
- **REQ-FN-010** — Admin user management: list, search, view, change role, enable/disable, delete (BRD-10).

<a id="d-req-fn-011"></a>
- **REQ-FN-011** — Profile read/update and password change with current-password verification (BRD-11, BRD-12).

<a id="d-req-fn-012"></a>
- **REQ-FN-012** — Post create, read, update and delete through `BlogSvc` returning `Result<BlogPost>` (BRD-14).

<a id="d-req-fn-013"></a>
- **REQ-FN-013** — Slugs are generated from the title, unique, overridable, and used for all public routes (BRD-15, BRD-33).

<a id="d-req-fn-014"></a>
- **REQ-FN-014** — Post bodies are rendered from Markdown to HTML via Markdig (BRD-16).

<a id="d-req-fn-015"></a>
- **REQ-FN-015** — Draft posts are excluded from every public query; publish/unpublish transitions the state (BRD-18).

<a id="d-req-fn-016"></a>
- **REQ-FN-016** — Scheduled posts are published automatically at their scheduled time by a hosted background service (BRD-20, BRD-21).

<a id="d-req-fn-017"></a>
- **REQ-FN-017** — Category CRUD and single-primary-category assignment per post (BRD-22, BRD-23).

<a id="d-req-fn-018"></a>
- **REQ-FN-018** — Tag CRUD, many-to-many post-tag junction, autocomplete, inline creation, and accurate per-tag post counts (BRD-24, BRD-26).

<a id="d-req-fn-019"></a>
- **REQ-FN-019** — Series CRUD, part ordering, and previous/next navigation resolution (BRD-27, BRD-28).

<a id="d-req-fn-020"></a>
- **REQ-FN-020** — Published-post listings, featured-post selection, related posts and reading-time calculation (BRD-30, BRD-31, BRD-32).

<a id="d-req-fn-021"></a>
- **REQ-FN-021** — Keyword search across title, abstract, body and tags with paging and result counts (BRD-34, BRD-35).

<a id="d-req-fn-022"></a>
- **REQ-FN-022** — Comment add/edit/delete, approval workflow, pending queue and per-post counts (BRD-36, BRD-37, BRD-38, BRD-39).

<a id="d-req-fn-023"></a>
- **REQ-FN-023** — One rating per user per post, changeable, with average and count aggregates and top-rated queries (BRD-40, BRD-41, BRD-42).

<a id="d-req-fn-024"></a>
- **REQ-FN-024** — Favourite add/remove/toggle, per-user listing and per-post counts (BRD-43, BRD-44).

<a id="d-req-fn-025"></a>

<a id="d-req-fn-026"></a>

  - *Acceptance:* a password-reset email is actually delivered in a configured environment; failures are logged, not swallowed.

<a id="d-req-fn-034"></a>
- **REQ-FN-034** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-09)** — Track total and unique post views per visit, writing to the existing `PostViews` table (BRD-60). Writer: `PostView.razor` → `IPostViewTracker.TrackCurrentVisitAsync` → `PostViewRepo.RecordViewAsync` (conditional insert, 24h de-duplication window, salted `SHA-256(salt|ip|userAgent)` visitor digest, no raw address stored).

<a id="d-req-fn-035"></a>
- **REQ-FN-035** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-09)** — Popular-post ranking and per-post engagement statistics (comments, ratings, views) (BRD-61). Surfaces: `/admin/analytics` (tiles, trend, popular grid, category share), the `/admin` popular-posts card, and the public post byline's total/unique view counts.

<a id="d-req-fn-036"></a>
- **REQ-FN-036** — Aggregate counts for the admin dashboard (posts, users, comments, subscribers) (BRD-62).

<a id="d-req-fn-037"></a>

<a id="d-req-fn-038"></a>

<a id="d-req-fn-039"></a>
- **REQ-FN-039** — `ThemeService` + `ThemeProvider` apply the site theme and light/dark mode from persisted preferences; all values come from CSS custom properties (BRD-65, BRD-66, BRD-67).

<a id="d-req-fn-040"></a>
- **REQ-FN-040** — Site settings persist and take effect without a restart where possible (BRD-69).
  - *Acceptance:* changing posts-per-page or the theme is reflected on the public site after save.

<a id="d-req-fn-041"></a>
- **REQ-FN-041** — **IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-07** — Seed/sample data: sample posts demonstrating Markdown, images and series; one user per role; categories, tags, comments and ratings (BRD-73).
  - *Delivered by:* `source/BlogDb/PostgresScripts/019-SampleData.sql` (idempotent, DbUp-applied) + the Test-users table in `docs/TechieBlog-UsageGuide.md`.
  - *Roles:* Admin (from 003) + Editor / Author / Contributor seeded here. **Reader is intentionally absent** — reader accounts and public registration were retired on 2026-08-06, and comments/ratings are now anonymous and email-keyed, so a Reader credential would open nothing.
  - *Credentials:* every seeded password is a PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 hash (210 000 iterations, fixed per-account seed salt) — no plaintext anywhere in the script (REQ-NFR-023). Plaintext lives only in the UsageGuide registry.

<a id="d-req-fn-042"></a>
- **REQ-FN-042** — **OPEN** — `IFileStorage` abstraction with local, network and cloud implementations behind the image service (BRD-45, BRD-46).

<a id="d-req-fn-043"></a>
- **REQ-FN-043** — **OPEN, BLOCKER** — Restore the solution to a green build and pin all package references (BRD-91).
  - *Failure (2026-08-02):* `NU1605` — `BlogUI` references `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components.Web 10.0.0`; `Microsoft.FluentUI.AspNetCore.Components 4.*` resolves to 4.14.4 requiring ≥ 10.0.9. Reproduced on ladder rung #2 (`~/.dotnet/dotnet`) and rung #4 (`cmd.exe /c dotnet`).
  - *Acceptance:* `dotnet build TechieBlog.slnx` succeeds with 0 errors; no `PackageReference` uses a floating version (`4.*`).

<a id="d-req-fn-044"></a>
- **REQ-FN-044** — The repository is consumable as a GitHub template and the rename script re-brands a clone (BRD-71).

<a id="d-req-fn-045"></a>
- **REQ-FN-045** — Adopter documentation covers getting started, customization, deployment and database migration (BRD-72).

<a id="d-req-fn-046"></a>
- **REQ-FN-046** — **OPEN** — `source/BlogApp` MAUI Blazor Hybrid project (Windows + macOS) referencing BlogUI, BlogEngine and BlogModels; registers the same DI graph as the web host (BRD-94). *Prerequisite:* MAUI workload on the Windows build host; runtime verification opts in via `core-config.yaml` `runtimeVerification` once the head exists.
  - *Acceptance:* the solution builds with the sixth project; BlogApp launches to the connection-setup/login flow on Windows.

<a id="d-req-fn-047"></a>
- **REQ-FN-047** — **OPEN** — BlogApp first-run connection-setup: capture + test the site's PostgreSQL connection string, store it in platform secure storage, and reconfigure from a settings surface; all data access goes directly to the site database — no local DB, no sync (BRD-96).
  - *Acceptance:* an invalid connection string is rejected with a clear error; a valid one persists across app restarts and is not stored in plain text; deleting it returns the app to the setup screen.

<a id="d-req-fn-048"></a>
- **REQ-FN-048** — **OPEN** — Double opt-in email verification (BRD-98): issue a single-use, 24-hour token for an anonymous comment, rating or subscription; email it; consume it on `/verify/{token}`; record the address as verified so later submissions from it skip the step. Tokens are **persisted**, not in-memory (contrast ADR-008 / REQ-NFR-019).
  - *Acceptance:* an unconfirmed comment never appears publicly; a confirmed one enters the moderation queue; a token works exactly once and expires; a verified address posts without re-confirming.
  - *Depends on:* REQ-FN-033 (real SMTP `IEmailService`) — with the console stub, verification cannot complete outside development.

<a id="d-req-fn-049"></a>
- **REQ-FN-049** — **IMPLEMENTED (2026-08-07)** — Self-hosted captcha (BRD-99): generate a random code with `RandomNumberGenerator`, render it as a distorted **SVG** server-side, and validate the answer against a server-held value (a short-lived `IMemoryCache` entry). **.NET base class library only — no third-party package, no external service, no `System.Drawing.Common`.** The challenge is drawn as **anonymous stroke/path vector glyphs** (`BlogEngine/Common/CaptchaGlyphSet.cs` + `CaptchaSvgRenderer.cs`), never as SVG `<text>`, so the expected answer never reaches the client in machine-readable form.
  - *Acceptance:* the expected answer never reaches the client; a challenge is single-use and expires in minutes; reload issues a new one; validation failure blocks the write and re-challenges.
  - *Note:* avoid `System.Drawing.Common` — it is Windows-only and unsupported cross-platform; SVG keeps the renderer portable and dependency-free.

<a id="d-req-fn-050"></a>
- **REQ-FN-050** — **OPEN** — Newsletter publishing and public archive queries (BRD-100, BRD-101): a sent issue becomes a public record with a slug; list published issues newest-first with paging; resolve previous/next by send order. Extends the composer/send pipeline (REQ-FN-032).
  - *Acceptance:* a draft or unsent issue is never publicly reachable; the archive count matches the issues listed; prev/next omit missing neighbours at the ends.

## RAG / AI requirements (→ /techierag)

*None. TechieBlog has no AI, LLM, embedding or vector-search features, and does not reference
TechieRag. This section exists for schema completeness only.*

## Non-functional

<a id="d-req-nfr-001"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-001** — **PARTIAL (2026-08-07)** — Public pages load in under 2 s on broadband and the app serves ≥ 100 concurrent users (BRD-78).
  - *Acceptance:* both budgets below must hold on a **Release** build. They restate BRD-78's own two numbers verbatim — the "under 2 s" page load and the "≥ 100 concurrent users" ceiling — and add nothing the requirement did not already say. Machine-read by `verify-phase` §4c.
    - `perf-budget: p95 load <= 2000ms @ concurrency 1`
    - `perf-budget: p95 load <= 2000ms @ concurrency 100`
  - **Page load < 2 s — MET.** Real navigation timings against the running app, 12 page/viewport combinations: FCP **72–428 ms**, load event **53–353 ms**, TTFB 6–255 ms. Slowest single figure (resume @1280, FCP 428 ms) is ~5× inside budget.
  - **≥ 100 concurrent users — NOT MET.** Degrades at **concurrency 10** (p50 2 354 ms, only 38 % of responses under 2 s) and collapses at **100** (249/500 timeouts, p50 22.9 s). Throughput is flat at **~3.5 req/s** from concurrency 5 upward and the process stays wedged after the spike.
  - **Diagnosis (not fixed here — both files are outside Cluster J's ownership):**
    1. `source/TechieBlog/Program.cs` sets Serilog `MinimumLevel.Debug()` with `Override("Microsoft.AspNetCore.Components", Debug)` and no `IsDevelopment()` gate, emitting per-component render-tree logs to console **and** a rolling file — **≈61 KB of log per request** (58 MB over a 955-request run).
    2. **Only 41 of 265 public `DbAccess` methods are `async`**; synchronous Dapper calls block thread-pool threads, which matches the flat-throughput/no-recovery signature. Postgres itself stayed healthy (16 connections), so the bottleneck is in the app.
  - **Caveat stated plainly:** measured on a **Debug build in Development**. A Release re-run with logging at Information is required before the concurrency figure is final. No production-representative load-test harness exists yet.
  - **[§4c perf gate] 2026-08-10 — the Release re-run the caveat above asked for. The concurrency verdict is now final: it is NOT a logging artefact.** First measurement through the framework's own harness (`.tfcore/utils/tf-perf.sh`), **Release build, `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT=Production`**, Kestrel on `localhost:5099` inside WSL, PostgreSQL in the usual `WinPostgre` container; five public URLs (`/`, two `/post/{slug}`, `/category/programming`, `/newsletters`); warm-up discarded. **Budget 1 — `p95 load <= 2000ms @ concurrency 1`: `PERF-OK`.** p95 **1109ms** over 40 samples, 0 errors; slowest page `/category/programming` at 1184ms. Note this is ~45× the 22ms quiet-`curl` figure recorded on 2026-08-09 — that number was a single warm best-of-3 against one URL, this is a p95 across five URLs, so the two are not comparable and this one is the honest headline. Still comfortably inside budget. **Budget 2 — `p95 load <= 2000ms @ concurrency 100`: `PERF-FAIL` (load shed).** **321 of 500 requests timed out at 60 s (64%)**; the 179 that completed had p95 **55.9 s**. All failures were `TimeoutError`, no non-200s, and the app recovered to 230 ms immediately after the run — so it sheds load rather than wedging (an improvement on the 2026-08-07 "has to be restarted" behaviour). **What this settles:** the ≥100-concurrent ceiling survives a Release build with logging at Information, so REQ-NFR-013's 456× log reduction did not move it and the remaining cause is structural — the 222 synchronous Dapper call sites tracked under REQ-NFR-026 stages 3–4, plus the 18.9 DB round trips per `/` render tracked under REQ-NFR-018. Status and % left unchanged: this was a **§4c-only run**, not a verify pass (no acceptance tests, no §4a render sweep, no §4b visual gate), and the row's existing `PARTIAL` / 60% already records exactly this split. Raw JSON: `tests/.artifacts/perf/` (gitignored, regenerable).

<a id="d-req-nfr-002"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-002** — **OPEN** — Passwords are stored with an industry-standard salted hash (BRD-79). ⚠ SECURITY — replace `AppEncrypt.CreateHash` with PBKDF2/BCrypt/Argon2 and re-hash on next login.

<a id="d-req-nfr-003"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-003** — All database access uses parameterised queries (BRD-80).

<a id="d-req-nfr-004"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-004** — HTTPS redirection and HSTS are enforced outside Development (BRD-81).

<a id="d-req-nfr-005"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-005** — **OPEN** — Authentication endpoints are rate-limited (BRD-82). ⚠ SECURITY.

<a id="d-req-nfr-006"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-006** — **Implemented (2026-08-07)** — All user input is validated and encoded against XSS and injection, including rendered Markdown (BRD-83).
  - **Two live stored-XSS vulnerabilities were found and closed.**
    1. **Markdown body render path** (`MarkdownRenderer` → `MarkupString` on post view, newsletter view, post preview, admin preview, resume descriptions). Markdig performs no sanitisation; **20 of 27 probe vectors rendered as executable markup** before the fix. Now defended in three layers in `BlogEngine/Common/MarkdownRenderer.cs`: `.DisableHtml()`, a scheme allow-list on link/image/autolink destinations (entity-, case- and whitespace-obfuscation resistant), and attribute stripping for `on*`/`style`/URL-bearing attributes injected through Markdig's generic-attributes extension. **0 of 27 vectors survive**; safe Markdown is unaffected.
    2. **Search excerpt path** (`SearchResults.HighlightSearchTerms`) injected the raw post abstract into a `MarkupString` unencoded — an abstract containing `<img src=x onerror=…>` executed on the public `/search` page. Now every segment is HTML-encoded and only the method's own `<mark>` tags are live.
  - **Comment bodies were audited and found already safe** — `CommentThread.razor` renders the comment as a plain Razor expression (auto-encoded), not Markdown.
  - **Vectors proven neutralised end-to-end** (seeded in PostgreSQL, rendered in headless Chromium with a dialog listener — the captcha was never weakened to do this): `<script>`, `<img src=x onerror>`, `[link](javascript:)`, `![img](javascript:)`, `<iframe>`, `<svg onload>`, `<div onload/onmouseover>`, raw-HTML block, generic-attribute `{onclick=}` and `{style=}`, `&#106;avascript:`, `JaVaScRiPt:`, `vbscript:`, `data:text/html`, `data:image/svg+xml`, autolink `<javascript:…>`, `<form action=javascript:>`, `<meta http-equiv=refresh>`, `<style>`, `<object data=javascript:>`. Result: **0 dialogs, 0 script/iframe/svg nodes, 0 `on*` handlers, 0 script URLs** on both the post-body and comment-body paths; safe control content (bold, link, code, table) still renders.
  - Build 0 errors; unit suite **189/189**. Probe fixture removed from the database.

<a id="d-req-nfr-007"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-007** — **PARTIAL (2026-08-07)** — WCAG 2.1 AA conformance audited: heading hierarchy, 4.5:1 contrast, keyboard operability, visible focus, ARIA labelling, 44×44 px targets, 200 % zoom (BRD-84).
  - **First real audit.** axe-core (`wcag2a/2aa/21a/21aa`) over 6 routes × 2 viewports, a 35-stop keyboard traversal, and a computed OKLCH→sRGB contrast pass over all 3 site themes × light/dark. **Violation nodes: 64 → 2. 10 of 12 runs are completely clean.**
  - **Fixed in app code:**
    - **2.1.1 Keyboard — primary nav was entirely unreachable** (0/6 links); TrBlazeUI `NavigationMenu` emits `role="menuitem" tabindex="-1"` with no `menubar` parent. `Header.razor` rebuilt on `<nav><ul><li>`+`NavLink` → all 6 reachable, `aria-required-parent` (critical, 30 nodes) gone. Gap **TR-044**.
    - **2.4.7 Focus Visible** — 2 px `--ring` `:focus-visible` outline in `base.css`; visible focus **10/34 → 34/34** stops.
    - **4.1.2** — `aria-required-attr` (critical, 30 nodes) from `Rating`'s `aria-checked=""`; `StarRating.razor` now hides the widget and exposes `sr-only` text. Gap **TR-045**.
    - **2.4.2 Page Titled** — only 3 of 11 public routes had a `<PageTitle>`; 8 added.
    - **1.4.1 Use of Colour** — two in-prose home links now underlined, not colour-only.
    - **1.4.3 / 1.4.11 Contrast** — 9 computed failures fixed **in `theme.css` tokens**, not in components: `--alert-warning` (2.87–3.16:1, all 3 light themes), `--alert-success` (4.18/4.45:1, 2 themes), `--muted-foreground` on `--muted` (4.34:1), and `--input` — the visible boundary of every form control — at **1.26–1.73:1 in all 6 theme×mode combinations**. All pass now.
  - **Deliberately NOT changed:** `--border` at 1.26–1.33:1. It styles decorative dividers and card edges, which are not "visual information required to identify UI components" under 1.4.11; raising it would restyle every surface. `--input` (the control boundary that *is* in scope) was raised instead. Recorded as a judgement call, not an oversight.
  - **Still failing — this is why the status is PARTIAL, not Implemented:**
    1. **1.1.1 Non-text Content — the captcha has no non-visual alternative.** Since it moved to anonymous vector paths there is no audio, logic or text challenge, and it gates the comment form, the rating step and the newsletter subscribe card, so anonymous engagement is impossible for blind and low-vision users. Owning feature **REQ-UI-056**. Needs a dedicated REQ for an accessible alternative challenge — implementing one is beyond an audit's scope.
    2. **2.1.1 + 4.1.2 — `Rating` stars (TR-031, LIBRARY-CAUSED).** Re-confirmed by measurement: **0 of 5 stars focusable**, **4 duplicate `<linearGradient id>`** in one control. Mitigated in app code with a keyboard-reachable `<fieldset>` radio group (`PostRatingPanel.razor` + `.tb-keyboard-fallback`), but **1 residual `aria-hidden-focus` (serious) node** remains on the post page because the library's own `tabindex="0"` radiogroup sits inside the `aria-hidden` wrapper. Not fixable from application code.
    3. **Target size** — icon buttons 40×40, nav/footer links ~20 px tall, short of the 44×44 the acceptance names. That figure is WCAG 2.5.5 **AAA** and inline text links are exempt from both 2.5.5 and 2.5.8, so this is a miss against the stated wording rather than a 2.1 AA failure.
  - **Not yet covered at all: 200 % zoom / reflow (1.4.10) and a real screen-reader pass.** Neither was exercised; do not treat them as passed.

Separation re-verified clean; the architectural contract surface (`IGenericRepository`, `IAuthService`, `AllRepoInterfaces.cs`) is now fully documented. Gap measured: **~744 model/service members still undocumented** (BlogModels 262, BlogEngine 482) — needs a dedicated documentation pass.

<a id="d-req-fn-053"></a>
- **REQ-FN-053** — **OPEN** — Manage Profile must not destroy data it never loaded.
  `SelectBlogUserById` projects every `BlogUser` column, so a read-modify-write round trip through
  `/admin/profile` preserves `Username`, `IsSiteOwner`, `Title`, `Tagline`, `InstagramUrl`,
  `PhoneNumber`, `Location`, `CVFilePath` and `ResumeEnabled`.
  - *Acceptance:* opening `/admin/profile` as the site owner and saving **without editing anything**
    leaves every column byte-identical (proved against the database, not the form); the resume inputs
    render stored values rather than empty boxes; `/` and `/resume` continue to serve the owner's live
    data; and a counterfactual against the old 17-column projection fails the same test.
